# Canonical Program and formal engine cut

Status: Wave B is reopened for the binding Program/Link composition cut. The
fixture-wide structural denominator remains evidence, but Wave-C/D production
work is paused until Program and Link satisfy the small-owner, single-authority,
factorized component contract below.

## Current authorized wave

Canonical Program, Target, Link, direct AST-to-Program lowering, and artifact
sealing remain the sole structural authority. The earlier seven Wave-B owner
rows and fixture-wide Program-to-Link denominator remain valid semantic
evidence, but they did not validate composition. The 2026-08-06 audit found
that Program and especially Link violate the plan's one-authority and
factorization laws. Target remains unchanged. Program/Link correction is now
the exclusive active wave; no further Rule, solver, body-compiler, fixture, or
diagnostic numerator advances until its exit gates pass.

The fixed-candidate read selector has been flash-replaced by the one staged
exact-read path. Completed predecessor observations select only owner-issued
exact Refs; Product preserves their Guard-row correlation; an epoch publishes
only the accepted sparse reverse relation and atomically removes stale routes.
Selected pairs never become Program, Link, Rule-instance, Point, Group,
manifest, or persistent identity. The dynamic Program/Link relation remains
the sole activation mechanism. A Rule may now bind one atomic
`RouteWrite` to its preceding staged selection: every selected owner-issued Ref
is paired with its route-local value, equal targets reduce only through the
Factor's admitted Join, unselected coordinates survive only through declared
Carry, and evidence authenticates the exact derivation ticket, row, target, and
route ordinal. The accepted laws cover owner fencing, row provenance, multiple
semantic tags on one physical unit, multi-stage selection, cancellation,
route replacement, demanded/full equivalence, recursive route changes and
Carry closure through Mu/WTO, canonical schema replay without active routes,
and additive cold `C + R` storage. Sealing declares the target Factor's exact-
unit universe once in `O(R)`; hot exact routing performs no target-universe scan
or allocation and there is no candidate-by-root or `C x R` surface.

The atomic Heap allocation cut is accepted. One explicit zero ingress feeds
the source-form-disjoint empty and closed construction Rules; the obsolete
per-root/per-field Rules are absent. Closed construction builds a complete
source-ordered object for every finite Value product world, uses Link's narrow
direct-read proof to preserve `{[x] = x}` diagonals, retains independent
coordinates otherwise, maps nil to raw absence, preserves typed key/value
containment, and applies `Create` from the same predecessor for recurrence.
Complete worlds merge through a balanced exact join forest with no cap or
budget. Full source reconstruction is cold; hot transfer uses exact owner
fences. Every Product disposition is replayed by evidence. Bounded allocation
and whole-Heap suites plus independent zero-residue review pass. Static
production assembly proves ingress, empty-table/closure, and closed Rule
admission. The canonical direct-field body now executes its dynamic local read
through the production analysis entrypoint. Broader multi-Guard body coverage remains an
explicit compiler gate: testlaw may not manufacture a Value producer or
alternate topology to make it green.

The accepted Heap raw-access leaf remains the sole typed Heap raw-access
boundary, including the Target `BootEntry` mutability law. It is one of the
fifteen Rules in the sole production analysis composition. The
leaf consumes the existing Link index candidate and typed Value/Call/Heap
observations directly; it may not revive a field-Fact plane, string key,
selector registry, or engine-owned Lua judgment.

`compiler/`, the root VM/runtime, and V2 are read-only. This wave does not
integrate Program with the existing compiler, bytecode generator, VM, runtime,
or V2. Program is designed as their future semantic input, but that integration
is a separately authorized wave. The production analysis may grow only by adding exact arms
to its total body compiler and Rules to its one sealed composition; no partial
production evaluator, compatibility field, or alternate solve path is
authorized.

No Program-to-CFG, Program-to-WIR, Program-to-old-equation, manifest
compatibility, or dual-entry adapter is permitted. The obsolete live analyzer
entrypoints must not regain semantic authority or select between old and new
representations.

Current executable truth supersedes the historical Wave-D census retained
later in this document: the root `analysis` package owns one production
Composition with five Factors and fifteen Rules. It classifies every mounted
Body through one closed sum and executes one receipt-owned solver. The
admitted arms are exact direct-field read, exact global-operation call/effect,
and direct literal return; an unknown body rejects the entire Link. Engine
imports no domain, Link, Target, or Lua package, and no domain owns another
Composition or Solver. “Oracle” names only the validation/test suite that
consumes this public production analysis path; it owns no production package,
composition, semantic key, body compiler, or result type.

`__legacy/` remains reference-only semantic archaeology. It has no live import,
test, gate, adapter, or fallback authority. “Remove legacy” means removing the
obsolete mechanisms and parallel authorities from the current live analyzer;
it does not mean deleting the reference tree.

The Future consumer contract states obligations only and does not authorize
compiler or runtime work. This section controls when later wording conflicts
with it.

## Goal

Build one formal, symbolic, modular abstract interpreter whose core has no Lua
domain knowledge and whose work is proportional to semantic demand.

The completed pipeline is:

```text
source
  → parse
  → bind
  → Program
      └→ Rules → Solver → State → Queries/certificates

future consumer, not authorized now:
Program + certificates → backend representation
```

The analyzer design reduces to five ownership laws:

1. Program/Link supplies every exact structural operand once.
2. A domain owns its lattice and Rules; every Rule soundly simulates one
   concrete operation.
3. Solver performs only generic dependency scheduling and verifies a
   post-fixpoint; each domain proves its own convergence without budgets.
4. Typed boundary Rules preserve packs, aliases, freshness, outcomes, and
   current provider/resumer state through ordinary relational composition.
5. Solver-private completed Factor roots are the solved authority; State is an
   immutable snapshot of registered Query projections and provenance. Neither
   State nor Query can invent semantics.

The concrete relational algebra later in this document is the proof meaning of
these laws. It is not a universal code carrier, new engine API, or request to
multiply every domain together.

## Frozen target profile

The analyzer semantic denominator is the parser-produced Wippy Lua profile:
every parser-reachable source form under the frozen Target rows, including the
parser's typed syntax and sealed Wippy provider/actor extensions. The dialect's
historical Lua-version label is not semantic authority: it retains Lua-5.1-style
lexical globals, admits specifically enumerated Lua 5.3 operators and operation
laws, and carries the explicit Wippy restrictions and extensions below.
Coverage means every valid structural
permutation in that closed profile has Program/Target/Link rows and a concrete
semantic law. It does not mean accepting arbitrary manually assembled AST states
or silently unioning every historical registry declaration, nor does a broad
"Lua 5.3" label admit a feature whose Target row is absent.

Source parsing is the analyzer's language-construction route. The exported AST
is a parser/lowering representation, not a second semantic language.
Parser-unreachable fields or nodes are outside the denominator unless they are
made parser-reachable with a defined Program law. This wave does not change
compiler entrypoints or production bytecode behavior.

Target owns the frozen dialect and provider laws: operation and lifecycle
relations, initial-environment rows, policy constants, and provider ABI laws.
Link owns project-specific binding: resolved imports/exports, host/provider
instances, module-cache instances and aliases, and the exact inter-project
correspondences that apply those Target laws. Link may select or reference a
Target law but never alter it. `Target/Link` is shorthand for the typed
composition of those two existing authorities, never a third schema, row owner,
or fallback authority; every concrete row names Target or Link as its owner.

Lexical globals are one deliberate Wippy divergence from stock Lua 5.3. An
unresolved source identifier binds the one Program `CellGlobal` for that name,
and reads or writes that Cell through the ordinary initial-global/Heap law.
Program seal derives one sealed structural
`GlobalBinding(CellGlobal(name), GlobalEnvRoot, canonical String atom(name))`
row for every such Cell under the frozen Target initial-global law. The Cell is the Program handle for exactly that raw
slot of the one `GlobalEnvRoot`; it is not independent mutable storage.  A
read or write of the Cell and an ordinary `_G[name]` read or write therefore
use the same Heap access relation, raw presence, payload, metatable route, and
write invalidation.  A dynamic `_G[k]` access uses that same relation: on a
branch where `k` equals a bound canonical String atom it reads/writes the
matching `CellGlobal`, while an imprecise key retains every matching global
slot plus the ordinary non-global-key alternatives.  Conversely a Cell write
invalidates the matching exact and dynamic-root lens observations.  This is
one Target/Program binding relation over existing Cells, roots, atoms, and
Heap lenses, not a global-value Factor, environment indirection, or alias
registry.
`_ENV` is an ordinary identifier under this profile: a local, parameter, or
captured `_ENV` has exactly its ordinary lexical Cell meaning and does not
redirect resolution of another unresolved identifier through a Table lens.
`_G` is the initial global Table root and its self-alias, not an implicit
lexical environment upvalue. Thus a source such as `local _ENV = {x = 1}; x`
uses the local `_ENV` only when it is named explicitly and reads the global
`x` Cell for its final expression. The Lua 5.3 laws claimed by this profile
apply to its admitted operations, not to Lua 5.3's `_ENV` compilation model.
The `GlobalEnvRoot` in every `GlobalBinding` is fixed by Target/Link actor boot,
not by the current Value stored in the `_G` slot.  Initially that slot
self-aliases the root.  Rebinding `_G` is an ordinary write to its own bound
slot: later `_G[k]` follows the newly flowed receiver, but bare global names
continue to use their fixed `GlobalEnvRoot` bindings. It neither retargets
another `CellGlobal` nor installs a new environment. Link's actor-boot row
chooses one exact actor-local instance of this mutable root for each actor and
seeds that actor's `GlobalBinding` rows from the same initial-root-ledger
Value, so no Cell/lens pair may begin with divergent contents. Link rejects an
alias of this mutable root between actors; roots within one actor retain their
ordinary exact aliasing.
No binder, lowerer, Rule, Link step, or future consumer may infer a second
environment indirection from the spelling `_ENV`; changing this rule is a
future Target/Program decision.

Program/Target represents the metamethod alternatives for every parsed
operator, including `__idiv`, `__band`, `__bor`, `__bxor`, `__shl`, `__shr`,
and `__bnot`. Primitive, raw, and metamethod behavior remain distinct typed
relations. Future executable consumers must preserve those relations; this
wave neither changes nor validates their implementation.

The analyzed library surface is the intersection of the frozen Target profile
and sealed host configuration. A declaration in an old analysis signature
registry does not make an operation available. Target-excluded `load`,
`loadfile`, `dofile`, `collectgarbage`, `string.dump`, `bit32`,
`io`, `os`, `package`,
`debug.getinfo`, `debug.getlocal`, `debug.traceback`,
`errors.call_stack`, `Error:stack()`,
`coroutine.close`, and `coroutine.isyieldable` declarations are outside the
analyzed Target. Adding one requires a later explicit Target-contract
decision. This profile does not add Lua 5.4 facilities merely because
historical signatures named them.

Weak tables and user-visible GC finalizers are deliberately outside the
analyzed dialect even though Lua 5.1 supports them. The Target has no relation
for a metatable `__mode` requesting weak keys/values or for a `__gc` hook;
there is no weak-key, weak-value, ephemeron, GC-driven entry-disappearance, or
finalizer branch in the analyzer. An unattached Table may hold those ordinary
string keys as inert data, but attaching a metatable whose *raw* `__mode`
String requests `k` or `v`, or whose raw `__gc` is non-nil, takes the typed
unsupported-target Throw without changing the target's metatable. The same
Throw precedes a raw or ordinary write that would create either active field on
any Table presently attached as one or more metatables; aliases observe the
same active-metatable relation. Nil/removal and a non-weak `__mode` String stay
ordinary data writes. Ordinary metatables remain.

Dynamic code and raw executable injection are excluded from the analyzed
Target. Lua `load`/`loadfile`/`dofile`, package loaders, raw `FunctionProto`
construction/injection, unchecked bytecode decode, and equivalent
provider/host aliases have no executable Target relation. Only the uniform
denied-operation Throw row below may represent their source binding in a
verified artifact/link. Removing or restricting any corresponding runtime API
is future integration work, not authorized here.

Static containment is one sealed, transitive derived classification over the
ordinary canonical Program graph, not a second expression, control, or static
IR. Its roots are every operand of authored static syntax, including `typeof`
and annotation arguments; its closure follows the existing ordinary
expression/Values/function/body topology needed to determine that operand.
The classification preserves each contained term's source occurrence, lexical
scope, span, static declaration/reference, and exact structural operand
identity. It does not copy Values, Cells, Functions, or type trees. Every
contained operation has exactly one static disposition: an admitted
static-only Target interpretation plus any required Link resolution, or one
exact static-operand diagnostic.
It never falls through to executable behavior. In particular, no
static-contained term may contribute a runtime control or outcome successor,
executable Call candidate, runtime import candidate, Module-cache transition,
runtime Heap/Cell mutation, runtime allocation/freshness result, Effect,
escape, callback, suspension, or provider transition. Static-domain Rules may
read only the declared static operand/source coordinate at the static frontier;
they do not turn containment into a reachable runtime path. Static type owns
the non-public, derived `ContainedOperand` judgment over that exact existing
Program closure and Link namespace:

```text
ContainedOperand(operand/source identity, N, rho, S) =
    RuntimeSubject(exact existing Value/type coordinate)
  | Known(static value/type authority)
  | Unknown(exact static reason)
  | Invalid(exact static diagnostic)
```

This is a Static-type subjudgment, not a Program row, Value identity, Factor,
second IR, or public engine vocabulary. `RuntimeSubject` names an already
existing coordinate and does not evaluate it anew. A literal, reference, or
Target-admitted static-only operation derives `Known` only from its declared
static Target law and existing Program/Link operands; in particular a resolved
contained literal `require` derives the exact type of its Link-supplied static
module namespace. A sealed opaque-but-admitted static law derives
`Unknown(reason)` with its exact Target/Link source dependency; it is neither
runtime Value Top nor `any`/`never`, and has no runtime fallback. A rejected
contained operation derives only `Invalid(diagnostic)`. `typeof` consumes this
judgment: `RuntimeSubject` installs the ordinary state-dependent operand read,
`Known` supplies its exact type result without any Value/Call/Module/Effect
edge, `Unknown` supplies the corresponding symbolic unknown result without a
Factor read, and `Invalid` supplies that exact static diagnostic. Seal derives
and validates this closure and judgment from the same Program relations, and
artifact decode rederives them rather than serializing a parallel mark graph.

Module resolution is closed-world: canonical Program Import observations are structural; only executable occurrences may supply runtime import candidates, while static-contained and source-dead occurrences have no runtime import authority. Exactly the
binder-proved calls through the unshadowed global `require` Cell whose source
form has one nonempty literal String argument receive a sealed Program import
observation beside the ordinary Call. The observation carries the evaluated
callee Value, request Values, and that exact literal module name; there is no
zero-name, nonliteral, or dynamically computed Import identity. An observation
in executable syntax has the runtime import candidate described here; one in
static containment has only the static-only disposition below. Project Link
resolves the literal name to one sealed shard or host module identity. The
executable runtime import branch is feasible only when the evaluated callee is
the exact initial `require` Value; all other flowed callees use the ordinary
Call branch. A lexically shadowed or aliased spelling has no runtime import
candidate. Assigning the global Cell is an ordinary Heap mutation, so a later
direct spelling is still structurally visible but gains no import behavior
unless the initial Value is proved to flow there. The initial `require` Value
has no generic external-call row: reaching it through a nonliteral or malformed
call with that Value produces the canonical unsupported-target diagnostic,
while an overwritten or shadowing user Value remains an ordinary call. No Rule
recovers a module name from runtime strings and no downstream layer may mint a
missing Import observation.

For each direct binder-unshadowed syntactic call whose sole source argument is
an exact nonempty literal String and which Link resolves, Link owns one literal-resolution row
`R = (call occurrence, literal module name, resolved shard-or-host identity,
static-export namespace content, disposition)`. `R` is one structural
resolution authority, not a Value or a second resolver. Its disposition is
exclusive. In executable syntax, its runtime projection supplies the existing
runtime import candidate, and its lexical projection exists only for that
call's declared alias and supplies that alias's static-import candidate. In
static containment, `R` has only a static-operand projection, keyed by the
contained call occurrence and its exact static scope; it needs no declared
alias and contributes the resolved module's immutable namespace to that
operand's `N`. Thus `typeof(require("api"))` has a static-only literal
resolution but no runtime import, Call, Module, Value, Heap, effect, outcome,
or result transition. The projections are different Program/Link semantic
sorts: an executable runtime projection has the evaluated callee Value and
Guard, while either static projection has neither and cannot create a runtime
transition. A contained direct `require` without an exact literal or Link
resolution produces its exact static-operand diagnostic and never takes a
runtime fallback.

The existing Program `TypePublication(assign, authored-pair, TypeRef)` relation
is the sole source-language producer of assignment-based static namespace
content. It retains the binder-proved dotted root/path, exact declaration
target, lexical visibility, source frontier, and original authored pair; it is
additive to the ordinary runtime assignment and is never inferred from Heap
contents or a later mutation. For every source module, Link derives its
static-export surface from the module's exact Program export/return-root
relation plus those eligible TypePublication rows, then uses that canonical
surface (and sealed host exports where applicable) as `R`'s
static-export namespace content. An ineligible root, path, pair, or target is
a Program/Link seal error, not a dynamic lookup. The surface is sorted and
content-addressed by module identity, publication/source target identities,
and exact root/path selections; it is part of `N.contentID` and `R` identity.
Runtime Module-cache/Heap results cannot add, remove, or alter it.

An exact-literal runtime candidate with no Link resolution row is not a
project-level Link-seal rejection. Link retains one typed unresolved-candidate
disposition containing the semantic call-occurrence identity, literal-String
identity, and attempted resolution identity, but creates no `R`, static
namespace, Module state, or imported runtime Value. At the candidate's
existing evaluated-callee Guard, only the branch where that callee is the
exact initial `require` Value takes the canonical
`LinkUnresolvedModule` unsupported-target Throw. Every other branch retains
the ordinary Call result and outcomes, including a branch reached after the
global `require` Cell has been overwritten. The oracle records that diagnostic
only for the feasible initial-`require` branch; its syntactic presence cannot
drop the root or suppress an ordinary-call branch. This is distinct from a
nonliteral call reaching the initial `require` Value: that call has no Import
identity and takes the ordinary unsupported-target diagnostic below.

The admitted `require` operation has exactly one source argument, and that
argument is the exact nonempty literal String carried by the import candidate.
Missing, extra, empty, nonliteral, or dynamically computed requests create no
Import identity; if their evaluated callee is the initial `require` Value they
take the one unsupported-target diagnostic. They never perform loader search
or string recovery.

The Wippy module contract deliberately replaces `package.loaded` and loader
search. Link rejects every source-module import cycle, so initialization is a
finite DAG rather than an implicit recursive module semantics. The rejection
is one canonical `LinkImportCycle` diagnostic containing the sorted semantic
module and import-edge identities of each cyclic SCC. It is a Link seal result,
not a Rule or Factor conclusion; the oracle records such a project as
`ExpectedUnsupported(LinkImportCycle)` rather than dropping its roots.

Link seals one complete module-cache ingress/transport authority: the exact
finite `ModuleCacheInstance` identity universe and actor-local alias-equivalence
relation for the analyzed deployment under the frozen Target module law; a
total exact analysis-root-to-actor-local-instance mapping; and one exact
`rep(instance)` correspondence on every existing call, spawn, callback/re-entry,
resume, and module-entry boundary. Thus an instance is never selected by a
Solver root, a domain, or runtime context. For every sealed alias class `C`, Link derives `rep(i) =
min(C)` under the canonical encoded `ModuleCacheInstance` identity byte order.
It emits exactly that one member for the class and rejects a non-equivalence
relation or any supplied representative row that is missing, out of class,
duplicated, or not that minimum. Each boundary correspondence canonicalizes
the inherited caller or retained-continuation instance, or an explicitly
selected Link alias, before transport. A module continuation retains only that
canonical representative. Thus every resolved-module access transported across
one of those boundaries denotes the coordinate `(actor, resolved module,
rep(instance))`. An alias class contains instances from one actor boot only:
distinct actors never share a mutable Module cache, even when their sealed
immutable Target or `BootShape` inputs agree. Intra-actor sharing or isolation
is represented only by that sealed equivalence and representative
correspondence, never inferred from execution. A dynamic context, current
provider/resumer state, or cache-state revision can neither select/change a
representative nor enter a Module Factor key. The Module Factor's stable key is
therefore `(actor, resolved module, rep(instance))`; it is not a Program/Heap
Cell. In each concrete configuration that key has exactly one state: `Cold`,
`Pending(Suspension generation)`, or `Ready(value)`.

At one shared-Guard terminal, the Module Factor element is a finite normalized
may-relation over those concrete alternatives:

```text
Cold | Pending(Suspension generation subject) | Ready(Value subject)
```

`Suspension generation subject` is a typed reference to the existing finite
structural continuation subject together with the exact live generation
alternative (`Recent` or `Summary` where recurrence requires it) selected from
the Suspension Factor at that terminal. The Module Factor owns only the
cache-state correlation to that reference; it does not own, duplicate, or
summarize generation liveness/consumption.
`Value subject` is the existing Value-domain subject for the one published
scalar. The element's bottom is the empty relation, its default is `{Cold}`,
its join is normalized alternative union, and its concretization is the union
of concrete one-state cache cells denoted by those alternatives under the
terminal. The sealed module keys, state tags, generation subjects, and Value
subjects are finite, so `Widen = Join` is a finite-height Module-domain law.
The shared Guard carrier, rather than a second Module Guard, keeps alternatives
correlated with their callers and outcomes. Unrelated provider/resumer
conclusions remain in their own Factors. After initiation, Module reads only
the matching already committed Suspension generation required by its Pending
transition; initiation instead uses the common structural inputs below.

Module cold start has one exact structural `ModuleInit` correspondence per
source-shard require candidate and canonical cache key. It names the common
predecessor Module Cold input, require demand, module-entry boundary, empty
entry Pack, presealed fresh Suspension generation subject, predecessor
materialization inputs, and all Normal/Return/Throw/Yield/Cancel/resume
outcomes. It is one successor transaction, not an intermediate cache state or
another Factor. Both single-output owner Rules read only that same predecessor
carrier and those structural inputs: the Suspension-owned initiation Rule
writes the fresh generation's liveness and private/shared classification, and
the Module-owned cache Rule writes only `Pending(g)`. The Module Rule never
reads the sibling Suspension patch or treats its uncommitted liveness as an
input. The transaction validates both independently produced distinct-Factor
patches against the same predecessor and commits them atomically as the one
declared `ModuleInit` successor; neither intermediate root is observable.
`Module -> Suspension` incidence comes from the initiation Rule's Cold read.
`Suspension -> Module` incidence comes from the later Pending completion,
Yield, Cancel, and resume Rules that read an already committed live generation,
not from a read-after-uncommitted-write during initiation. Neither owner writes
the other Factor. A host-module Cold demand uses its separately declared direct
`Ready(exposure)` transition and has no Suspension generation.

Module Rules apply the following transitions pointwise to matching concrete
alternatives and preserve every unrelated may alternative. A source-shard Cold
demand takes that `ModuleInit` transaction, becomes `Pending` with its exact
generation subject, and invokes Entry with the empty Pack. Each Pending
completion, Yield, Cancel, and resume Rule
reads the matching Suspension generation at the same terminal: it may act only
while that generation is live and its declared resume/cancel support survives.
Normal fallthrough, an empty Return Pack, or a first returned `nil` changes
that matching live Pending alternative to `Ready(true)`; otherwise the
scalar-adjusted first returned Value becomes its Ready subject. The Value
owner's require-result Rule then reads that exact Ready alternative and writes
the calling result as the same subject. A Cold demand of a host module
atomically becomes `Ready(host-module-exposure Value)`, and that same Value
rule returns the one sealed exposure Value: every host-module identity has
exactly one such declared exposure Value and no Entry, initializer callback,
or Pending state. A Ready hit reads its same subject through that Value rule
without re-entry. Throw from the matching live Pending generation restores Cold
and propagates. Yield retains the matching Pending generation and publishes
nothing; another demand of that Pending alternative takes the typed
initialization-in-progress Throw and leaves it unchanged. Cancel acts only on
the matching live generation, lets Suspension exclusively consume it, and then
restores Cold. A resume whose generation is consumed, superseded, or does not
match the Pending alternative takes the ordinary invalid-resume Throw, leaves
the current cache alternative unchanged, and cannot publish into it. A retry
from Cold takes a new `ModuleInit`: from the common predecessor and structural
materialization inputs, the Suspension patch materializes the prior generation
before creating the fresh one, while the independent Module patch writes only
`Pending(fresh)` from that same presealed fresh-generation operand.
Completion, Yield, Cancel, or resume from the old Recent/Summary generation
cannot change or publish into that new cache state. These transitions are total
and atomic per concrete cache cell;
their finite may relation is the Module Factor's ordinary sound abstraction.
Every Module Factor key construction, read, and write first applies the
Link-sealed `rep` relation and therefore uses exactly `(resolved module,
rep(instance))`; two aliases cannot address distinct cells. Mutable
provider/resumer state other than the matching Suspension generation is read
only by the Rules of its provider/Target-declared owning Factor and never
becomes cache identity.
Program/Link owns only their exact call/outcome/resume correspondence. `R`'s
executable lexical projection gives its alias one immutable Link
static-namespace relation; a static-only literal-resolution projection gives
the contained operand that same immutable namespace at its exact static scope.
These relations are guard-free and have no Module-cache, Heap, Value,
capability, outcome, or result authority. They are compile-time name authority
only: they cannot activate a runtime import, create a runtime Value or provider
capability, or turn an ordinary Call into an import. Conversely, `R`'s
executable runtime projection alone uses the evaluated-callee Guard and may
take the ordinary Call branch after mutation or flow. A dynamic, shadowed, or
unresolved syntactic candidate creates no static namespace unless it has its
own admitted static-only law; otherwise it closes with its exact
static-operand diagnostic.

The sealed Target contract is the language authority; root VM/library code is
a later conformance subject, not a hidden source of analyzer semantics. An
admitted standard operation follows its Lua 5.3 law unless an explicit Wippy
Target row states a deliberate deviation. A current implementation panic,
defensive recursion limit, missing return, accidental extra return, or other
divergence is recorded for future conformance and is never copied into a
Factor as language law or convergence machinery. This wave does not repair or
execute the VM.

Each admitted operation is closed by two kinds of typed authority:

1. its Target row owns frozen operation/provider law visible outside one
   transfer; any required Link row owns the project binding, instance, or
   correspondence that applies that law. Together they supply accepted Pack
   shapes, possible outcomes, aliases/fresh results, callback and produced
   operations, causal callback chaining, suspension/resumption, transfer,
   protocol, policy constants, and dependency identity;
2. each affected Factor has one domain-owned Rule consumer for the conclusions
   that Factor owns: coercion, defaults, value-dependent result selection,
   numeric or string transformation, heap mutation, effects, escape,
   ownership, and abstract loss may therefore be separate Rules over the same
   structural row. Placement has no Rule conclusion owner: a placement-facing
   Rule writes only its named Heap, Escape, Ownership, Transfer, Suspension,
   Residence, Footprint, or Typestate conclusion, and placement later projects
   those facts. No operation-wide mega Rule owns their union.
   Every consumer's semantic version is part of Solver/equation/cache/
   certificate identity.

Every typed callback, meta-application, or ordinary-access sub-edge already
owned by a Target row also seals its exact yield disposition. `PropagateYield`
names that edge's existing suspension/resume correspondence and preserves the
Yield outcome. `RejectYield` maps a callee Yield to the canonical Target Throw
whose payload is the exact Lua 5.3 `"attempt to yield across a C-call boundary"`
error, with no continuation created for that edge. The row also states direct
Function versus ordinary callable admission and its scalar/Pack result
selection. This is per existing Target causal edge and its outcome map, not a
global yieldability state, Factor, Guard atom, or engine service. A library
operation whose implementation enters a non-yieldable Lua C-call boundary uses
`RejectYield`; an operation such as `pcall` whose specified boundary is
resumable uses `PropagateYield`. The disposition table validates one such
choice and complete outcome handling for every internal edge.

A generated disposition table proves that every admitted Target operation has
one structural row, the complete declared set of affected Factor consumers,
exactly one writing owner for each conclusion, and a consumer for every
outcome/causal edge. It is a static coverage proof, not a runtime registry or
operation DSL. A structural choice cannot hide in Rule code; a transfer
formula cannot be duplicated in Target metadata. Changing either invalidates
the smallest identity that interprets it.

Initial environment construction is also explicit Target data. It creates the
ordinary global and library-table roots once, including `_G`'s self-alias,
`_VERSION = "Lua 5.3 - Wippy Modification"`, the build-sealed
`_GOPHER_LUA_VERSION`, exact builtin function identities, Wippy module/global
bindings, binary64 `math.pi = 0x1.921fb54442d18p+1`, binary64 positive-infinity
`math.huge`, signed-64-bit `math.maxinteger = 9223372036854775807`, and
`math.mininteger = -9223372036854775808`, the exact bytes of
`utf8.charpattern = "[\x00-\x7F\xC2-\xF4][\x80-\xBF]*"`, and these exact
`errors` constants:

```text
NOT_FOUND="NotFound"              ALREADY_EXISTS="AlreadyExists"
INVALID="Invalid"                 PERMISSION_DENIED="PermissionDenied"
UNAVAILABLE="Unavailable"         INTERNAL="Internal"
CANCELED="Canceled"               CONFLICT="Conflict"
TIMEOUT="Timeout"                 RATE_LIMITED="RateLimited"
UNKNOWN=""
```

The Wippy runtime's current `utf8.charpattern` upper byte `FD` is a conformance
defect; the frozen Target uses Lua 5.3's valid UTF-8 upper byte `F4`. Initially
`string.gfind` and `string.gmatch` contain the same callable identity, as do
global `unpack` and `table.unpack`; later writes mutate only the selected table
slot. The primitive String
metatable is a distinct initial root whose `__index` aliases the same `string`
library root. The canonical initial-root ledger is an ordered, content-addressed
finite set of `(root identity, key, initial Value, mutability)` rows. It names
every `_G` base binding and every `table`, `string`, `math`, `coroutine`,
`utf8`, `debug`, and `errors` field; no implicit registry or wildcard supplies
a member. The `_G` root is the `GlobalEnvRoot` named by the sealed
`GlobalBinding` rows: for every bound global its exact canonical String slot
and the Cell's initial value are this same ledger row, including `_G`'s initial
self-alias. The ledger contains no duplicate Cell store or implicit global
environment. `_G`, the six ordinary library roots, and the primitive String
metatable are mutable. The `errors` table, Error metatable, and Error method
table are frozen. The ledger also names every initial `io`, `os`, `package`,
and `bit32` root and every one of their denied paths, plus each denied base,
string, coroutine, debug, and Error path; an excluded callable occupies its
ordinary denied-operation Value, while any excluded noncall field has its
explicit ordinary initial Value or is explicitly absent. Thus a field lookup,
overwrite, deletion, or alias is never inferred from “all members” prose.
After initialization ordinary Heap writes—not a signature registry—govern
every mutable row. Reading a library constant is therefore a normal
root/key/value relation, not an untracked type shortcut. A runtime that
exposes a different literal, field, mutability, or root alias is a conformance
failure, not another Target profile.

The binding-level denominator is finite and exact. Wave B materializes this
set as canonical Target data and the generated disposition gate compares
against it directly:

```text
base operations = {
  assert,error,getmetatable,ipairs,next,pairs,pcall,print,rawequal,rawget,
  rawlen,rawset,select,setmetatable,tonumber,tostring,type,xpcall,
  require,unpack
}
table = {concat,insert,move,pack,remove,sort,unpack,
         getn,maxn,create,freeze,isfrozen}
string = {byte,char,find,format,gmatch,gfind,gsub,len,lower,match,pack,
          packsize,rep,reverse,sub,unpack,upper}
math = {abs,acos,asin,atan,ceil,cos,deg,exp,floor,fmod,log,max,min,modf,
        rad,random,randomseed,sin,sqrt,tan,tointeger,type,ult,
        atan2,cosh,sinh,tanh,pow,frexp,ldexp,log10,mod}
coroutine = {create,resume,running,status,wrap,yield,spawn}
utf8 = {char,codes,codepoint,len,offset}
debug = {getupvalue}
errors = {new,wrap,is, Error.__tostring,Error.__concat,
          Error.kind,Error.retryable,Error.details,Error.message}
```

`require` is the closed module operation below; global `unpack`, `gfind`,
`table.getn`/`maxn`/`create`/`freeze`/`isfrozen`, `coroutine.spawn`, and the
trailing compatibility math names are explicit Wippy additions rather than
mislabelled Lua 5.3 defaults. The compatibility identities and ABIs are
equally explicit: `math.atan2` is a distinct `(Number, Number) -> Number`
Wippy callable, not an alias of `math.atan`; `cosh`, `sinh`, `tanh`, and
`log10` are distinct `Number -> Number` callables; `pow` is distinct
`(Number, Number) -> Number`; `frexp` is distinct `Number -> (Number, Integer)`;
`ldexp` is distinct `(Number, Integer) -> Number`; and `mod` is a distinct
`(Number, Number) -> Number` Wippy modulo callable, not an alias of `fmod`.
Static values are `_G`, `_VERSION`, the Wippy version string, the four declared
math constants, `utf8.charpattern`, and the sealed errors constants. All `io`,
`os`, `package`, and `bit32` members; every
`debug` member outside the singleton admitted set; the named dynamic-code,
stack-observation, Error-stack, GC, and Lua-5.4 coroutine bindings; and
`string.dump` are denied. Adding, removing, or aliasing a binding changes the
Target content identity and its counted disposition row.

Every initial unshadowed binding has one content-addressed classification:
`Admitted(operation)`, `Denied(diagnostic)`, or `Ordinary(value)`. The first
two materialize ordinary callable Values in the initial Heap; the denied Value
has exactly one unsupported-target Throw outcome. The classes are disjoint and
total over the frozen binding ledger. Shadowing or later mutation is ordinary
Heap behavior and does not inherit the original class. Link rejects a host
contract that re-exports a denied Value under another path. Thus missing
members never stand in for exclusion and a generic dynamic call cannot erase
the required diagnostic.

The admitted-operation ledger has these mandatory closures:

- Core and metatable Rules preserve full Packs for `assert`, `error`,
  `select`, protected calls, `next`/`pairs`/`ipairs`, and unpacking;
  distinguish empty Pack from one `nil`; retain exact error payload and
  result aliases; and model raw/protected metatable access separately from
  ordinary dispatch. Standard tag-method invocation uses ordinary callable
  resolution, including `__call`; any current VM restriction to a direct
  function is a conformance defect, not a Target fork.
- `error(message[, level])` scalar-adjusts the first two arguments, ignores
  later arguments after evaluation, and accepts a missing message as `nil`.
  Level is an exact integer with nil/missing default one. A String message at
  level one is prefixed by the exact Program call-source location; a
  nonpositive level or non-String payload is thrown unchanged. Level greater
  than one is an explicit unsupported-target case because it observes an
  unbounded caller stack excluded from this profile. A non-integer level takes
  its ordinary type Throw. `assert` with a truthy first argument returns its
  entire input Pack unchanged. A present falsy first argument, either false or
  nil, throws its second argument (including explicit nil), or the exact
  `"assertion failed!"` literal when that argument is absent, through the same
  level-one source-location rule; later arguments are ignored by the error
  path. A missing first argument instead takes the Target's argument/type Throw.
- `getmetatable(v)` has the exact three-way result: no metatable returns nil; a
  metatable whose raw `__metatable` field is non-nil returns that protection
  Value; otherwise it returns the actual metatable identity. `setmetatable(t,m)`
  accepts only a Table and a Table-or-nil metatable and tests the current
  metatable's same raw `__metatable` field: a non-nil protection rejects the
  mutation, while an absent or nil field does not. On success it mutates that
  same table root and returns the same Value. `rawlen` accepts only String or
  Table and never invokes `__len`. `print` reads the mutable global `tostring`
  Value once before conversion, calls that same Value with each argument in
  order, scalar-adjusts the result, and accepts String or Number through the
  Target's exact primitive string conversion. Nil and every other result take
  the typed conversion Throw. Its captured `tostring` application is a
  `RejectYield` internal edge: a yielded conversion takes the canonical
  C-call-boundary Throw before any later element/output, rather than creating
  a print continuation. It preserves its other declared outcomes and performs
  the ordered external-output effect; it does not call `__tostring` directly.
- Iterator construction preserves Lua 5.3's `pairs` `__pairs` branch on any
  input Value and its exact three-result Pack. With no `__pairs`, it returns
  the exact initial `next` callable identity, the unchanged input identity,
  and `nil`; it creates no iterator. A later `next` call performs its own Table
  check. `ipairs` returns the fixed initial `ipairsaux` callable identity, the
  unchanged input identity, and exact Integer zero; it does not allocate a
  closure. Its auxiliary performs the Target's ordinary integer access sequence
  and stops with exactly one `nil` when the selected value is nil, as does the
  default `pairs`/`next` iterator. Current helpers that terminate those
  iterators with the empty Pack, conflate their closures, or use a raw access
  where the Target declares ordinary access are conformance defects.
- `pcall` and `xpcall` have separate causal rows. They carry the protected
  callback's exact arguments and every outcome, convert caught failure to the
  declared false-result Pack, preserve resource duties, and retain a yielded
  continuation. `pcall` uses ordinary callable resolution for its first
  argument; `xpcall` does the same for its protected first argument but requires
  a direct Function as its scalar-adjusted error handler. `xpcall` additionally
  relates the protected Throw payload to the handler's one error argument and
  relates the handler outcome back to the owner. Independent callback rows with
  coincident Pack variables are not this relation.
- Standard Table operations have per-operation Lua 5.3 routes rather than one
  raw-range shortcut. `concat`, `insert`, `move`, `remove`, `sort`, and
  `unpack` use the specified `__len`, ordinary integer `__index`, and ordinary
  integer `__newindex` routes in exact evaluation/mutation order, so a
  non-Table with the required metatable capability remains feasible.
  `table.move` preserves source/destination identities. Only after a nonempty
  range passes its integer overflow checks and the destination can overlap
  (`f < t <= e`) does an explicit non-nil destination argument perform the
  guarded ordinary Target `LUA_OPEQ` application (including `__eq` and its
  outcomes); an equality result may choose backward copy even for distinct
  identities. Empty or non-overlapping moves do not invoke `__eq`. It then
  chooses the direction required by the resulting overlap law and returns the
  destination. `table.pack` creates a
  fresh table, preserves each argument position (nil positions remain absent
  raw entries), writes exact field `n`, and returns it. `table.sort` computes
  `__len` first; only a length greater than one validates an optional
  comparator as a direct Function. Each comparator call scalar-adjusts one
  result by Lua truthiness; the default path uses the ordinary `<` relation.
  Comparator and default-order applications are `RejectYield` edges, so a
  yielded comparator or ordering metamethod takes the canonical C-call-boundary
  Throw; their remaining outcomes and mutation order remain visible. The
  Wippy-only `getn`, `maxn`, `create`, `freeze`, and `isfrozen` are explicitly
  direct-Table/raw laws. `getn` returns one Integer raw sequence border.
  `maxn` returns one Number equal to the maximum among all present positive
  numeric keys, including non-integral keys, or Number zero when none exists;
  it ignores nonnumeric and nonpositive keys. Neither operation invokes
  `__len`. `create` requires exactly two nonnegative Integers,
  returns one fresh mutable empty Table, and its capacities are structural
  hints with no semantic capacity bound; a negative or non-Integer capacity
  Throws. `freeze` and `isfrozen` retain the shallow laws below.
  All operations retain their exact optional/default arguments, aliases,
  in-place ordering, and type, invalid-key, protected-metatable, and
  frozen-route Throws. Freeze remains the shallow law stated in the Heap
  section.
- String pattern operations derive the complete capture Pack for every literal
  pattern. Dynamic patterns use the Pack domain's unbounded open tail and never
  a fixed capture count. `find`, `match`, `gmatch`/`gfind`, and `gsub`
  distinguish no-match, whole-match, captures, position captures, callback
  scalarization, aliasing, and all row-authorized callback outcomes. `gsub`'s
  replacement relation is closed per successful match: a String or Number
  replacement uses its exact replacement-escape law; a direct Function
  replacement receives the complete capture Pack, or the whole match when
  there are no captures; and a Table replacement performs one ordinary
  `IndexGet` using that same first-capture-or-whole-match scalar key. The Table
  access has its own exact raw-presence/`__index` delegation, aliases, effects,
  causal outcomes, and result correlation with that match; it is not a
  callback shorthand or a Rule reconstruction. Function replacement and this
  Table-access edge are `RejectYield` C-call-boundary edges. Their selected
  scalar result is `nil`/`false` to retain the whole match, String-or-Number to
  apply the replacement bytes, and every other Value kind to take the typed
  replacement Throw. The standard
  `gmatch` relation returns exactly one produced iterator Value. Its
  unobservable iteration state is part of that one fresh iterator closure root,
  never a second fresh root or Pack result; cursor transitions mutate that
  closure root and iteration ends with the empty Pack. The
  current two-result runtime helper is a conformance defect.
  Literal `pack`/`unpack` formats derive exact input/output Packs; dynamic
  formats retain a typed open Pack plus the fixed final position.
- The remaining string operations have their own byte-versus-character index,
  negative/default bound, coercion, empty/multi-result, format-directive,
  repetition/overflow, and Throw laws. In particular `byte` may return any
  finite result length through Pack's open-tail relation, `char` consumes the
  entire input Pack, and `format`/`packsize` never borrow a fixed argument or
  directive-count cap from an implementation helper. The base `tostring`
  operation and every admitted `string.format` conversion that invokes
  `__tostring` carry their own ordinary meta-application edge and
  `RejectYield` disposition; neither borrows `print`'s captured global lookup
  nor propagates a Yield through its non-yieldable library frame.
- Numeric and UTF-8 operations have operation-specific arity/default/coercion,
  integer-versus-float, NaN/infinity, range, error, and result-representation
  laws. The exact Target-machine relations for `math.random` and
  `math.randomseed` thread the concrete hidden PRNG state: `math.random`
  returns the target-selected result and successor PRNG state, while
  `math.randomseed` applies its ordered seed effect and ordinary result/Throw
  law to that same state. The state-insensitive abstract Rule then
  existentially hides that concrete state at each operation boundary and
  retains the full exact zero/one/two-argument result range rather than a
  seed/history-correlated subset. This is recorded as explicit precision loss:
  no certificate, must conclusion, or evidence may claim a seed/history/result
  correlation from the abstraction. Neither operation has a Factor key,
  analyzer-visible mutable root or state transition, read/write/invalidation
  dependency, or FrameAccessClosure root for PRNG state; `math.randomseed`
  remains ordered only by the existing Program effect/control order. This is
  an observational quotient in the abstract Rule, not a claim that the exact
  Target machine has no PRNG state or that two executable random results are
  independent.
  `math.min` and `math.max` require at least one Value, compare candidates by
  the ordinary `<` relation (including `__lt` and its outcomes), and return the
  original first tie-winning Value identity rather than a coerced Number.
  `utf8.codes` returns the fixed initial codes-auxiliary callable identity, the
  unchanged String identity, and exact Integer zero; unlike `gmatch`, it does
  not allocate an iterator closure. UTF-8 iteration advances by decoded byte
  width, rejects invalid input through a typed outcome, and never adopts a
  current implementation panic or multibyte-index bug.
- Coroutine rows distinguish creation, user Yield, host/system Yield,
  resumption, wrap rethrow, invalid-carrier Throw, current-provider-state restoration,
  and the exact Lua 5.3 `running` result `(current thread, is-main Boolean)`.
  `coroutine.create` and `coroutine.wrap` require a direct Function first
  argument rather than applying `__call`; their exact Values and effect occurrences
  cross protected calls and produced invocations without losing continuation
  or resource identity.
- The Wippy `coroutine.spawn` row requires a Lua Function as its
  scalar-adjusted first argument, ignores later arguments after evaluation,
  and otherwise Throws. It system-Yields through its existing Target
  continuation/event law and Link boundary correspondence, which bind the
  parent suspension, that exact Function and retained closure environment, and
  the created child activation.
  The carrier's sealed protocol case consumes the parent suspension generation
  once, enables one detached child application with the empty Pack, and
  resumes the parent continuation with the empty Pack. Child entry and parent
  resume are sibling enabled events in one sealed Target causal partial-order
  relation: neither precedes the other unless that same relation declares a
  causal edge, and each legal sibling order is a concrete nondeterministic
  structural alternative. This is not a synchronous call and reads or mutates
  no provider scheduling state or ordering Factor. No child handle is returned.
  Every child
  Normal/Return/Throw/Yield/Cancel has one closed event/protocol case: Yield
  retains its exact child continuation generation and names its allowed
  resume/cancel trigger, while each terminal outcome names the detached
  completion endpoint and its exact resource-duty discharge or external
  handoff. Child outcomes do not become parent outcomes. Thus captured Values
  escape to the child, but a child mutation never becomes a synchronous proof
  at the statement after `spawn`.
- `errors.new(x)` ignores later arguments and creates one fresh Error. A String
  supplies its message. A Table form raw-reads a required String `message`, an
  optional String `kind`, optional Boolean `retryable`, and optional Table
  `details`; a present optional field of another type is ignored, while a
  missing/non-String message and every other first-argument kind Throw. Details
  are a deliberate hardened-dialect restriction: the snapshot is a finite flat
  string-keyed map of only nil, Boolean, Integer, Number, and String values.
  Non-String keys are ignored; a nested Table or another Value kind takes the
  typed unsupported-detail Throw. The immutable snapshot retains no input Heap
  or Table identity.
- `errors.wrap(parent, context)` requires each argument to be String or Error,
  ignores later arguments, retains an Error parent identity when present, and
  creates one fresh wrapper. An Error context contributes its raw message; an
  Error parent contributes its displayed message and copies kind, retryable,
  and primitive detail snapshot content, never a Heap identity. `errors.is(value, kind)` accepts String-or-Number
  primitive conversion for `kind`, returns false for a non-Error, and otherwise
  compares the exact kind bytes. The immutable Error metatable and method table
  contain `__tostring`, `__concat`, `kind`, `retryable`, `details`, and
  `message` Target operations. On a non-Error receiver the four methods return
  respectively `""`, nil, nil, and `""`. On an Error they return exact kind
  bytes, Boolean-or-nil retryability, nil or one fresh outer detail Table
  initialized from the immutable primitive snapshot, and raw message. Each
  `details()` call allocates exactly that one outer Table; its later ordinary
  mutation cannot affect the stored snapshot. `__tostring` exposes `context .. ": " .. message` when context is
  nonempty. `__concat` accepts only Error, String, or Number operands, uses
  their exact primitive display bytes without invoking another `__tostring`,
  and Throws for every other operand; this is the hardened Target law even if
  a current runtime formats opaque pointer-bearing Values.
- Hidden captured traces are observationally erased: `errors.call_stack`,
  `Error:stack()`, and any provider access to hidden trace fields are excluded.
  Historical fixture APIs not present in the exact contract above are rejected
  rather than supplied by a compatibility signature.
- A project host contract, not a package-name string, supplies every admitted
  process/channel/time/resource/stream/global/provider operation. It carries
  module-versus-global binding, static exports, Values/outcomes, lifecycle,
  transfer, callback/re-entry/cancellation, semantic content identity, and
  binding-world revision. Every mutable runtime/provider root names its
  provider/Target-declared owning Factor and is an explicit input to that
  Factor's Rules, not Program identity. `channel.select` additionally seals the finite
  channel-to-payload result relation for its source cases; equality with the
  selected channel refines only the correlated payload arm. Dynamic case
  inventories remain opaque.

The host contract is a content-addressed finite relation over existing Target,
Program, static-type, and Value handles, not prose or a legacy manifest. Every
runtime exposure/member selector row carries one exact Value endpoint and one
dispatch mode. Its rows map:
`(global-or-module exposure, exact binding path, endpoint, dispatch mode)` to
a stable provider root and, when paired with an existing exposure-access
occurrence, to that endpoint Value; `(provider root, exact export path)` to an
authored static type application; an initial root, declared provider ABI input,
or existing Target outcome/result coordinate to a provider-instance capability;
`(capability, exact member key, endpoint, dispatch mode)` to that endpoint
Value at an existing member-access occurrence;
`(exact direct Function endpoint)` to one canonical sealed
`EndpointTarget(endpoint) = Target operation` record;
and a direct exposure/member call candidate references its call-independent
selector row, that exact `EndpointTarget`, and the caller's existing
application/boundary row. Each selector-candidate identity is keyed by its
existing Program access occurrence plus its exact selector tuple; it contains
no call application, formal, receiver binding, or substitution. The exact
existing application/boundary row alone owns its runtime/direct/method call form, formal
correspondence, receiver binding, and static substitution, and Link validates
that its Target operation is the result of its referenced `EndpointTarget`.
It also alone seals any application-scoped Pack classes described below.
`EndpointTarget` supplies only the Target operation; it never supplies a call
form, formal, receiver, substitution, `ValueClass`, or class ledger.
`EndpointTarget` is partial-functional at its endpoint key: Link rejects two
rows that assign different Target operations to the same exact direct Function
endpoint. It permits distinct application/boundary rows with different call
forms, receiver bindings, formals, or substitutions. Distinct runtime behavior
requires distinct Function endpoint identities or explicit wrapper Functions. Thus
exposure/member selection, endpoint-to-operation dispatch, and application
binding are three composable identities rather than copied dispatch authority.
Global and module exposures may be deliberately aliased or distinct. Host
modules are import leaves: a host contract may call only its sealed
operations/callbacks and declares no hidden module initializer dependency. A
future host-to-module dependency would need one explicit content-addressed Link
edge before it could be admitted.

Link validates and freezes these rows. `Lookup` mode is the ordinary current
global/cell/Table/metatable lookup law: it selects an endpoint only on the
guarded branch where that lookup yields the row's exact endpoint. The
Value/identity Factor seeds `Capability(v, c)` only from the
declared initial-root, ABI-input, Target-result, or matching exposure sources.
It transports that capability with the same receiver Value identity through
ordinary alias, Heap store/load, callback/formal, and boundary flow. Only an
explicit sealed cross-identity transfer law may transform or lose it; no
receiver type, endpoint, annotation, or inferred fact may create it. For an
exact existing access occurrence `x`, later call application/boundary row `a`,
receiver `r`, capability `c`, key `k`, and endpoint `v_h`,

```text
HostMemberSelect(x, r, k, v_h)
  iff Capability(r, c)
   and CurrentLookup(r, k) = v_h
   and MemberSelect(x, c, k, v_h, Lookup, ...).

HostExposureSelect(x, exposure, path, v_h)
  iff CurrentLookup(exposure, path) = v_h
   and ExposureSelect(x, exposure, path, v_h, Lookup, ...).

HostEndpointCall(a, v_h, op)
  iff DirectFunction(v_h)
   and EndpointTarget(v_h) = op
   and ApplicationBoundary(a, op, runtime-call-form, formal-correspondence,
                           receiver-binding, static-substitution).

HostMemberCall(x, r, k, a, op)
  iff exists v_h . HostMemberSelect(x, r, k, v_h)
   and HostEndpointCall(a, v_h, op).

HostExposureCall(x, exposure, path, a, op)
  iff exists v_h . HostExposureSelect(x, exposure, path, v_h)
   and HostEndpointCall(a, v_h, op).
```

For a `SealedVirtual` selector row, only the `CurrentLookup = v_h` premise in
the corresponding `Host*Select` relation is replaced by that row's sealed
no-overwrite premise for the same endpoint; `HostEndpointCall` never reads a
mutable source slot.

The member and exposure selector Rules read their exact terminal tuple while
evaluating the source expression: the member selector reads both
`Capability(r, c)` and its current lookup outcome, and the exposure selector
reads its current lookup outcome. Their result is the ordinary endpoint Value,
not merely a receiver capability. A selector ends at its access occurrence
`x`; it can return, store, pass, or otherwise flow that Value without any call
application existing in the selecting body. Direct and method-call syntax
first evaluates its callee to that Value, selects the operation only through
`EndpointTarget(v_h)`, and then applies `a`'s own existing application/boundary
row for runtime call form, receiver insertion, formal correspondence, and static
substitution. It never rereads the source binding or member slot after callee
evaluation, and it never reuses those fields from a selector row. The endpoint
Value itself flows through aliases, Heap, callbacks, returns, module boundaries,
and other boundaries. Consequently,
`local f = g; g = user; f()` invokes the host operation from `f`'s exact
captured direct-Function `v_h` and the later `f()` application's own boundary
row, while a later `g()` performs a new lookup and may invoke `user`; likewise
`local f = r.m; r.m = user; f()` invokes the operation from the endpoint
captured in `f` with `f()`'s own receiver/formal/substitution row, while a
later `r.m` lookup sees `user`. A slot write therefore affects future selector
evaluations only, not an already flowed endpoint Value.

`EndpointTarget` applies only when the evaluated callee is the exact direct
Function endpoint declared by that record. A Table, userdata, other
non-Function Value, or unknown value that is callable only through `__call`
has no host endpoint-target candidate: ordinary Call/metatable resolution reads
its current `__call` route and retains its ordinary/opaque alternatives. Thus a
later metatable write cannot be bypassed by a stale host endpoint relation. A
may-lookup retains the matching direct-Function host branch and every
ordinary/metatable/opaque branch under their exact Guard alternatives. The
sole other host-selector mode is `SealedVirtual`; Link admits its member or
exposure selector row only when an exact sealed Target no-overwrite law
proves that no source field, metatable, or binding write can replace or
override that direct Function endpoint. An authored/annotated receiver type
only participates through a later application's static validation/substitution
and can never create a selector, provider provenance, or satisfy either
lookup/no-overwrite premise.

Exposure and member selector rows are distinct exact Link relations over only
their access occurrence and selector tuple. A direct exposure/member call
boundary may reference that selector identity, the one canonical
`EndpointTarget`, and its own existing application/boundary identity, but none
owns or copies another's fields. No synthetic member or type-based dispatch is
permitted. Call resolution at a source slot unions only these enabled host
selector rows with ordinary Table/metatable candidates; after evaluation, a
known exact direct Function endpoint uses only its `EndpointTarget` to select
the operation and its own application/boundary row to bind it, while an unknown
capability, key, receiver, or endpoint stays on the explicit opaque-call
branch.

A `Lookup` selector row reads its exact ordinary Heap/metatable lookup
dependency (base/root, key or binding path, raw presence, dispatch, and
endpoint); that read is in the Rule input/read log and a relevant write
invalidates the selector result branch, not an already flowed endpoint call.
`HostEndpointCall` reads the evaluated direct-Function callee endpoint, its
content-addressed `EndpointTarget` record, and the exact application/boundary
row, never a mutable source slot; overwriting the callee binding itself remains
an ordinary Value-flow invalidation. A non-Function callable instead reads its
ordinary `__call` lookup chain and is invalidated by that chain's exact reads.
Access occurrence, exposure/member selector row, endpoint, direct-Function
classification, endpoint-target record, referenced application/boundary row,
dispatch mode, instance seed/transport law, and any sealed no-overwrite Target
law participate in Link/artifact identity and host exposure, selection,
exposure-call, and member-call coverage; mutable Heap state itself
remains a Factor input and is never serialized as Link state. No package
string, Rule, signature registry, inferred runtime type, or `as`/`::` claim can
manufacture a host member or exposure call.

An operation such as `channel.select` whose result fields depend on one source
case also has a finite call-specific Link arm range. Each arm is keyed by the
existing application and source case occurrence and names that case's exact
receiver, send/receive/default/closed kind, payload source, result-field
layout, and existing Target operation/member row with its endpoint/dispatch
predicate. A dynamically sized or opaque case inventory contributes one
explicit opaque arm rather than inventing cases. Equality with the
selected-channel result retains precisely the alias-compatible arms and
therefore refines all correlated result fields together. Marginal result unions
and the legacy string-marker schema are not semantic authorities.

`collectgarbage`, `bit32`, `string.dump`, `debug.getinfo`,
`debug.getlocal`, `debug.traceback`, `debug.getmetatable`,
`errors.call_stack`, and `Error:stack()` are outside this
analyzed profile. Bit operators are the language operators already represented
by Program; dumped executable representations conflict with the closed-code
law; stack/frame inspection would make an unbounded caller stack a semantic
input and invalidate body reuse unless a separate stack-language abstraction
were added. `debug.getinfo` is excluded as one whole callable rather than
splitting its argument-dependent identity. The already excluded debug mutation
operations remain excluded.

`debug.getupvalue(f, i)` is the sole admitted debug read. It requires a direct
Function and Integer index; without a debug Factor it selects the sealed
upvalue name and exact captured Cell value, or returns the one-slot Pack
`(nil)` when the index is outside that finite layout; bad arguments retain the
Target's typed Throw. The out-of-range result is not the empty Pack, so final
list expansion and `select("#", debug.getupvalue(f, i))` observe one result.

Every excluded binding is represented in the sealed initial environment by
one ordinary denied-operation identity whose only call outcome is the typed
unsupported-target Throw. It therefore flows through aliases and dynamic
table reads without a new Value kind or outcome vocabulary. Ordinary Heap
mutation may replace that field with a user Value, after which calls follow
that Value and gain no denied operation semantics. Host contracts cannot
reintroduce a denied identity under another binding. A checked-in fixture using
an excluded operation stays in the source denominator and must reach that
exact Throw/diagnostic; its historical inferred facts are not preserved.

Error-stack erasure is an explicit observational quotient. It removes only the
Lua/Go trace payload from each Error while preserving nominal identity,
kind/message/details/retryability/context, parent and argument aliases, and all
ordinary outcomes. Every admitted Error operation, metatable method, equality,
string conversion, serialization boundary, and sealed provider relation must
be congruent under this projection. `errors.new`/`wrap` may still allocate
fresh Error identities, but their caller stack is not a Factor or cache input.
Seal rejects any operation/provider capable of observing the erased payload.

Only project-sealed callable, global, module, and provider relations enter
Link under an exact typed contract. Post-seal mutation or an unsealed entry has
no analyzed boundary relation.
Callbacks, retained callbacks, host transfer, suspension, resumption,
cancellation, and host/system-yield behavior are explicit typed boundary and
outcome relations.

Authored effects have one authority: the canonical typed `RowSpec` in source,
module, or provider contracts. It preserves Koka-style duplicate occurrences
and formal rows where authored. Concrete external causal relations
record callbacks, retention, mutation, suspension/resumption/cancellation, and
outcomes. Analyzed operational path, heap, escape, placement, ownership, or
typestate results are Factor conclusions and certificates; they are never
serialized back as provider authority.

There is one Program. Analysis CFG, WIR, source-program overlays,
engine-program inventories, path-string identities, and reconstructed source
relations do not exist.

The engine's public conceptual vocabulary remains:

```text
Term  Guard  State  Factor[T]  Rule  Solver  Query[T]
```

Program language handles such as body, call, allocation, field, effect
operation, and source span belong to `program`, not to the engine.
Implementation details such as partitions, roots, schedules, caches, and
equations are private and do not appear as additional public lifecycle nouns.
`Mu` is the one mathematical fixed-point binder. The private equation system
has one kind of variable: a structural `Point`. A private `Group` is one atomic,
ordered, multi-input transfer term whose output is a Point; it is never a state
coordinate, scheduler/WTO node, Query target, publisher, or second fact plane.
Several Groups may contribute alternative paths to the same Point. Ordered
inputs inside one Group are simultaneous operands, not alternatives.

The equation compiler projects every Group input occurrence to an
input-Point-to-output-Point edge and computes one deterministic point-only WTO.
Its outer and nested cyclic components each have a Point head selected solely
from canonical Point identity. Rule or Group identity may order private work
inside a transfer but can never select a WTO head. A head exists for every
cyclic Point component, including pure Rule/reduction, dynamic call, callback,
and resume cycles with no Program/Link recurrence witness. Acyclic components
have no operational `Mu`, and there is no whole-program or
largest-Factor-partition `Mu`.

For a cyclic component `K`, the local recurrence carrier consists of exact
shared outer support plus `M_K`, the canonical unique union of every Factor
written or explicitly carried by a Group whose output lies in `K` and whose
ordered inputs contain at least one Point in `K`. The selected footprint is the
canonical union of every such member's complete sealed write-target alternatives
plus the full declared reachable footprint of every explicitly carried Factor;
a Carry is never reduced to the writer's target list. This footprint scopes
widening inside each selected Factor. This is
an occurrence-local write/carry closure, not a transitive traversal of the cold
Factor-incidence SCCs. A Factor outside `M_K`, or a coordinate outside its
associated selected footprint, is recomputed exactly from the complete
right-hand side and may be excluded from widening only after the closure proves
that it cannot sustain an infinite recursive change. It is never preserved
stale. Support has no Factor rank or widening: it evolves by exact structural
transfer, boundary reindexing, union, and declared monotone prune over the
finite sealed Guard universe.

Every Program/Link edge independently owns its exact Guard reset,
alpha-renaming, freshness/restoration, outcome, and typed boundary
substitutions. The point WTO never invents or unions those interfaces.
Program/Link `Mu` marks exact semantic recurrence transport only and never
authorizes or selects an operational head. Operational heads implement the one
mathematical fixed-point protocol; they do not create a public semantic noun,
second graph, carrier, or lifecycle object.

`Term` is the one compact tagged identity minted by an immutable Program shard
for an entity that can be a Factor subject. Its semantic identity is the
shard's complete semantic identity plus a dense shard-local tagged index.
Engine and domains use that exact identity unchanged; they never wrap, alias,
re-intern, or mint a second Term. A project link retains shard identity and
connects shards only through explicit boundary relations; it does not
re-intern shard entities into a project-global Term plane. The in-memory
representation may pack a dense project shard slot with the local index, while
hot body/domain storage indexes directly by the local dense component.

Raw packed bits are stable only while that exact shard namespace is retained.
Eviction/rebuild may choose a different representation but has the same
semantic `(shard, local)` identity; handle-bearing in-memory artifacts then
miss and rebuild rather than remap. Its Go package placement follows the final
dependency graph and is not an architectural law. A domain may instead use its
own typed key (for example a heap slot), but every source identity inside it is
still a Program-minted Term.

## Formal model

### Theory basis

The engine instantiates established results rather than inventing an iteration
calculus:

- Knaster-Tarski and Cousot/Cousot supply the collecting and abstract
  fixed-point semantics; existence of a least fixed point does not imply finite
  Kleene iteration ([Cousot & Cousot, POPL 1977](https://www.di.ens.fr/~cousot/COUSOTpapers/POPL77.shtml)).
- Bourdoncle supplies deterministic nested weak topological ordering and
  localized head iteration over equation variables
  ([Efficient Chaotic Iteration Strategies with Widenings, 1993](https://doi.org/10.1007/BFb0039704)).
- Amato/Scozzari supply the separation of external ingress from internal/back
  contributions at a widening head and the checked localized post-fixpoint
  discipline
  ([Localizing Widening and Narrowing, SAS 2013](https://www.sci.unich.it/~scozzari/paper/SAS2013.pdf)).
- Monniaux supplies the general well-founded widening contract: termination is
  a property against arbitrary successive proposals, not monotonicity of the
  binary widening function
  ([A Minimalistic Look at Widening Operators, 2009](https://arxiv.org/abs/0902.3722)).
- Demanded evaluation is accepted only by from-scratch consistency with the
  same complete equation/SCC closure and iteration policy
  ([Demanded Abstract Interpretation, PLDI 2021](https://plv.colorado.edu/papers/dai-pldi21.pdf)).
- Separation logic's local frame principle and bi-abduction are the comparison
  standard for compositional heap footprints; they do not authorize a second
  symbolic heap or procedure-summary engine
  ([Compositional Shape Analysis by Means of Bi-Abduction, POPL 2009](https://www.cs.ox.ac.uk/people/hongseok.yang/paper/popl09.pdf)).
- Incorrectness Separation Logic and Infer Pulse are the comparison standard
  for compositional true-bug and latent-condition evidence. Their
  under-approximating bug-presence interpretation is kept distinct from this
  analyzer's over-approximating must-safety and placement certificates
  ([Incorrectness Separation Logic, POPL 2020](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7363320/),
  [Infer Pulse](https://fbinfer.com/docs/next/checker-pulse/)).
- Actor isolation and placement must preserve graph ownership, affine transfer,
  and remote-access effects rather than reducing placement to lexical escape
  ([Affine Types for Isolated Actors](https://arxiv.org/abs/1704.03093),
  [Behavioural Types for Non-Uniform Memory Accesses](https://arxiv.org/abs/1602.03599)).

The 2025 verified WTO solvers establish a useful post-fixpoint proof shape but
use fuel to make the mechanized functions total; fuel is not a semantic
termination argument here. Every production loop terminates by the finite
structural measures and admitted well-founded domain ranks below.

The mathematical kernel is one typed nominal relation semantics. This notation
does not add production engine nouns. For each finite Program/Link boundary
schema `Γ`, let `Cfg(Γ)` be concrete configurations containing exactly the
values, full result packs, lexical cells, heap, metatables, closures,
continuations, module state, sealed external state, and any hidden
Target-machine state needed to compose exact Target relations (including PRNG
state) at that boundary. Concrete identities range over a countably infinite nominal atom
set and are equivariant under consistent renaming.

```text
Out(Δ) = Normal(Cfg(Δ))
       + Return(Pack, Cfg(Δ))
       + Throw(Pack, Cfg(Δ))
       + Yield(Pack, Cfg(Δ))
       + Cancel(Cfg(Δ))
       + internal Break/Goto routes

Rel(Γ, Δ) = P(Cfg(Γ) × Out(Δ))
Pack       = Val*
```

`nil` is an ordinary Pack slot and is distinct from the empty Pack. These
typed relations are closed under union, guarded restriction, typed
product/natural join, equality and alias diagonals, relational composition,
existential hiding, typed reindexing, non-injective actual/formal binding, and
nominal fresh binding. Concrete complement and absence tests exist, but no
negative premise may occur recursively in an ascending may-equation. Priority,
raw absence, dominance, key validity, and similar negative judgments are
therefore sealed target primitives, stratified must judgments, or separately
checked structural projections with monotone abstract transfers.

Every admitted Program occurrence and target operation denotes one relation in
this algebra. Sequential composition feeds only `Normal` onward and propagates
other outcomes to their Program routes. A call through boundary `σ` is:

```text
Callσ(B) = Enterσ ; Freshen([[B]]) ; union_outcome Exitσ,outcome
```

`Enterσ` retains the caller frame and relates actuals to formals; it is not a
rename because two formals may receive the same actual. `Freshen` alpha-renames
nominal allocation binders for every application. Resume is a binary boundary
over the stored continuation state and the current resumer/provider Factor
inputs; it
never restores a stale whole heap.

For every monotone cyclic equation family,

```text
Φ(X) = Init ∪ Transfer(X)
[[cycle]] = lfp Φ
```

exists by Knaster-Tarski. This is the semantic fixed point. Program/Link `Mu`
describes exact transport at a real source/call/resume/module boundary; the
private equation `Mu` only schedules and stabilizes any cyclic Rule SCC. A
compiler SCC never invents semantic transport, and no semantic cycle executes
by recursive Go calls. Infinite execution has no fabricated terminal outcome;
any future definite-divergence diagnostic is a separate sound Query/domain,
not a change to safety reachability.
