The concrete relation is exact. A finite terminating analyzer is not exact for
arbitrary recursion, integers, Pack lengths, dynamic keys, or allocation
identities. For every abstract context `Γ`, the implemented domains must
therefore declare an abstraction/concretization (preferably a Galois insertion)
and the exact fragment for which their primitive operation is complete. All
other admitted cases return a marked sound overapproximation. “Maximally
precise” means best correct approximation for each implemented primitive where
computable and no avoidable loss in composition; it never means globally
optimal invariants for every program.

For each declared Factor `i`:

```text
A_i = K_i → V_i
```

`K_i` is a stable typed, totally ordered, hashable dependency-unit space.
`V_i` is a bounded abstract join-semilattice for the operations the engine consumes:
explicit bottom/top, equality, partial order, binary least upper bound,
widening, and any domain-supported meet/narrowing. The engine does not require
an algorithm for arbitrary infinite joins. Every declared widening `▽` is a
pointwise sound extrapolation:

```text
a ⊔ b ⊑ a ▽ b
a ▽ a = a
a ▽ b != a  implies  rank(a ▽ b) ≺ rank(a)
```

The first law holds for every pair of normalized elements and the second is
semantic equality, not pointer identity. The idempotence law is required and
executable-tested because it is the authority for skipping a plane whose two
immutable input roots are already equal; a domain that needs a widening
"clock tick" on equal input is not an admissible Factor. The strict-transition
rank is well-founded and applies for every proposed `b`, not only proposals
from one fixed transfer: equivalently every sequence
`a[n+1] = a[n] ▽ b[n]` stabilizes. This general widening law is mandatory
because a nested WTO component may present different sound candidates when an
enclosing head changes and restarts it. A law only for one fixed monotone
transfer is insufficient. The executable carrier checks every selected
transition before publication: the result must contain both operands and every
semantic change must strictly descend its declared finite lexicographic rank.
This is a proof guard for the executed transition, not a universal proof that
an opaque domain callback is total, deterministic, monotone, or lawful for
every admissible proposal. Finite carriers may discharge that universal
obligation exhaustively; an unbounded carrier requires a checked proof or an
explicitly versioned reviewed theorem in the trusted domain boundary.

With one fixed component interface, ascending support and Group candidates do
not retract. The sealed Guard support can grow only finitely often; between
support changes, structural finiteness of reachable keys or the admitted
map-support stabilization theorem leaves only finitely many selected
Factor/key/Guard cells, each with finitely many strict rank descents. Nested
termination follows by induction over the finite
WTO nesting. A support removal, weaker candidate, changed reindex, activation,
revocation, or interface revision during ascent starts a fresh comparison epoch
from the canonical reset; it is never another ascending proposal. Ranked
narrowing may remove support only after the verified ascending post-fixpoint.
No scheduler cutoff
substitutes for these measures. An optional narrowing supplies its own
well-founded descending rank and sandwich law. Executable laws and adversarial
proposal sequences test these obligations but do not replace their universal
proof. A
Factor must also prove that its reachable
abstract key support is structurally finite for a sealed Program, or provide a
canonical key abstraction whose map-support widening stabilizes at `Mu`.
Cardinality is never that abstraction. The total map is represented canonically and
sparsely relative to a Factor-declared default element: writing the default
removes the stored entry, so there is one representation of the same semantic
map. An unstored-key read still logs the root/key absence and is invalidated by
the first non-default write. An undemanded computation is a distinct
operational state, never a Factor value. A Factor whose key may not exist lifts
existence into its element/default law; the engine never assumes that storage
absence means either semantic top or nonexistence.

For the one engine operation that needs a keyed observation, a Factor also
supplies an opaque typed unit partitioner. Given one of its immutable roots, a
compatible Guard region, and a declared dependency unit, it partitions that
region only by the normalized value at that unit, including the Factor default
and stored/absent presence token. It coalesces regions whose normalized unit
observation is equal even when unrelated keys in the same Factor root differ.
For an exact-key read the unit is exactly that key: the partitioner may not
walk, fingerprint, compare, or partition another key or the containing plane.
The engine receives only private Factor-owned observation handles and Guard
pieces; it cannot inspect `V_i`, compare semantic values, or project another
unit. The Factor proves that this partition is exact for the requested unit,
finite for its sealed support, and that a write invalidates every unit token its
declared closure law requires. This is an input primitive of `Product`, not a
generic semantic Factor-projection service.

A Rule read schema is a sealed finite staged DAG. Its initial nodes name typed
Factor units and Guard predicates. A later typed selector may inspect only the
already partitioned observations named by its predecessor nodes and returns a
finite set of presealed next units, or one Factor-proved presealed summary unit
covering that set. Its domain law proves that the returned units/summary cover
every concrete unit reachable from those predecessor observations; a summary's
stable-token closure law invalidates it for every covered write. A selector may
neither inspect an unprojected Factor nor mint a key, edge, or semantic
relation. `Product` applies every selected/covering unit partitioner to the
same current Guard region and repeats until that DAG closes, before the Rule
callback runs. It logs selector inputs, every selected or covering unit,
default/presence observation, and the resulting Guard pieces. Cyclic,
unbounded, or undeclared selection is a seal/composition error. Thus a dynamic
heap/root/key choice remains a typed domain decision while its exact regional
correlation and reverse invalidation remain ordinary engine work.

A Factor-output Rule's write schema is likewise either exact or a sealed finite
staged write-target DAG. A dynamic target node may inspect only the
already-projected observation handles in that Rule's completed read DAG; it
returns only typed, presealed `(its output Factor, dependency-unit)` write
capabilities or one Factor-proved presealed target-summary capability for that
same output Factor. A structural-output Rule has no write-target schema, plane
patch, or Carry.
It cannot inspect a new
plane, mint a key, root, relation, or output coordinate, or use a generic
whole-plane write fallback. The owning Factor proves that the returned target
set/summary covers every concrete location the Rule can affect under that
regional read tuple, and that its target-summary closure invalidates every
reader which an included concrete write can affect. The Rule logs the resolved
target capability and exact output Guard with its reads. A strong replacement
is legal only when the exact target capability and the owning domain's
pointwise singleton/uniqueness proof hold for every represented concrete
state; otherwise the Rule weakly updates every covered presealed target through
the Factor's ordinary operation. A target summary cannot manufacture a strong
update privilege. Cyclic, unbounded, undeclared, or coverage-incomplete target
selection is a seal/composition error.

Sealing indexes the finite possible target surface in reverse by
`(Factor, unit-or-summary)` without materializing a staged transfer. Backward
demand for an exact queried unit includes every exact or staged writer whose
sealed surface can cover that unit, then the write-target node's predecessor
read closure. Runtime target logs refine only the actual reverse invalidation
slice; they cannot remove a statically possible demanded writer. Thus dynamic
heap/alias writes remain exact-unit demandable without scanning a whole plane,
and the same target disposition is part of the canonical Rule instance and
artifact identity.

A declared opaque `Carry` is different from a read. It transports the selected
immutable Factor plane and its outer support into the output without observing
any terminal, so it neither partitions nor fingerprints that plane. A Rule
with no declared read unit or Guard predicate receives exactly one callback
row for its nonempty intersected outer support. A legacy whole-plane dynamic
read remains lawful only as an explicit expensive declaration: it authorizes
partitioning the whole plane because the callback may choose any key. It is a
named scaling hazard, never the default generated signature, and new dynamic
selection is expressed by the sealed staged selector/summary-unit protocol
above. `Carry` does not provide a capability to perform that dynamic read.

State-dependent partitions never appear as `K_i`. A relational domain chooses
a stable Program-derived variable pack as one key and places the complete
relation over that pack in `V_i`. A heap/key domain chooses a stable aggregate
or allocation root as one key and places its changing symbolic key partition
and slot assignment in `V_i`; join transports assignments through the
domain-defined sound common partition, and its widening proves stabilization
of both partition and contents at `Mu`. Cross-pack or cross-root information
moves only through declared Rules. The Factor key is therefore the atomic
read/invalidation unit unless the owning Factor declares a finer stable
dependency token together with a closure law proving that every affected token
is invalidated. The engine never assumes that individual variables,
equivalence classes, or range cells are independent map keys.

A Factor boundary follows the smallest semantic observation that its actual
consumers require, not historical package size. The existing BDD/FDD carrier
already preserves branch-alternative correlation for every Factor; it does not
decide which semantic observations belong in one Factor. The current
non-splitting obligations are deliberately small: when a Rule, Query, or
certificate needs one of the following, it consumes the components from one
exact joint terminal tuple or one opaque domain relation, never from recombined
marginals:

```text
one Value's presence/kind/type/identity observations
one full Pack with its residual tail and any demanded length/selection result
one heap access resolution: base, key equality, raw presence, dispatch, value
one call boundary: callee completeness, actual/formal Packs, outcome
one resume boundary: stored continuation state, current resumer/provider Factor inputs, outcome
```

These lines are a demand table for domain Rule signatures, not new Factor
kinds and not a global semantic product. Escape, ownership, typestate,
effects, placement, numeric, heap, and value may remain separate Factors.
Rules read them from the same exact terminal tuple. Their componentwise
confluence is a lawful overapproximation; if it loses a cross-domain
correlation, the result is marked and a dependent proof is withheld. A new
relation Factor or reduction is admitted only for a concrete consumer whose
joint observation cannot be proved from existing opaque Factor elements and
same-tuple reads. Adding a domain otherwise adds one typed plane and Rules to
the existing BDD/FDD schema; it changes no Guard language, carrier type, or
engine branch.

A domain may use immutable normalized cases, typed tuples, nominal partitions,
or constraint primitives internally and evaluate them in factored form. Such a
physical helper has no concrete meaning of its own, does not materialize
Cartesian products unless demanded, and cannot hard-code Lua/domain vocabulary
into the engine.

A scalar answer is only a projection of that authority. It may be cached or
published independently only after proving, for the relevant transfer `T`,

```text
πI(x) = πI(y)  =>  πO(T(x)) = πO(T(y)).
```

Projected Pack positions, per-axis value products, separately persisted
effects, or per-root path summaries may never be recombined into evidence.
Canonical bytes/hash denote one normalized abstract value under exact domain
and codec versions; they do not claim a decision procedure for equality of all
concrete model sets.

The whole abstract state is not a bag of independently guarded Factors. Let
`L` be the template-local Program locations of a body: source occurrences,
entry, and one exit for each declared outcome. Program and Link seal only their
finite canonical constituent sets and exact already-resolved boundary rows;
neither constructs, numbers, stores, or persists a candidate universe. Wave C
Composition declares a finite family constructor over those constituent sets
and a pure eligibility predicate. The mathematical family may denote a subset
of Application x Program-body-or-Target-endpoint tuples, but it has no product
rows, handles, ranges, reverse maps, or portable identity.

`I` is the monotone Solver-private set of accepted structural incoming-relation
tuples instantiated from that family during one solve session; it is not an SCC
quotient or Program/Link relation. Its identity formula is the canonical typed
tuple `(family, Application, endpoint constituents)`, so declaration/discovery
order, activation count, capacity, and call-stack path are irrelevant. A typed
trigger Rule may prove and monotonically admit such a tuple only from already
sealed constituents and the family predicate. Domain values therefore neither
invent a constituent nor allocate a portable Candidate identity; they provide
constructive evidence for an instance of a fixed finite constructor. Known
exact Program/Link boundaries seed the same constructor directly. The finite
constituent product is a termination argument, never retained storage.

Each accepted
structural revision is compiled into a fresh deterministic SCC/WTO schedule
with one compiled-equation `Mu` head for
every cyclic component. Both initial compilation and every revision use the
same domain-independent `analysis/engine/internal/schedule.Prepare` SCC/WTO
machinery and the same Point/Group equation compiler; there is no relation-only,
Factor-only, or persistence-owned scheduler. Dynamic relation instances remain
epoch-local compiler inputs and are never portable equation rows. This complete
epoch rebuild is the initial canonical implementation; affected-component
replacement is a later internal
optimization only after a measured regression and semantic-equivalence laws.
The operational head never supplies semantic recurrence transport. A sealed
lazy reverse-activation family index is keyed only by existing Program/Link
application ranges and endpoint-family metadata. Each family carries its typed
trigger Rule coordinate, that Rule's sealed read-schema DAG, its pure direct
eligibility predicate, and its required opaque alternative. Demand visits the
family declaration without enumerating or interning pairs. Only a tuple proved
by its domain trigger receives epoch-local endpoint/boundary work. This is
demand/scheduling metadata: it neither interprets trigger-domain values nor
materializes a Candidate relation. Inactive tuples create no state
contribution, join, or premature widening. The Solver coordinates are:

```text
C = ⨆_body (I_body × L_body)
Σ(c) = (S_c, {f_i,c}_{i ∈ F})
S_c    ⊆ valuations of the sealed Guard atoms
f_i,c  : S_c → A_i
```

For one sealed Solver composition, `F` is its finite typed Factor schema and
`A_i` is the opaque lattice root owned by Factor `i`. This is denotational
shorthand for the existing guarded sparse-root carrier, not a materialized
tuple carrier. Adding a domain changes the composition's sealed Factor schema,
like adding a typed column to one record instance; it does not change State,
Guard, scheduling, or the carrier architecture.

`S_c` is an engine-owned finite exact BDD lattice coordinate. Its only semantic
sources are `Init`, structural transfer/boundary Guard transport, and declared
contradiction Rules; canonical Guard reduction may only preserve that denotation.
A Factor neither owns nor treats support as a key/value conclusion.
Every coordinate update reports the canonical added and removed regions
`S_new minus S_old` and `S_old minus S_new`, even when every plane root is unchanged.
Those regions are semantic deltas, not a cache hint: an added region schedules
each applicable outgoing transfer and Rule over that region, including an
empty-read or Carry-only Rule, while a removed region retracts/recomputes that
Rule's prior guarded contribution before successors are considered. A support
delta is routed by exact source coordinate and Guard compatibility, without
inventing a Factor read or scanning unrelated Factors or Rules.

For dynamic application, the implicit finite family is a mathematical subset
of Program application occurrences x Program bodies/Target endpoints. It is
never sealed or enumerated as data. A proved tuple receives storage only in the
Solver epoch. A statically resolved Target operation may instead cause Link to
seal its exact callback, produced-operation, suspension, and resumption
boundary rows because those are actual two-owner correspondences, not members
of an Application x Operation table. Sealing cost is proportional only to
canonical Program/Target/Link rows actually present plus declared direct
indexes; unselected dynamic pairs contribute zero retained bytes.

The Program location remains the sole source identity. Invocation identity is
Solver-private and never becomes a second Program coordinate. Separate
structural callers keep separate coordinates; equal observed boundaries may
share the compiled equation result through the semantic cache without merging
their States. Equation recurrence follows dependency backedges among those
stable coordinates and converges by the owning Factors' widening at the
compiled-equation `Mu`, not by creating contexts until a quota is full. Exact
Program/Link boundaries independently transport Guard decisions and activation
identity on the transfers that cross them.
A Factor Query root is the exact tuple of its queried Factor dependency unit
(normally one key), its registered Program/Link structural selector, and its
declared outcome projection. A registered structural support-completion root
instead names only one exact occurrence/outcome selector and the
support-projection/evidence mode; it has no Factor unit. Structural selectors
remain only body, application, activation relation, occurrence, and declared
outcome. Demand for a Factor Query starts from both parts of its tuple: Solver
resolves the structural selector to private coordinates and walks backward from
the exact queried Factor/unit through sealed Rules whose exact write/carry or
staged write-target surface can cover it, then through those Rules' declared
read units, write-target predecessors, and ordinary structural predecessors.
Demand for support completion starts from that coordinate's
structural incoming predecessors and every sealed Rule/support-prune dependency
that can preserve or remove its outer support. It does not install every Factor
or Rule attached to a selected occurrence. Query may explicitly request a sound
join of structural coordinates, but any domain-dependent filtering is an
ordinary typed Factor/Rule conclusion computed before projection. Query has no
abstract-context predicate or semantic selector, and no private coordinate
handle enters the public API.

Each occurrence location has one fixed phase: the joint state immediately
before that Program operation. Its Rules transfer that input to the
Program-declared normal or non-local successor locations; outcome exits are
terminal coordinates. Produced Values and conclusions are Program subjects in
the outgoing fiber. A Query for an operation input selects its occurrence; a
Query for a produced conclusion selects the declared successor/outcome and the
operation's Program subject. There is no parallel before/after point plane.

For a sealed Factor schema, the guarded state is the exact finite Boolean
path-condition completion over the recurrence-scoped Program-decision atoms
reachable at a structural invocation coordinate. Its implementation has one
reduced ordered BDD support. Each declared Factor supplies an opaque sparse
MTBDD/FDD function over that same ordered valuation universe; it does not own
an independent guard partition. For a Rule's declared typed read schema, the
engine executes its sealed staged read DAG: it refines the shared support only
by the Factor dependency units and Guard decisions selected by that DAG, using
the Factor-owned unit partitioner, until the regional read closure is complete.
It invokes the Rule on each nonempty exact tuple of that read projection. This preserves
alternative correlation without giving the engine a semantic tuple language:
it cannot name, solve, or relate Pack, heap, value, effect, numeric, or other
domain components. A domain relation remains an opaque Factor element or a
domain Rule. No DNF row list or Cartesian product is materialized merely
because the denotation is written as a guarded product.

At control confluence:

- supports combine by exact BDD union;
- Factor functions join pointwise on their overlap;
- Rules execute over nonempty exact tuples of their complete declared read
  projection on the shared support, so
  every Factor read for one invocation comes from the same alternative;
  successor states merge only after transfer;
- only a Query or an explicitly correlation-insensitive domain operation may
  request a joined Factor projection across compatible alternatives;
- the reduced BDD/FDD form never joins semantic
  alternatives merely because there are many of them.

Exact projection occurs at every structural death interface, not only at
`Mu`. Program records the real scope/consumption boundary for source Values and
Cells. Domain-owned Rules at that boundary perform any existential cleanup
inside their own opaque lattice and prove it by the ordinary Rule soundness
law; a domain uses an exact complete projection when it has one, otherwise it
retains the subject or explicitly marks its own precision loss. Guard removes
a decision only when no downstream Rule/registered Query reads it and
canonical reduction sees equal resulting joint products. A demanded
intermediate observation therefore keeps its subjects and decisions live.
Beyond the Factor-provided opaque unit partitioner used by `Product`, the
engine has no generic semantic Factor-projection subsystem, and nothing is
removed because of leaf count.

Joint semantics does not require a stored DNF. `Product(ReadSet)` partitions
the shared BDD/FDD state by the complete tuple of the Rule's declared and
staged-resolved typed dependency units, then merges equal tuples before
invocation. The checked pure read interface and sealed staged DAG are the
noninterference proof that an omitted plane cannot affect that Rule or its
output; an undeclared read is a composition failure. There is no all-Factor
baseline product. Its compiled input is the Rule's read projection, not the
sealed Factor schema: an exact-key projection calls only that key's typed unit
partitioner, `Carry` contributes no partition, and an empty projection yields
one support row. Fingerprinting, equal-tuple merging, and representative
selection likewise inspect only those projected units. Worst-case
exponential correlation among simultaneously live,
observably distinct values is inherent and reported honestly; the architecture
introduces no node, row, or cardinality cap.

Outer support is the first lattice coordinate of a carrier, not an ambient
precondition to its plane operations.  For carriers
`X = (S_X,{f_i^X})` and `Y = (S_Y,{f_i^Y})`:

```text
X = Y  iff  S_X = S_Y
              and, for every i, EqualUnder(f_i^X, f_i^Y, S_X)

X <= Y iff  S_X subseteq S_Y
              and, for every i, LessOrEqUnder(f_i^X, f_i^Y, S_X)
```

The Guard comparisons are canonical BDD equality/inclusion.  A plane is
unobservable outside the support on which it is checked; support-only
`Restrict` consequently never licenses comparison by immutable-root identity.
The same order is used for a no-op, cache validation, local and whole
post-fixpoint checks, and evidence publication.  In particular, synchronizing
two supports by intersecting them cannot establish equality or order, because
it would hide a leaf present in only one carrier.

Whole-carrier equality and order do not use `Product`.  After the stated
support equality or inclusion check, they are the conjunction of each Factor's
typed `EqualUnder` or `LessOrEqUnder` check over the left support. Each check
traverses only the two corresponding immutable planes and may stop at an
unequal terminal; it never constructs cross-Factor rows, representatives,
restricted roots, or a Cartesian tuple. Direct equal-root and empty-support
cases are exact fast paths.

`Restrict(X,R)` first computes the one outer support `support(X) ∩ R`. Because
a plane has no private support and every typed operation is parameterized by
the outer support, its initial implementation reuses the immutable plane
roots and changes only that support. This support-only row view is admitted
only after executable carrier laws prove that reads, writes, join, widening,
narrowing, `Mu`, delta, and publication never observe a plane outside its
outer support; failure of that law uses the exact masking implementation and
is a release blocker for the scaling target, not permission for approximation.

At ordinary control confluence, whole-carrier `Join` computes its Boolean
left-only, right-only, and overlap regions once:

```text
L = S_X minus S_Y    R = S_Y minus S_X    O = S_X intersection S_Y
S_J = L union R union O

f_i^J(v) = f_i^X(v)                 for v in L
           f_i^Y(v)                 for v in R
           Join_i(f_i^X(v),f_i^Y(v)) for v in O
```

The carrier passes `(L,R,O)` opaquely to one typed `Merge3Under` operation for
each carried plane; the plane may not recompute, widen, or reinterpret those
regions.  This is required even when `O` is empty: equality on an empty overlap
is vacuous and cannot justify retaining either root over the other side's
support.  A pre-existing root may be reused only after its Factor proves it
equals this complete three-region result over every region in `S_J`, not merely
over `O`. Rule read/write membership never authorizes dropping an otherwise
differing carried plane, because a later successor may observe the incoming
difference.

One whole-carrier `Widen`, `Narrow`, or `Mu` update uses the same exact
three-region contract: it preserves the old root on the old-only region, the
candidate root on the candidate-only region, and invokes the Factor's declared
binary recurrence operator only on the common region. The operation's declared
support rule chooses the resulting support, but it is always explicit: ascent
uses the exact union of current and candidate support, while a narrowing or
revocation may remove a leaf only through a declared contradiction/prune Rule
and only if the resulting carrier still satisfies its post-fixpoint.  The
precomputed regions or recurrence cofactors are passed opaquely to every
participating typed plane; a plane may neither recompute nor reinterpret them.
Compiled-operation membership limits only recurrence Factor operations: planes
outside it are never widened or narrowed merely because they were declared,
but are still recomputed exactly from the complete right-hand side. Membership
closure must prove that such a plane cannot sustain an infinite recursive
change; it is never carried forward stale. Equal immutable roots are reused
for Narrow, and for Widen under the mandatory `a ▽ a = a` law, only with the
complete-region proof above. `Mu` reuses a plane proved not to depend on the
discharged atom. Empty total support and genuinely one-sided total inputs
preserve their existing plane roots. These are structural sharing laws for the
one carrier, not a second sparse semantic route.

Localized head publication is one atomic three-State carrier operation, not a
chain of binary merges. Given current head `X`, selected recurrence proposal
`P`, exact full right-hand side `R`, and sealed `M_K`, it publishes once:

```text
selected target key in M_K:  Widen(X, P)       // or Narrow(X,R)
every other key/Factor:      exact R
outer support:               exact support(R)
```

For ascent `P = X ⊔ B_h^K` and `R = E_h^K ⊔ B_h^K`; external ingress is
therefore outside the widening proposal. The three roots reach each typed
Factor operation so that an unselected key inside a selected Factor also takes
`R`, not `P`. The operation creates one final `ChangeSet` from `X` to the single
committed result and exposes no intermediate State. A factor-free cyclic
support component uses the same operation with empty recurrence membership and
publishes exact `R`. Narrow is applied only to coordinates in the sealed
`M_K` footprint that declare both Narrow and its rank before Work opens; every
other coordinate, including an `M_K` coordinate without Narrow authority,
installs exact `R`. A one-right merge cannot satisfy this law.

On an exact Program source-recurrence relation, or a Link boundary that
references that exact Program relation, `σ`, Guard applies that
boundary's declared source-to-target reindexing
`ω_G^σ : Guard_src(σ) -> Guard_tgt(σ)`: completed visit-local decisions are
existentially discharged and predicates proven live across `σ` are renamed to
its canonical target parameters. The order and concretization are interpreted
at their respective interfaces, and its soundness obligation is
`γ_G,tgt(ω_G^σ(R)) ⊇ post_σ(γ_G,src(R))`. Thus a later visit is not the same
historical Boolean event, and no untyped/raw law such as `R ⊑ ω_G^σ(R)` is
stated or needed. For composable boundaries `σ : I -> J` and `τ : J -> K`,
Link requires `N_K(ω_G^τ(ω_G^σ(R))) =
ω_G^(τ∘σ)(N_I(R))`. Each interface normalizer is an exact canonical
representation law: `N_I² = N_I` and
`γ_G,I(N_I(R)) = γ_G,I(R)`. It validates a finite canonical source/target
interface orbit per recurrence SCC. For every normalized recurrence circuit
`C`, there are finite `k_C ≥ 0` and `p_C ≥ 1` such that
`(N ∘ ω_G^C)^(k_C+p_C) = (N ∘ ω_G^C)^k_C`; fixedness is only the special case
`p_C = 1`. That sealed eventual orbit is a relation over the presealed Guard
universe, not a fresh atom, renaming map, coordinate, or cache identity.
Convergence comes from ordinary joins in the finite Guard-support lattice
together with each participating Factor's declared widening/rank, never from
an orbit cap or a forced transport fixed point. A pure Rule/reduction edge has
the typed identity
reindexing. At a compiled-equation `Mu`, scheduling is over Points only. Shared
outer support is the mandatory exact coordinate of every cyclic Point head; it
stabilizes by finite BDD order under structural transfer, exact boundary
reindexing, union, and declared monotone prune. `M_K` is derived from the
internal Group write/carry closure defined above. Read-only or disconnected
Factors neither enter it merely because they were declared nor block Solver
seal. A nonmember is nevertheless recomputed from the full RHS and is excluded
only after the closure proves it cannot sustain recursive change. Product
remains a transient exact synchronizer for declared Rule reads and is never a
widened semantic tuple. Widening is not restricted to boundary targets. Away
from exact boundary transport, Guard reduction and exact
death projection preserve the registered observable denotation. No operation
widens because there are many leaves. A widened coordinate retains
precision-loss metadata; exact diagnostics and backend certificates may not
silently use a proof invalidated by it.

A reusable body result is the solution of its formal entry/outcome coordinates
in this same carrier,
not a program-scoped fact and not a private second fact plane. Conditional
existence is represented in the joint product and in the owning Factor's
support lattice.

No feasible leaf is the bottom/unreachable state. A Rule computes a successor
fiber from an input fiber: an exact assignment may strongly replace a key,
whereas an uncertain alias performs the domain's weak update. Confluence joins
successor fibers. Revocation writes the owning Factor's sound default/unknown
through a typed key operation and invalidates causal dependents; it is not an
anti-monotone deletion.

A domain Rule that derives a contradiction may return no current successor
leaf through its declared typed write result. Arbitrary Factor bottom does not
imply global unreachability: the owning domain must declare the contradiction
Rule and its reads. That Rule prunes its typed successor fiber/support; it does
not create, write, or revoke a reachability datum. Incoming Program and
boundary outcome structure contributes only the surviving guarded successor
support. Semantic Rules execute only on nonempty Guard support and do not read
a reachability conclusion as a semantic premise. The prune is a derived,
dependency-tracked transfer result; if an input widens or a must-proof is
revoked, invalidation recomputes the predecessor and can restore the leaf. Its
exact removed or restored region is a support delta, so every affected outgoing
Rule—including a no-read or Carry-only Rule whose sole input is support—is
re-evaluated or has its old guarded contribution retracted. Pruned leaves do
not confluence, publish diagnostics, or produce evidence/certificates.

Every Rule denotes a monotone abstract transformer. Its versioned domain
checker or reviewed theorem establishes local soundness and monotonicity;
domain law tests exercise that boundary and detect regressions but do not prove
an unbounded universal quantifier. A least abstract fixed point is claimed only
for a complete/finite-height composition or when its declared ascending
iteration is proved to stabilize; Knaster–Tarski applies unconditionally only
to the concrete collecting relation above. With widening, the Solver is not
claimed to compute a least abstract point: it computes a deterministic sound
post-fixpoint that contains the concrete least semantics. A domain may declare
a narrowing only with a well-founded termination measure; the Solver applies
it to stability after the widened state is stable, invalidates/rechecks all
dependents it changes, and verifies the same post-fixpoint obligation. Without
that proof the domain does not narrow. No transfer, Query, cache, cardinality
limit, or iteration cutoff may invent semantic truth.

Before evaluation, every cyclic compiled-equation SCC receives a deterministic
point-only WTO whose outer and nested cyclic components each receive a private
local Point head. This includes source recurrence, dynamic application cycles,
Rule self-dependencies, and mutual cross-domain reductions. The component does
not need a Program/Link recurrence edge. It follows the localized external/
internal producer partition, complete-interface reset, exact nonhead
recomputation, general widening-rank, and narrowing protocol in the canonical
denotation above.
Program recurrence annotations remain on their exact source transfers and alone
own typed source-to-target Guard reindexing. A Link boundary may reference that
exact Program relation while owning only its boundary substitution and evidence
transport; it computes no recurrence annotation. The compiled heads are the private operational
implementation of the one mathematical `Mu`: they own deterministic SCC/WTO
scheduling, restart, per-plane invocation order, local convergence, and
recursive evidence identity, not a heterogeneous semantic tuple, a second
semantic authority, or a boundary interface. Product remains a transient exact
synchronizer for declared Rule reads.

Admitting one eligible relation tuple atomically installs one validated
structural delta, advances the structural revision, and rebuilds the disposable equation
epoch from canonical `Init` and the complete accepted relation set. Each
transfer inside the resulting components independently applies either identity transport or its exact
Program/Link Guard reset, alpha-renaming, freshness/restoration, and value
correspondence. Multiple boundary interfaces remain distinct and are never
derived from or unioned by the SCC. There is no implicit Go-recursive evaluator
and no cycle hidden behind repeated Rule scheduling.

Cross-domain precision is an iterated reduced product. A domain owns its
Factor and primitive Rules. A reduction is also a monotone Rule, lives in a
domain child package that names the judgment it owns, reads its exact Factor
dependencies, and writes the one Factor that owns the resulting invariant.
Reductions may read more than one Factor when the invariant is genuinely
relational. They stabilize in the ordinary equation system after join,
Guard normalization, widening, narrowing, and revocation. The composition root only
declares Factors and Rules. It contains no semantic closure, authority ladder,
admission registry, priority, or one-shot reduction order.

### Canonical denotation

- Program denotes an exact transition/outcome relation over concrete machine
  states and Program locations, parameterized by the concrete interpretation
  of its sealed external-operation handles. A body denotes the least solution
  of that relation, parameterized by its formal boundary. An opaque external
  operation means nondeterminism in that concrete interpretation, not a
  missing Program edge.
- Factor `i` has abstraction/concretization `α_i, γ_i`; its order is semantic
  inclusion (`a ⊑ b` implies `γ_i(a) ⊆ γ_i(b)`). Its sparse default denotes the
  value of every unstored key.
- For support `S` and opaque Factor functions `a_i`, guarded concretization is
  `⋃_{ν∈S} ⋂_i γ_i(a_i(ν))`. This is denotational notation:
  valuations and their regions are represented by one reduced BDD and sparse
  per-Factor MTBDDs, not a stored DNF or universal semantic relation.
- BDD reduction and exact declared-read-tuple partition preserve that
  denotation.
  No cardinality-triggered Guard abstraction exists.
- For a registered observable projection `P`, structural-death projection
  uses ordinary domain Rules to abstract the concrete lifetime boundary.
  Exactness is claimed only for a Rule with a complete domain projection;
  otherwise its normal sound overapproximation is marked or the subject
  remains live. Guard then existentially removes only a decision absent from
  all downstream dependencies whose joint terminals are equal.
- For each exact Program source-recurrence relation, including a Link boundary
  transport that references it, `σ`, Guard transport is a
  typed source-to-target reindexing
  `ω_G^σ : Guard_src(σ) -> Guard_tgt(σ)`. It existentially eliminates decisions
  re-evaluated on the next visit and substitutes the finite live target
  interface. It is monotone at those respective interface orders and sound when
  `γ_G,tgt(ω_G^σ(R)) ⊇ post_σ(γ_G,src(R))`; it is not raw extensive, because
  its input and output need not share an atom namespace. For composable
  `σ : I -> J, τ : J -> K`,
  `N_K(ω_G^τ(ω_G^σ(R))) = ω_G^(τ∘σ)(N_I(R))`; every interface normalizer is
  exact (`N_I² = N_I` and `γ_G,I(N_I(R)) = γ_G,I(R)`). Each normalized recurrence circuit has
  a finite canonical eventual orbit: for finite `k ≥ 0` and `p ≥ 1`,
  `(N ∘ ω_G^C)^(k+p) = (N ∘ ω_G^C)^k`, with a fixed point only when `p = 1`.
  The orbit is over presealed Guard support and allocates no atom, renaming-map,
  coordinate, or cache identity. Ordinary finite-support Guard-lattice joins,
  together with participating Factor widening/ranks, provide convergence;
  neither an orbit cap nor forced reindexing fixedness does.
  A compiled-equation `Mu` never invents this interface, and a pure
  Rule/reduction cycle uses typed identity Guard transport. This is the only
  Guard abstraction beyond exact Boolean reduction and exact structural-death
  projection.
- For each Rule `r`, local soundness requires the concrete successors of
  `γ_G(D)` at its Program operation to be included in `γ_G(F_r(D))`. `F_r` is
  deterministic, monotone, and exact-read-tuple-wise. A contradiction Rule
  proves that its concrete successor set is empty before returning no support.
- For one fixed active structural epoch, let `P` be the finite Point set and
  `G` the finite set of atomic Groups. A Group `g` has an output `out(g)`, an
  ordered, multiplicity-preserving input vector `q[g,1..k]`, exact input
  reindexes `rho[g,j]`, and a deterministic sound transformer `F_g` monotone
  in every input. Genuine immutable external inputs are `u`; mutable external
  state may not be hidden in `Init`. The sole abstract equation is:

  ```text
  C_g(X,u) = AtomicFinish_g(
      F_g(rho[g,1](X_q1), ..., rho[g,k](X_qk); u))
  R_p(X,u) = Init_p(u) ⊔ ⋁ { C_g(X,u) | out(g) = p }
  X_p = R_p(X,u)
  ```

  Ordered inputs of one Group are simultaneous relational operands. Alternative
  control predecessors, outcomes, and call candidates are separate Groups that
  confluence only at their common Point. Every Group member reads one immutable
  ordered input snapshot. `AtomicFinish` either returns the complete declared
  support result and distinct-Factor patch/carry set or no candidate; a missing,
  extra, stale, wrong-predecessor, or sibling-dependent patch publishes nothing.
  A candidate contains bottom for every undeclared plane: no predecessor plane
  is inherited implicitly. A zero-input Group is an explicit immutable ingress.
- The Point graph contains `q[g,j] -> out(g)` for every input occurrence;
  duplicate graph edges may be collapsed only after input positions remain in
  `F_g`. Points alone are equation variables, schedule nodes, WTO nodes,
  publications, and Query roots. Groups are private RHS hyperedges and caches.
  Acyclic components evaluate their complete RHS once.
- For a point WTO component `K` with head `h`, partition only the head's
  producers:

  ```text
  E_h^K = Init_h ⊔ ⋁ { C_g | out(g)=h and inputs(g) intersect K = empty }
  B_h^K =          ⋁ { C_g | out(g)=h and inputs(g) intersect K != empty }
  ```

  A mixed Group is in `B`; each of its outside inputs belongs to the complete
  external interface of `K`. After canonical reset establish `E_h^K ⊑ X_h`,
  then iterate
  `X_h' = E_h^K ⊔ W_K,h(X_h, X_h ⊔ B_h^K(X))`. Every nonhead replaces its
  complete exact RHS `R_p`; it never retains a stale producer contribution or
  evaluates only a backedge fragment. The head applies widening only to `M_K`
  and exact support; every other plane is recomputed exactly. This is localized
  widening: external ingress is not degraded merely because an internal
  recurrence exists. Operationally the head computes exact `R=E⊔B` and
  selected proposal `P=X⊔B`, then performs the one atomic selected/exact
  carrier publication above. Before ascent it requires `E⊑X`,
  `support(X)⊆support(P)`, and equal `P`/`R` support in the fixed interface
  epoch. Failure is a reset condition, never permission to widen through stale
  support or to retain `X` outside `M_K`.
- The complete component interface consists of every outside Point input to a
  Group whose output lies in `K`, `u`, `Init`, active topology, and all relevant
  reindex/boundary identities. Any interface, activation, or revocation change
  discards the entire component and nested-component candidates, read logs,
  local values, and evidence, then restarts from canonical local bottom and
  current `Init`. The executor stores nonwrapping Point versions and an exact
  snapshot of every external interface occurrence plus structural, reindex,
  activation, and external-input revisions. It traverses the WTO with explicit
  frames. Any interface-token change, including an `E` producer/input revision,
  immediately discards the old frame and all descendants; only freshly reset
  children under the new snapshot may reach stable exit before the parent
  advances. The snapshot is checked before every head publication, before a
  child exit is accepted by its parent, and before final global verification.
  A frame exits only with no local
  ready Point, current producer/input/read versions, and a local full-RHS
  post-fixpoint. With a fixed interface, nested termination is by induction on
  this finite WTO: every head has finitely many support growths and strict
  Factor-rank descents, and every child traversal terminates before its parent
  continues. No Go recursion, fuel, empty-queue assumption, or iteration cap
  represents this nesting.
- Narrowing begins only after `R(X) ⊑ X` is verified for the entire active
  equation. A narrowed head uses the full RHS and satisfies
  `R_h(X) ⊑ Narrow(X_h,R_h(X)) ⊑ X_h`; nonheads use exact full-RHS replacement.
  Every decrease wakes all dependent Groups, including support-only and
  Carry-only transfers. Narrowing has its own well-founded complete-phase rank,
  and the final result must pass the whole-equation post-fixpoint check. An
  empty queue is insufficient: every demanded producer snapshot must still
  match its final typed inputs/reads and every exact refolded `R_p` must satisfy
  `R_p ⊑ X_p`.
  Activation, revocation, or interface change aborts descent and rebuilds
  ascent from `Init`. Narrowing improves a sound post-fixpoint; it does not
  claim the least abstract fixed point.
- Every `SolveComplete`, State/Query materialization, ordinary evidence result,
  and certificate requires one fresh whole-demanded-equation refold and cache/
  interface-version check, whether or not any Factor supports narrowing. Queue
  quiescence alone never qualifies.
- A Program boundary correspondence `σ` has domain-owned abstract entry/outcome
  Rules whose concretization contains the concrete call/module transition.
  Identity, composition, alpha-renaming, activation freshness/restoration, and
  Guard substitution commute with `σ` up to sound abstraction. Outcome
  instantiation is indexed by that same `σ`, so the summarized equation denotes
  only interprocedurally valid call/return and suspend/resume paths.
- The sole Program/Link-to-equation compiler issues each private Point/Rule
  anchor as an opaque capability after validating its exact structural
  location, outcome, source/target Guard scopes, and boundary correspondence.
  Equation identity retains that capability and each reindex identity; it may
  not accept an arbitrary hash as equivalent authority. The engine interprets
  none of the Program or domain payload. Guard reindex is only the Boolean
  coordinate transport. Non-injective actual/formal Pack substitution,
  receiver/capture/type/effect binding, fresh allocation/result/generation,
  `Recent`/`Summary`, restoration, and provider state remain typed owner Rules
  over the same sealed Program/Link correspondence. No generic mega-boundary
  record or second substitution language is introduced.
- The concrete body meaning is
  `Φ_b(p) = outcomes(lfp X. E_b(p, X))`. The compiled abstract body artifact
  is a locally sound `E#_b`; for abstract boundary `p#`, its verified solution
  overapproximates `Φ_b(γ(p#))`. It is symbolic because `p#` remains formal;
  specialization supplies an abstract boundary projection without rebuilding
  `E#_b`.
- A cache hit is valid only when every dependency token read by the prior
  deterministic equation has the same semantic value/version. It therefore
  returns the same abstract result. Cache eviction changes work, not `Φ_b`.
- For registered Query roots `Q`, the dynamic backward dependency closure must
  satisfy `π_Q(Solve_demanded) = π_Q(Solve_full)`. Both modes use the same
  fixed relation-family constructors and, after domain-driven tuple admission reaches
  closure, the same rooted active edges, Program recurrence relations and exact
  Link boundary transports,
  widening operators, Guard normalization, and deterministic per-component order.
  If late activation changes topology, both modes discard all Point values,
  Group candidates/read logs, widening history, narrowing state, and evidence
  from the aborted structural revision, then solve the same rebuilt point WTO
  from the same current `Init`. Pure transfer memoization may cross that cut
  only when its complete immutable inputs and structural revision match.
  Each root begins at its exact `(Factor, dependency unit, structural root)`;
  backward traversal follows only Rules whose declared exact write/carry or
  staged write-target surface can cover that unit, their declared reads and
  write-target predecessors, support-prune dependencies needed to retain the
  rooted successor, and ordinary structural predecessors. Before
  that comparison, traversal also follows every declared
  lazy relation family's reverse activation declaration reachable through its
  indexed application/endpoint range to the trigger Rule coordinates, trigger
  read-schema DAG, and their ordinary predecessor/control/boundary
  dependencies, even while all pairs in that family are inactive. This
  schedules the finite typed work needed to decide activation without
  enumerating/materializing a relation pair or treating its transfer as
  reachable. Consequently an inactive edge cannot be omitted merely because
  its activation predicate has not yet been evaluated.
  Omitting dependency-disconnected components cannot perturb the rooted
  solution. Undemanded is not an
  abstract value, and demand/cache/evidence have no concrete denotation of
  their own.

Any implementation mechanism that cannot be placed in this denotation is
outside the design.

## Canonical Program

`program.Program` is the sole immutable semantic IR.
`analysis/lua/bind` is the transient lexical-resolution authority for this
wave. Program is built directly from the AST and binder result. Lowering copies
resolved lexical identity into the sealed Program; no downstream consumer
receives binder state or AST pointers.

Runtime TypeValue source acquisition follows that same one-way authority. The
binder alone marks an exact call-base occurrence that has the frozen runtime
type meaning. Runtime-loadable primitive authorities are considered first,
then direct declarations of a type or interface in the bound chunk; a nested
declaration never supplies this runtime authority. Any active local, parameter,
upvalue, or pending local-function binding of the spelling disables the special
meaning. Lowering copies a successful mark and its exact target into the
existing Program TypeValue row for that call base, with no mutable global Cell
read. An unmarked, aliased, or dynamic base instead uses the ordinary mutable
Value lookup and Call relations. Neither Link, a domain, artifact decode, nor a
future consumer may repeat the name lookup or infer a TypeValue from spelling.

Program records exact causes, never abstract conclusions:

- authored bodies, bindings, parameters, captures, globals, imports, exports,
  labels, source occurrences, and spans;
- exact evaluation order and all normal and non-local outcomes;
- values, mutable cells, allocations, reads, delayed write groups, and source
  order; every `CellGlobal`'s one `GlobalBinding` to the exact Target
  `GlobalEnvRoot` String slot;
- allocation kind and structural origin; source record/field schema; symbolic
  size/capacity Values; initialization/publish order; every actor
  send/receive; and each explicit ownership, retention, suspension, or runtime
  allocation boundary supplied by the language/provider ABI;
- scalar versus multiple/open results and Lua list adjustment in assignment,
  calls, returns, table construction, varargs, and generic `for`;
- exact fields, literal keys, dynamic key values, and deep structural lenses;
- branches, short-circuit selection, loops, labels/gotos, suspension and
  resumption sites;
- closure origin, capture installation, direct callee evidence, method receiver
  insertion, arguments, results, and unknown/imported boundaries;
- structural activation lifecycle: which operation creates an invocation,
  closure environment, coroutine, continuation, or module initialization and
  which resume/re-entry operation restores an existing activation;
- static declarations, type parameters, recursive declarations, constraints,
  annotations, assertions, `typeof`, `keyof`, indexed and conditional types,
  each source-only `TypePublication` with its exact assignment pair and
  dotted export path, plus each binder-authorized runtime TypeValue call-base
  seed and its exact primitive-or-direct-chunk-declaration target;
- authored/static effect schemas where the parser, provider signature, or
  module contract actually supplies them; concrete effectful operations and
  handler/resumption structure implied by supported Lua constructs;
- module/provider identity, initialization order, cache result Packs, export
  surfaces, import edges and cycle-rejection anchors, and typed module
  boundaries;
- explicit `Mu` for exact source-control and direct-call recurrence.

An ordinary branch, loop edge, or goto is not renamed “continuation.”
Continuation identity exists only where the language/runtime can suspend,
resume, throw, yield, or otherwise re-enter at a non-local outcome. This keeps
the vocabulary semantic instead of generic.

Program does not contain:

- inferred types, abstract values, inferred value/heap aliases, inferred effect
  rows, abstract heap slots, escape, placement, ownership, typestate, diagnostics, or
  JIT decisions; source type aliases and exact binding/call relations remain
  Program facts;
- solver contexts, partitions, widening, caches, worklists, registers, bytecode
  PCs, ABI layout, deoptimization state, or runtime shape offsets;
- opaque `any` content, string encodings of paths/facts/effects, rule-kind
  codes, operand-role registries, or generic topology records.

### One IR, closed typed relations

“One Program” means one sealed semantic authority and identity, not one
universal instruction stream. Its physical representation is a product of
private dense typed table families sharing the same Program handles:

```text
Program =
    identity/order relations
  × local-operation relations
  × control relations
  × boundary/schema relations
```

These are storage ownership boundaries, not public lifecycle objects and not
independently buildable or serializable Programs. Cross-family edges are typed
foreign keys to the one owning row. A semantic relation is stored exactly once.
Nothing is copied, translated, wrapped, or re-interned between families.

The Program implementation may not define a universal `Node`, `Operation`,
`Kind + operands` row, optional-slot instruction record, `Operations()` stream,
generic visitor, runtime family/handler registry, reflection/`any` payload, or
central language-semantics switch. Family-local closed enums such as arithmetic
operators are permitted; they may not become a global operation-code taxonomy.

The sole unfinished builder is the final Program storage before seal, not an
intermediate model. Each lowering child receives a narrow concrete typed writer
for its owned relations. It cannot mutate another family, mint or wrap shared
identity, publish a fragment IR, install a callback, or add an alternate
builder. Seal validates referential integrity, freezes direct ranges/indexes,
links shards, and annotates exact recurrence; it never translates the typed
tables into a central executable graph.

Program exposes immutable typed dense ranges and direct lookups. Domain Rule
constructors consume only the ranges they need; they never scan a generic
operation stream, dispatch through a registry, or reconstruct a private
Program view. The root package owns identity, storage, sealing, and generated
contracts only; language judgments remain in lowering children and domain
judgments remain in domains.

One canonical Program schema is the source of typed rows, concrete writers,
frozen views, validators, analysis completeness obligations, and persistence
sections. Generated code is static and typed; it creates no runtime metadata
plane. Adding a language operation or boundary event changes the closed schema
and fails build/seal until lowering, codec coverage, and every
analyzer semantic family have an explicit disposition. There is no default or
fallback disposition.

### Small owners and declarative components

The one semantic authority is not implemented as one god object. `Program`
and `Link` are small owner fences over immutable vertical components generated
from the canonical typed relation schema. A non-generated semantic owner,
builder, scratch aggregate, row, or view with more than sixteen direct fields
is a design failure that must be decomposed before acceptance. A public
semantic owner or view with more than sixteen methods is likewise a design
failure. Generated output does not waive the invariant: its schema must be
decomposed so the generated representation also satisfies it.

The Program owner contains only root identity and these existing semantic
children:

```text
Program
  Source       authored identity, spans, literals, bindings
  Flow         Program-local evaluation, control, Values, ports, transfers, Mu
  Static       declarations, TypeRefs, annotations, publications
  Module       imports, exports, entry and boundary rows
```

`Flow` is the sole Program-local executable geometry. It owns only Program
terms and Program-scoped body, scalar, Pack, storage, operator, capture,
control, and Mu coordinates; it imports neither Link nor Target. Link may add a
shard owner and an exact boundary correspondence that references a Flow
coordinate, but may not re-lower it into Link-local roots, scalars, packs,
transfers, operator arms, or another endpoint identity. Application, callback,
resume, spawn, module-entry, and Target-value loci remain typed Link Boundary
coordinates because they cross Program/Target/project ownership; they never
enter Program Flow and are never merged with it under a universal root/port
handle.

Target remains the unchanged frozen ABI/dialect authority. Link contains only
project-specific composition:

```text
Link
  Project      shard mounts, base Applications, direct boundary anchors
  Boundary     exact Program x Target correspondence
  Module       resolution, actor-local cache/root transport
  Static       project namespace and qualified resolution
  Host         sealed provider and boot ingress
```

These component names denote storage/ownership boundaries over existing
relations, not independently sealable Programs, public lifecycle objects, or
a new semantic vocabulary. The root owner is assembled only after every child
builder succeeds. Each builder receives explicit immutable inputs and returns
one immutable component; build order is a declared acyclic dependency graph,
never an implicit sequence of mutations on a partially observable root.

The physical Go package graph is binding and generated into the Wave-B
ownership ledger. It is vertical rather than a same-package file split:

```text
program/keyspace           -> no Program/Link/Target/engine/domain package
program/internal/canonical -> no semantic package; framed codec primitive only
program/source    -> program/flow + program/keyspace + internal/canonical
program/flow      -> program/keyspace + internal/canonical
program/static    -> program/source + program/flow + program/keyspace + internal/canonical
program/module    -> program/flow + program/keyspace + internal/canonical
program           -> keyspace + source + flow + static + module
program/artifact  -> program and its children; never imported by them
program/lower     -> compiler AST/binder + program builders; never imported by them
program/lower/{lexical,eval,store,control,call,static,module}
                  -> compiler AST/binder + program/keyspace + only the typed
                     Program child builders named by that lowering owner

program/link/project  -> Program + Target + program/keyspace
program/link/boundary -> Project + Target + program/keyspace
program/link/module   -> Project + Boundary + program/keyspace
program/link/host     -> Project + Boundary + Module +
                         program/keyspace + Target declarations
program/link/static   -> Project + Program + program/keyspace
program/link          -> Program/Target + all Link children
program/link/artifact -> Link root and children; never imported by them

analysis/domain/<owner>
                  -> engine declaration/carrier leaves + only the Program/Link/
                     Target keyspace and mathematical libraries named by its schema
analysis/domain/<owner>/<judgment-rule>
                  -> its owner + exact read-owner roots + engine Rule declaration
                     leaves + exact Program/Link/Target component views it reads
analysis/domain/<owner>/<query-or-evidence>
                  -> its named owner roots + completed-State Query/evidence leaves
analysis/composition
                  -> engine assembly + declared domain owners and Rule/Query roots
```

The braces denote separate sibling packages, not horizontal files. The arrows
denote permitted imports. `program/lower` imports its lowering children and the
root Program assembler; no lowering child imports another lowering child or
the parent. A component child imports only the explicit acyclic authorities in
the DAG above; it never imports its root, lowerer/artifact, engine, or a domain.
Cross-component rows
contain only canonical typed handles from the relevant keyspace leaves; the
root assembler validates foreign keys against immutable child views. No child
stores `uint32` in place of a typed foreign handle. `program/keyspace` owns the
existing `Term` and other cross-component Program handle identities;
`program/link/project` owns the owner-fenced `Shard`, `Key`, and `Application`
handles issued by its `Mounts`, `Keys`, and `Applications` views.
Every remaining Link handle belongs to its actual issuing component and is
owner-fenced there; no Link-local keyspace or bare ordinal dialect exists.
Private compact slots are permitted only behind a retained issuing view and
its exact `At(index)`/`Index(handle)` boundary; aliases and re-exports are
forbidden. A component child imports neither its own root nor an undeclared
sibling; the explicit upstream child and foreign immutable-authority edges in
the DAG above are the only exceptions. Existing root-defined types move to
their one owning child and all callers change imports atomically; root
re-export aliases are forbidden.

The Program root exposes at most the direct Source/Flow/Static/Module views and
identity. Link exposes at most Project/Static/Host/Module/Boundary views and
identity. A component with more than sixteen methods decomposes into named
vertical subviews in the same owning child package (for example Flow control,
value, Pack, storage, function, and operator views); it does not create another
package layer or a root forwarding surface. Root ContentID is assembled from
semantic child digests. Cold Identity/rebind storage is a post-root derivative
owned by the artifact package, not a semantic child that would create a digest
or import cycle.

A non-test generated import-DAG command reads `go list -json`, the schema owner
ledger, and source imports; it covers Program, Link, lowerer, every domain
owner/Rule/Query/evidence child, and the sole composition root. It rejects
every undeclared edge, root/sibling backedge, domain import into Program/Link,
owner import of its Rule/Query children, Rule-to-Rule or Query-to-Rule edge,
root alias/re-export, and same-package horizontal implementation of a declared
child. This structural command is a build/review gate, never a Go unit test of
file composition.

Construction has one private non-semantic allocation plan derived directly
from the canonical schema and source order. It counts rows and mints the final
Program Term/component ordinals once, then component builders fill their final
dense storage and validate typed foreign keys against already allocated final
identities. The allocation plan is discarded before the root becomes
observable; it contains no operation, Rule, fallback, callback, generic node,
or fragment IR. Each foreign key is validated by its destination owner. Root
ContentID is computed only after all components and cross-component
referential-integrity laws pass.

Every exported construction Draft is one linear capability even though Go
values are copyable. All value copies share one private lifecycle state: the
first finalization attempt, successful or failed, consumes every copy and
removes every construction-only view. A per-value `consumed` flag beside a
shared component pointer is forbidden because copying the Draft creates a
second finalize/read path. Each owner law copies its Draft before finalization
and proves both copies are fenced afterward.

A narrow proxy/capability view is permitted only when it directly exposes one
child component without allocation or identity translation. It contains the
same owner fence and direct typed tables, mints no handle, retains no semantic
state, and has no compatibility forwarding methods. A facade over the old
hundreds of root methods is forbidden.

The canonical schema generates each component's typed rows, dense storage,
validation, direct range access, content contribution, artifact disposition,
and source-coverage declaration together. It is compile-time typed code, not
a runtime registry, generic tuple store, visitor, blackboard, ECS, or erased
`Kind + payload` relation. A relation is stored once. Measured direct indexes
are private derivatives of that relation. Artifact/rebind-only indexes are
post-root cold derivatives keyed by the minimal component digest they observe;
they are not semantic components and are never permanently retained in
solver-hot components.

Analysis interpretations are domain-owned components. Value provenance,
runtime-type classification, resource/holder classification, ownership
origins, allocation/retention judgments, and solver support selection are not
Program or Link components. Their owners declare typed flow laws over exact
Program/Link/Target coordinates. The generic engine consumes only Factor,
Rule, Guard, reduction, evidence, and Query declarations; it imports and
switches on none of the component or domain meanings. Adding a domain changes
no Program, Link, Target, or engine code unless the domain proves that a
genuinely absent authored structural relation is required.

Link never permanently materializes a logical dependent product merely to make
enumeration convenient. In particular, Call x Operation x Subedge and
Application x Operation x ABI-port remain exact factorized coordinates whose
membership is decided by sealed typed guards/signature classes. A cold complete
enumerator may traverse the mathematical product for coverage, but the hot Link
stores no product rows, empty roots, duplicate identities, or reverse maps for
unselected combinations. This is exact representation, not a cap, budget,
widening, or approximation.

The virtual boundary coordinate is the validated value tuple
`(base Application, Target Operation, Target-owned Port-or-Subedge)`. It is not
a Link row, handle, ordinal, ContentID, rebind target, root, artifact payload,
cache key, or persisted identity. Only its three canonical constituents have
identity. Membership is one generated pure predicate over the two canonical
constituent descriptors and, when present, the Target-owned port range. Each
descriptor has a fixed schema whose retained representation is independent of
the cardinality and identity of the opposite side; a variable-length Target
signature remains Target-owned input to the direct predicate, never a cross-side
class. Link may retain total descriptor storage
`O(Applications + Operations + TargetPorts)`. It may not intern one class per
opposite-side member or retain any class-pair relation, compatibility matrix,
bitset, map, cache, table, site, range, ID, or reverse map. The predicate may
compare fixed enums, arities, modes, and exact constituent IDs directly; it may
not dispatch through a precomputed cross-product. Hot membership and
constituent projection are bounded direct operations with zero allocation and
no scan of unrelated operations or ports. Artifact decode rederives membership.
The cold coverage enumerator emits tuple values transiently and retains `O(1)`
product storage after completion. Subedge-shaped Applications are forbidden.

Link has no `Candidate` relation or identity. Direct function-to-body
correspondence is an existing Program Flow relation; Link may shard-scope its
constituents but may neither copy that relation nor mint a Link body boundary.
Target-operation eligibility is only the virtual guarded boundary tuple above.
It has no Candidate row, handle, ordinal, ID, range, reverse map, cold
reference, artifact member, or complete-source enumerator. This prohibition
also covers a Candidate renamed as an Application-operation/site or embedded
inside an assertion/occurrence row. A selected operation, Guard, activation,
or relation choice is epoch-local domain/engine evidence and has no Link
identity.

Portable Program identity is `(Program ContentID, Term)`. A dense Link Shard
slot is process-local and never persisted as Program identity. Replay resolves
the Program ContentID, validates the Term in that exact canonical Program, and
then binds the current Link-local shard. Byte-identical canonical Programs
therefore rebind; merely semantically equivalent Programs with different
canonical bytes miss. Every retained Link cold reference declares the
narrowest canonical preimage that proves its meaning: the exact owning
component/relation digest plus its retained constituent identities. Full
`Link.ContentID` is used only when the reference semantically observes the
whole linked project. A deleted bridge or virtual tuple receives no replacement
ID. Rebind validates membership in the named component digest and never widens
a dependency-local reference into a whole-Link dependency.

Identity construction is an acyclic DAG. Each canonical Source/Flow/Static/
Module/Project/Boundary/Host component digest hashes only its versioned
semantic rows and already-owned constituent identities. Root Program/Link
ContentID hashes the schema/producer identity and the ordered canonical child
component digests. Derived indexes, cold replay maps, and cache references are
built only after the root digest exists; they are excluded from every semantic
component and root digest and may not mutate semantic storage. The root becomes
observable only after those derivatives validate against their named minimal
component digests. A hash cycle, post-ContentID semantic mutation, or derived
index contributing to semantic identity is a seal failure.

Link construction uses the exact child DAG below; an omitted sibling edge is
not permission to inspect that sibling during Seal:

```text
Project  -> Target
Boundary -> Project + Target
Module   -> Project + Boundary
Host     -> Project + Module + Boundary
Static   -> Project
Link     -> Target + Project + Boundary + Module + Host + Static
Cold     -> the finalized root and only the minimal child/relation digests it indexes
```

Project owns mounts, the one unified Program-plus-Target exact-key quotient,
every Application kind and typed subsequence, including the exact
Import-to-Call relation. Its Target dependency does not widen `ModuleKey` or
`ApplicationID`: those identities use their narrower mount/application
relation digests. Boundary owns Values, operation/
loader/bootstrap Seeds, call operands, and endpoint requests. Module owns
Actors, analysis roots, cache instances/entries, module coordinates, init
generations/outcomes/terminals, and binding-world identity. Host owns provider
capabilities, selectors, boot roots, metatable attachments, and global
bindings. Static remains Project-only. `ModuleReadySubject` is a Module-owned
projection over a Boundary-owned Value and validates both authorities; Boundary
never reads Module. A mixed dependency list is forbidden: provider, schema,
and binding-world dependencies remain Host-, Static-, and Module-owned rows,
respectively, and only a cold root projection may enumerate their union.

A hot public or cross-child handle is opaque `{issuing component instance,
ordinal}`. Equivalent reseals deliberately have different hot authorities;
ContentID is not a substitute for an instance fence. Compact ordinals remain
legal only in private storage that permanently retains and validates the exact
issuing child and never accepts or exposes the ordinal without that context.
The sole handle-to-storage boundary is the issuing view's canonical
`At(index)`/`Index(handle)` pair: `Index` checks the exact instance and bounds,
the consuming child retains that issuing component and stores only a private
`uint32` canonical index, and `At` reissues the owner-fenced handle. This is a
representation codec, not another relation or identity. It uses no callback,
linear scan, per-child handle map, stored ownerful row, exported slot type, or
bare-index semantic API.
In particular, public Shard, Key, Seed, Actor, AnalysisRoot,
ModuleCacheInstance, BootRoot, BootMetatableAttachment, and GlobalBinding
coordinates may not remain bare scalars across the child cut. Value,
Application, module-init/cache handles, capabilities, and Static handles move
their existing fences to their issuing children. Artifact/rebind references
instead contain `{minimal relation digest, canonical discriminator}` and are
resolved through post-root cold indexes; they never persist an instance token.

Each independently reusable relation/subview has its own canonical digest.
An ID or cold reference hashes exactly that relation digest and its canonical
discriminator, never the whole Boundary/Module/Host or root Link digest merely
because it is convenient. Delta laws prove that changing an unrelated endpoint
request does not change Value, cache, coordinate, or module-init identities.
Boundary derives `EndpointRequestID` from the finalized endpoint-request
relation digest plus the canonical Host-identity discriminator; the ID is not
an input to that digest and never depends on the Host digest. Host consumes the
owner-fenced hot request and retains only its validated Seed/selector mapping.

Every retained private index has a generated declaration naming its exact
query, production callers, source relation, retained cardinality, asymptotic
bound, allocation expectation, replay disposition, and benchmark/law. An index
without that declaration is deleted. A removed product has a negative scaling
law proving retained storage and unrelated-operation query work have zero
Application x Operation and Application x Operation x Port coefficients.

The sixteen-field/sixteen-method invariant is enforced by a schema/codegen
validation command, not a Go composition unit test. It applies per published
semantic surface/type, not to the aggregate child package. Method counts use
the effective `go/types` method set and include promoted/embedded, generated,
handwritten, aliased, interface, generic-wrapper, and method-expression
surfaces plus package-level semantic constructors/queries. Field accounting
follows authority/state pointers transitively so one pointer cannot hide a god
object. Builders, scratch aggregates, row types, cold/ref wrappers, and each
public view are checked separately; aggregate inventory is retained only to
detect undeclared parallel APIs. No `_test.go` asserts package or file
composition. A failed count prevents generation/build acceptance and identifies
the schema component that must be decomposed.

Every migration into this shape is a destructive relation-family flash cut.
The new owner, all consumers, persistence/identity laws, and semantic tests land
together; the former rows, handles, methods, indexes, tests, and generation
paths disappear in the same cut. No proxy, alias, bridge, translated handle,
dual-read period, or compatibility adapter may connect old and new forms.

`cmd/flashrefactor` is a non-semantic migration instrument, not a production
dependency. The frozen Wave-B ledger classifies every declaration, field,
method, and typed reference edit as `semantic`, `mechanical`, or `delete`.
Its stable fact IDs are cold, reproducible commitments to typed Go declarations,
uses, structural surfaces, and source positions. They exist only for ownership
audit, generation freshness, and exact migration accounting; they are never a
Program or Link identity, an artifact/rebind identity, an analyzer Point or
Group, a solver key, an SSA value, or a runtime coordinate.
Semantic rows require human/agent design; the tool must execute and account for
every mechanical row exactly. An unknown reference shape, method expression,
reflection/string dependency, import cycle, private dependency outside the
declared closure, source-hash mismatch, new diagnostic, or residue fails before
publication. Dry-run is mandatory; apply is transactional and emits the exact
mapping, reference counts, touched-file diff, diagnostic delta, and zero-residue
report. It never generates a forwarding method, alias, proxy, or temporary API.

The helper intentionally owns only syntax-preserving same-package mechanical
routes. Cross-package ownership, export surface, and import-DAG changes are
semantic ledger rows: they are reviewed and applied atomically with `gopls`
rename/reference evidence and the import-DAG gate, never guessed by the helper.
The helper is accepted for Wave B only after two levels of bounded evidence:

1. a multi-file adversarial package plus an external-package reference
   inventory covers field extraction,
   child ownership, receiver migration, internal/external selector routing,
   imports, baseline diagnostics, rollback, and unsupported reference
   rejection; the external reference proves fail-closed accounting and is not
   silently rewritten across an ownership boundary; and
2. in a disposable exact source copy, it completes the current densest coherent
   Link scalar/Pack/storage extraction (22 owner fields and 217 typed selector
   rewrites at the binding digest), then the focused Pack-transfer incidence/
   replay/foreign/artifact law and affected package compile pass through
   `scripts/bounded_test.sh`, with zero old-owner selector residue.

The generated migration ledger records tool-covered versus semantic edits. The
helper must cover all mechanically classified edits and those edits must be the
majority of the full Wave-B typed-reference volume; otherwise it has not earned
use in the production cut. Same-package component extraction is only an
unaccepted staging operation inside one destructive workspace cut. The
separately reviewed cross-package semantic rows perform the final package move,
caller import rewrite, and deletion before any Wave-B gate, so no intermediate
horizontal component, root API, or dual ownership is accepted.

### Program identity and key space

All shard-local Program/compiler identities are dense within their immutable
shard; project relation/shard slots are dense within one sealed Program link.
A Term is compact and deterministic for its shard. Reusing that exact shard
retains its Term namespace across project re-link; rebuilding an equivalent
shard preserves semantic `(shard, local)` identity but does not require the
