same packed bits or object identity. Persisted body/component-template identity
and byte reuse therefore require that exact owning immutable shard and retained
Term namespace; an equivalent rebuilt shard is a derived-section miss, never a
Term remapping or a second template identity. Cross-module relations use only
explicit exported/imported boundary Terms. Runtime `Value`
representation and equality are tag-specific and are not covered by this
handle law. Strings are accepted only at source parsing and external
serialization boundaries.

Program still retains authored names, string literals, source text identity,
and exact source spans as immutable payload atoms/ranges for Lua debug and
reflection behavior. They are not semantic keys and are never parsed in an
analysis/backend hot path.

Shard seal canonicalizes every semantically exact Lua table key to one typed
static key value with a dense shard-local atom handle:

- dot field `t.x`, bracket field `t["x"]`, `{x = v}`, and `{["x"] = v}` point
  to the same string-key atom while retaining their source spelling/syntax;
- numeric literals normalize by the runtime's table-key equality law, so an
  integer and its equal integral-float form do not create different slots;
- exact boolean/string/number keys use typed atom variants. Nil and NaN never
  become stored-key atoms, but key validity is operation-specific: an ordinary
  read can observe absence and enter `__index`; an ordinary write can enter
  `__newindex`; only a primitive/raw table commit that requires a storable key
  takes the target Lua contract's invalid-key error outcome. Program preserves
  these alternatives instead of attaching one unconditional error to the key;
- a dynamic key remains only a Program Value. Program never consumes an
  inferred proof. An equality/heap Rule may later refine its own dynamic-key
  partition to the Program's same canonical literal atom or to an exact
  identity class, without formatting or parsing. Lua key equality is one
  closed law: integer and equal integral float coincide, strings compare by
  content, and every other valid non-scalar key compares by object identity.
  Array versus hash placement is never a semantic key class.

An atom's semantic identity is its typed normalized value under the frozen
target key-equality law, not its shard-local or project-link handle. Reusable
shard equations store the local atom plus its canonical typed payload.
Cross-shard boundary Rules compare/substitute that semantic value; project
linking may intern equal atoms as a semantics-neutral internal optimization, but no equation, cache key, or
serialized artifact depends on the link-local representation. Project seal
validates cross-shard key equality and direct indexes; it does not mint a
second semantic atom plane.

A cell is one lexical/global/upvalue storage identity. A lens is one evaluated
member/index location:

```text
base Value
  + exact field atom
  | exact literal key
  | dynamic key Value
```

A read consumes a cell/lens and produces a Value. A write targets a cell/lens
after all required base/key/RHS evaluations have occurred. An Assignment has
one exact transaction: it evaluates every LHS address (base then dynamic key)
left-to-right in source LHS order, evaluates the RHS expression list once in
its ordinary left-to-right order, applies the one canonical Values/list
adjustment once, and maps each source LHS ordinal to that adjusted Pack slot
(including final-tail expansion, truncation, and nil-fill). Its ordered Write
range remains in source LHS order, but commit successors apply that range
right-to-left: adjusted Pack completion enters the last Write, each Write
enters the preceding Write, and the first enters the ordinary lexical tail.
Each Write therefore retains its source LHS ordinal, evaluated Cell/Lens
target, and the exact adjusted Pack-slot correspondence; a source-only
TypePublication independently retains its original authored LHS/RHS pair and
does not reinterpret dynamic assignment results. No Heap, Pack, metatable, or
static namespace consumer reconstructs transaction order or pairing from AST,
spans, or target shape. A deep access is the structural chain of those
read-produced Values and subsequent lenses; it is never flattened to a textual
path. The heap domain may derive exact-key slots, finite symbolic key
partitions, equality classes, array summaries, or an unknown-key summary. The
source Program retains the distinction so the domain never reparses or
reconstructs it.

There is one aggregate write operation, including writes to a currently shaped
record. The Program row distinguishes ordinary assignment from raw assignment
and declares the primitive and typed `__newindex` application/outcome
relations. It does not decide which dynamic alternative is feasible. Heap and
metatable Rules determine raw presence and feasible primitive/dispatch
alternatives under Guard; Program does not choose a physical check.
For Lua dispatch, a physically declared shaped field whose current value is
nil is absent: it cannot suppress `__index` or `__newindex`. Only a proved raw
or no-dispatch path may keep a compatible present string field in the shaped
representation or treat a missing-field nil primitive write as a no-op.
Otherwise the ordinary metatable application and its
call/effect/escape/outcome edges stay visible. A raw/no-dispatch missing
non-nil or incompatible declared-field value changes the same semantic object
to an ordinary dynamic table.

The semantic transition preserves source identity, aliases, and contents.
Program does not acquire a second shape-transition node or physical-shape
identity. A future executable consumer must preserve that Target relation;
shape indexes, offsets, headers, tags, forwarding, and table layout are outside
this analyzer wave.

One canonical Values relation represents Lua result lists: an ordered fixed
prefix plus an optional open tail. Every consumer names that relation and one
of the language's exact adjustments:

- scalar/parenthesized use takes the first result and supplies nil if empty;
- every non-final expression in a list is scalar-adjusted;
- a final open producer may expand;
- a fixed target count truncates or nil-fills;
- an open consumer preserves the final tail.

Program exposes the total sealed positional view of this existing relation:
for an index in the fixed prefix it returns that Value; beyond the prefix it
returns the same tail Value plus an offset when a tail exists, otherwise the
`nil-fill` result case. This is one derived Program view, not stored rows and
not a new Pack/Slot/Nil identity. Link consumes that view and cannot own a
second implementation of the positional law. Target Values variables are
scoped by their existing owning operation; `(Operation, ValuesVar)` is their
identity and no global Values-variable plane is minted.

Assignment, return, arguments, table array tail, vararg, and generic `for` all
use this relation. There is no second `ListShape` descriptor.

Field atoms and literal constants are canonicalized once at shard seal and
validated at project link.

Program shards and project Link have separate acyclic identities. Program
ContentID covers only canonical source/reflection/module requirement content,
the frozen language contract, and parser/binder/lowerer/sealer/schema producer
digests. Link ContentID separately covers the ordered Program IDs, Target and
binding identities, Link schema/producer, and canonical Link component digests.
No Program digest contains a Link digest or resolved project relation. Equal
retained Program shards reuse pre-link work directly; eviction/rebuild misses
rather than remaps. A shard seals
only wholly local source-control recurrence. Existing typed import,
module-initialization, resumption, and callback relations carry their own
entry/outcome correspondence and recurrence fields. Project seal resolves and
validates each exact boundary interface independently, including boundaries
participating in wholly same-shard and mixed cross-shard cycles; it never
unions a cycle-wide interface. Static equations reference those boundary
annotations and are rejected if they mismatch; no SCC head is minted. Only the
Solver adds dynamic edges or recompiles an operational epoch. The
project link is part of the one Program and does not
re-lower bodies, mint Terms, infer semantics, or create a second IR. Recurrence
fields stay on existing typed Program boundary relations; there is no `Port`
type/plane, summary, or deferred name lookup.

### Program artifact persistence

The canonical module-artifact phase is the persistence boundary for precomputed
modules, not another semantic IR. The two construction paths meet at the same
immutable Program shard:

```text
source → parse → bind → Program shard
stored module artifact → validate/decode ─┘

Program shard → canonical encode → stored module artifact
Program shard + Target/bindings → Link → optional linked-project section
```

After decode, project link, Rules, Solver, and Queries cannot observe which path
produced the shard. There is no manifest-specific Term, type/effect vocabulary,
summary plane, engine Rule, or
compatibility adapter.

One versioned canonical artifact envelope has three strictly layered sections.
Only the first is required:

1. **Program core.** It contains:

- format/schema and target-language-contract digests; parser/binder/lowerer/
  sealer producer versions for the canonical shard core;
- the complete semantic shard identity, source/module/reflection identity,
  dense shard-local Term and atom tables with canonical typed payloads, spans,
  Program rows/direct indexes, explicit boundary/import/export relations,
  typed unresolved requirements and content identities for declared external
  operations/providers, authored access occurrences/endpoints/dispatch modes,
  and shard-local source-control `Mu`. It contains no resolved Target operation,
  EndpointTarget, Link boundary, provider binding, module representative, Link
  digest, or Link Guard/substitution/evidence reference. The Program rows include each runtime
  TypeValue call-base seed, its source occurrence, and its already resolved
  primitive-or-direct-chunk-declaration authority. Decode validates those
  closed references and the producer/Target digest but never reruns binding or
  resolves a source name. Complete provider/operation contract bodies
  remain Target-law and Link-binding authority and are not copied into the
  shard core;
- unresolved typed dependency requirements for imported boundaries,
  providers/schemas, and module-cache formals, but no project resolution or
  actor-local representative relation;
- static declarations/types/effect schemas and body entry/outcome
  correspondences in their one canonical representations;

2. **Linked-project section.** This optional section is produced and admitted
only by Link after Program cores, Target, bindings, and module representatives
are sealed. Its identity names the exact Program ContentIDs and minimal Link
component/relation digests. It contains only resolved Link relations and cold
rebind data: EndpointTarget/boundary correspondences, provider/import binding,
module-cache representative transport, and exact references to Program
source-control Mu/Guard/substitution/evidence coordinates. It contains no
Program row copy, domain value, Solver topology, Candidate/product tuple, or
recurrence/SCC/activation witness. Program decode treats the entire section as
opaque framing; Link validates or discards it after linking.

3. **Equation-template section.** This optional Solver-produced section carries the producer
  `CompositionID`, exact engine equation-schema identity, and boundary-schema
  identities. `CompositionID` is whole-Solver provenance, not a template key.
  Each body/component template's canonical identity and bytes are reusable only
  while its owning immutable shard and that shard's Term namespace are
  unchanged, and then only for its exact engine equation-schema identity and
  local Factor/Rule/anchor/boundary/applicability closure, including its
  complete anchored Rule-instance closure.
  It depends on Program ContentID plus only the exact linked-project component
  digests observed by each template; it is never part of Program or Link
  semantic identity. The section contains only body-local point-equation topology: stable Point
  specifications, atomic Group rows with exact output Point, ordered
  input-Point/reindex occurrences and member ranges, shard Terms, and canonical
  Rule-instance rows,
  typed references to the core's shard-local source-control `Mu`, and typed
  references to existing boundary dependencies. Each Rule-instance row retains
  its Rule schema identity; its canonical `FactorOutput(Factor)` or
  `StructuralOutput` disposition; ordered input-position/Factor reads,
  including dynamic-versus-exact-key disposition and the exact key when
  present; for a Factor output, exact writes or the canonical staged
  write-target DAG disposition, its presealed target/summary capability
  surface, and its coverage/closure-law identities, all belonging only to that
  output Factor; for a structural output, no write schema or Carry and the
  exact support-prune/activation capability surface; and exactly one full
  typed anchor:
  `At(shard, term)`, `From(shard, exact Program Edge)`, or
  `Relation(exact Link Application)`. A Rule schema may have any finite number
  of instances. Rows are sorted and deduplicated only by the full canonical
  Rule-instance identity: Rule `SemanticKey`, output disposition, full
  typed anchor, canonical ordered read schema, and exact-or-staged write-target
  schema. The compiler emits at most one row per that identity; the section
  decoder rejects a duplicate rather than coalescing it. `(Rule schema, typed
  anchor)` is only
  an ordering prefix and may never break a tie or stand in for instance
  identity. Reducing `From` to its destination Term or `Relation` to its source
  occurrence is invalid because those projections do not identify a Rule
  instance. It contains no
  Factor value, producer candidate/fold tree, Point state/version, read log,
  demand cone, evidence, certificate, project SCC/WTO, schedule, or
  compiled-equation head. Selected dynamic-relation instances, their supports,
  and their solve-epoch coordinates are likewise absent.

The optional section orders Factor identities and complete Rule instances by
their canonical semantic identities; declaration order, package registration
order, and dense Solver slots are absent. Each body/component template is
independently addressed only for its unchanged owning shard and retained Term
namespace plus its exact rederived local Factor/Rule/anchor/boundary/
applicability closure. An irrelevant change in a different shard, or in a
dependency outside that closure, leaves its canonical identity and bytes
unchanged; any owning-shard or Term-namespace change is a miss even when it
does not alter a local relation. `CompositionID` remains whole-Solver
provenance only: a mismatch triggers that exact local rederivation, and reuse
is permitted only when the owning-shard/Term-namespace precondition and the
rederived local closure and applicability coverage match the stored template
exactly. This is canonical compiler validation, not a second template
authority. A change from
declaration-ordered Factors to this canonical schema
bumps the equation-section schema and engine-producer versions. An older
ordered equation section is an ordinary derived-section miss and is rebuilt;
it neither invalidates the Program-shard core nor authorizes an old decoder or
ordering fallback.

Domain/engine identities never enter the canonical Program-shard core,
Term namespace, project link, or source-built/decoded Program equality.
Producer/dependency identity is scoped to the smallest section that interprets
it: engine/Factor/Rule/widening for equations. Proof/evidence-checker and
Query-projection versions apply only to separate State-bound operational
caches.

Portable equations contain no cross-shard head: project binding creates only
exact Link boundary rows and never an equation or relation-family product.
The Solver alone extends its structural topology and creates solve-epoch-local
support, read logs, values, and reverse dependencies. A whole-project static
binding cache is valid only under the complete project-link and `CompositionID`;
a mismatch discards and recomputes that binding. It does not govern admission
of a body-local equation template, whose exact local closure/applicability
rederivation plus its unchanged owning-shard/Term-namespace precondition
remains the sole reuse decision. A
State/evidence/certificate cache is operational and State-bound. Stored
evidence and certificates must pass the same local-law, post-fixpoint,
constructive-proof, and dependency validation as fresh artifacts. Every absent
or invalid derived section recomputes from the canonical shard, never selects
another semantic path.

A certificate projection held in that cache remains State-bound merely cached
analysis output unless it independently satisfies the future-consumer
eligibility law below. Matching a State or observation revision cannot by
itself promote the projection into a published future-consumer certificate.

A context/input-specific solved State is never a portable module section. Any
non-portable State, evidence, or certificate cache is discarded on a
universal-validity mismatch.

The Program-shard core records a `typeof` occurrence only through its existing
static reference and exact operand/source identity. Static containment is
rederived and checked from the encoded Program topology; Link records only its
resolved static namespace inputs, including a static-only literal-require row
where admitted. Source TypePublication rows retain their exact
assignment-pair/path/frontier/target evidence; Link records the resulting
content-addressed export-surface resolution, never a Heap/cache observation.
After Link has supplied `N` and the normalized substitution environment,
static-domain installation deterministically derives `ContainedOperand` under
`S` and then its `typeof` result: an exact known/unknown/diagnostic static
result, or the canonical `TypeofHandle` only for `RuntimeSubject`. Only that
handle's interpreted static-type Rule result and exact Value/type read are
State-bound. Neither the core nor an optional equation section serializes a
contained-operand result, a converged `Closed` interpretation, an operand
Factor value, or a duplicate static-containment graph. An equation section
retains only the typed Program/Link operands of a runtime-subject Rule
instance; admission rederives and verifies the judgment/handle when applicable
and the boundary schema's finite class-LUB/rank identity under the section's
existing Rule/Factor/schema dependencies. A mismatch discards the derived
section and recomputes it, never selects another type authority.

The storage-only format uses canonical typed encodings, ordering, graph
backreferences, lengths, and digests; it forbids process pointers, link slots,
Go types/interfaces/reflection, paths, sentinels, and parsed strings. The core
digest excludes optional sections; each derived section has canonical bytes
under the core plus all producer/dependency identities. The outer directory is
canonical for a selected section set, so decode followed by encode reproduces
the complete container bytes for that set; different valid optional-section
sets may differ without changing shard semantic identity. Program owns and
validates only its core codec. The Solver alone admits and revalidates the
optional equation section carried by the envelope; absent, unknown, or
mismatched equation data is discarded and regenerated from the core.
Equation-template persistence records only semantic identities and typed
Program/Link operands; it does not require or authorize a generic Factor-value
codec, a Program-owned Rule decoder, or a second registry.

The reusable shard core and a body-local equation template retain only the
typed `ModuleCacheInstance` formal and representative-relation schema; they do
not copy a deployment's alias map. Every persisted linked-project binding, and
every derived artifact or cache that closes that formal, names the exact
canonical `ModuleCacheRep(instance, rep(instance))` relation digest and the
minimal owning Link component/relation digests it actually observes. It names
full `Link.ContentID` only when it observes the whole linked project. A missing
or mismatched named digest discards and relinks/recomputes the derived artifact;
it cannot fall back to raw instance identity or silently broaden the dependency
set.

Dependency fields are semantic claims, never unverified caller metadata. A
shard core hashes each unresolved external boundary/interface requirement that
its rows declare, and core decode validates those claims against the embedded
typed requirement declarations. Project Link then resolves every import,
host/provider contract (including call-independent exposure/member selectors,
their access occurrences/endpoints/dispatch modes, `EndpointTarget` records,
exact selector/endpoint-target/application-boundary references for direct
  exposure/member calls, and no-overwrite-law rows), schema, exact `ModuleInit`
  common-predecessor Cold, presealed fresh-generation,
  predecessor-materialization, and atomic two-owner successor correspondence, the sealed finite
  `ModuleCacheInstance` alias equivalence, its exact deterministic
  representative relation and canonical digest, and the sealed binding-world
  revision, rejects any missing or
mismatched identity, and verifies in the linked-project section that each
`EndpointTarget` contains only its direct-Function endpoint, Target operation,
and content identity—never runtime call form, formal, receiver, or substitution
payload—and that every application/boundary row's Target operation equals its referenced
`EndpointTarget` operation before it
deterministically derives the canonical sealed Link relation from those exact
inputs. That relation contains the sorted canonical
`ModuleCacheRep(instance, rep(instance))` rows and their digest.
`Link.ContentID` hashes the versioned Link-schema/producer digest, Target and
shard IDs, ordered resolved semantic IDs, that representative-relation digest,
and the ordered canonical semantic component digests. Derived cold/index/cache
data is excluded and is constructed only after this root digest. Two projects with different
resolved semantics, or a different Link interpretation, cannot share a Link
identity even when their source shards are byte-identical. A
body/equation/State/certificate cache may
key only the proved subset it observes, but must name and revalidate that exact
subset; dependency-minimal reuse never means dependency-free publication.

Mutable provider/resumer state and its observation revision are solve inputs
only through their provider/Target-declared owning Factors. They participate in
the exact Factor read projection and any resulting State/evidence identity, and
in the identity of any State-bound cached certificate projection; they do not
churn Program shards or Link when the sealed binding and operation semantics
are unchanged. An observation-revision match validates only that operational
reuse. It is not a remaining condition under which a future consumer may use a
published certificate.

The live `analysis/module/signature` and `analysis/module/manifest` formats,
including their `OperationalEffects` cold lanes and the root runtime's
`FunctionProto.TypeInfo`, are a read-only runtime artifact until a separately
authorized integration. They are not this Program artifact and have no
Program, Target, Link, engine, domain, equation, effect, or analyzer-denominator
authority. The canonical analyzer never imports, reads, emits, translates, or
falls back to them. Their continued runtime use cannot justify a compatibility
codec or bridge in the analyzer.

Decode is iterative, total, bounds-checked, transactional, and constructs the
ordinary builders only; decoded wire structs never escape the persistence
package. It validates schema/producer/Target-contract identities,
section digests, declared dependency identities, canonical ordering, all
index/range/kind references, boundary
closure, and recursive core backreferences. The Program/envelope decoder
checks only the length and digest framing of an optional equation blob and
otherwise treats it as opaque. After project Link and the live Factor/Rule
composition exist, the Solver's one section decoder reads the recorded producer
`CompositionID` as provenance and validates canonical ordering, typed Program/Link references,
recursive backreferences, equation/lattice/widening versions, and exact
observed dependency digest before admission. It deterministically rederives the
complete expected body/component topology, Rule-instance closure, and
Rule-schema applicability coverage from the current Program/Link and sealed
Factor/Rule composition, then requires each template to match that local result
exactly: no applicable transfer, Rule instance, body boundary, or source-control
recurrence reference may be omitted, added, or changed. Equality includes the
full typed Rule anchor, output, ordered reads/exact keys, and exact-or-staged
write-target disposition; it never assumes a Rule semantic/version key uniquely
identifies one occurrence. A
producer `CompositionID` mismatch is neither a fallback nor an automatic
section rejection: the same local rederivation permits reuse only for a
template whose owning shard and Term namespace are unchanged and whose
canonical local closure and applicability coverage match; otherwise that
template is an ordinary derived-section miss.
Dynamic relations admitted during solving pass through the same compiler. A
validated eligible constituent tuple and its admission evidence become
monotone Solver-resident structural metadata only inside one solve session.
A solve session is bound to one immutable Program/Target/Link/Composition and
one exact external-input revision vector. It starts with only exact known
boundaries, ends on publication, cancellation, or any input-revision change,
and never carries an accepted tuple or admission evidence into another
session. Inside it, an equation epoch is one disposable Point/Group/SCC/WTO
realization. Accepted tuples participate in the session's
accepted-relation-set digest and survive only equation-epoch rebuilds caused by
monotone admission.

Admission is irreversible for topology during that session, while the Rule's
Guard support and semantic contribution remain ordinary dependency-tracked
outputs and may retract to empty. A retained dormant tuple with empty support
therefore contributes no behavior and cannot make stale evidence true. Point
values, Group proposals, support, read logs, reverse buckets, dirty state,
SCC/WTO instances, and derivation evidence are disposable and are rebuilt
against the session's accepted tuple set. Neither the tuple set nor its digest
becomes a portable Rule instance, equation template, Program/Link artifact, or
cross-session cache seed. An immutable published State records the digest only
as provenance for that same completed session. A
stored source-control `Mu` is only a typed reference to the exact canonical core row;
it never carries a second recurrence definition or transport authority. No
other layer can mark that section semantically valid.

A malformed or unverifiable core publishes nothing. An absent, malformed,
unknown-version, or semantically mismatched optional section is discarded and
recomputed through the canonical Solver; it never invalidates an otherwise
valid core or selects another semantic path. Resource exhaustion publishes
nothing; unavailable source in a precomputed-only deployment fails explicitly.
There is no fallback to an old decoder or weaker analysis. Storage commits
atomically; corrupt leftovers are invalid. Storage addresses the shard core by its semantic digest and each
derived section/container by its own canonical digest. Schema replacement
rebuilds or rejects all old blobs with one live decoder. I/O/hashing remain
cold path, and decoded shards use the same dense local hot-path representation
as source-built shards.

### Program sealing

Sealing is structural and deterministic:

- validate all handles and relation cardinalities;
- resolve lexical references, labels, outcomes, and direct structural calls;
- freeze ordered relation ranges and direct indexes needed by Rules;
- build control predecessors/successors and exact occurrence ownership;
- at shard seal, compute source-control recurrence wholly owned by the shard
  while retaining exact direct-call edges and recurrence fields on existing
  import/module-initialization/resumption/callback boundary relations;
- at project seal, resolve those explicit relations and validate each boundary
  row's own decisions re-evaluated on the next visit plus the Program
  predicate/value parameters that remain live. Distinct boundary interfaces
  are never composed or unioned merely because their equations share an SCC;
- retain unresolved/higher-order applications as exact application sites; the
  Solver recompiles the active equation epoch when a dynamic call/resumption
  edge is discovered;
- validate packs, lenses, captures, authored type/effect formals and boundary
  operands, and source anchors;
- hash the immutable Program and its module shards.

Every source operation family has a typed Program row keyed by its Occurrence
and body-local dense range. Domain construction iterates the exact typed range
once to instantiate Rules. The engine receives already-instantiated Rules and
never learns a relation kind; Rule application performs no scan. There is no
generic rule-kind/operand-role/topology code plane.

The frozen target-language contract is the sole denominator that generates and
validates the finite semantic operation matrix, builtin/provider bindings,
lifecycle rows, and conformance ledger. The matrix is
explicit and typed. It covers
literal/constant creation; every unary, binary, relational, logical, length,
concat, conversion, and truthiness operation; cell/lens read and write; all
table-constructor field modes; Values adjustment; closure/capture creation;
plain/method/dynamic/external calls; returns and every supported outcome;
branches, loops, labels/gotos; vararg and generic iteration; metatable/raw
operations; coroutine/handler operations; and module/provider initialization.
Any parser, builtin, provider, or lifecycle addition must extend that contract
and matrix or fail sealing. No analyzer fallback infers a missing row from AST
shape, a numeric operation code, or runtime behavior.

An outcome that creates a nominal runtime object carries one sealed
`FreshResult(outcome, fixed-result, ordinal, kind)` row. The ordinal is unique
within the operation outcome and proves that result distinct from all prior
roots and every other fresh ordinal in that outcome. It is orthogonal to a
Produced callable row, so one result may be both a fresh closure and the carrier
of a produced operation. Open Pack tails cannot be declared fresh. This one
relation covers table/closure/coroutine/error/reflection allocation,
`table.create`/`table.pack`, pattern iterator closures with their embedded
state, and provider-declared
fresh results; alias/imported/unknown results have no such row. Target sealing,
Link queries, canonical codec, content identity, and the coverage matrix all
validate it.

Every operation controlled by the target Lua metatable contract has a closed
typed application relation in that same matrix; “metatable operation” is not a
catch-all row. The relation records primitive eligibility, raw-presence lookup,
the specified operand/fallback order, function or table delegation where the
operation permits it, argument and Values adjustment, normal/error/non-local
outcomes, and raw bypass behavior. This covers `__index` and `__newindex`
chains, `__call`, arithmetic/bitwise/unary/concat/length/comparison fallbacks,
protected metatable access, and every iteration or lifecycle metamethod
actually supported by the frozen language contract. Program declares the
exact source alternatives; metatable/value/heap Rules select feasible branches.

No synthetic “metamethod operation” Term exists. Link seals a function-style
application for every live Program meta source in unary/length,
arithmetic/bitwise/concat/equality/order, index-get, and index-set families,
and for every Target-declared internal sub-edge that invokes one of those
families. An internal application is anchored by its existing owning
application/boundary and the Target row's sealed sub-edge coordinate; it does
not mint a Program occurrence or a second Call relation. It supplies the
family's exact operands, ordinary callable receiver convention, formal/actual
Pack transport, scalar result placement, yield disposition, and every declared
outcome. Direct bodies and typed external operations are eligible endpoints of
that same implicit relation family. Ordinary `__call` remains the existing Call application.

The selected tag-method Value is dynamic Factor data, never structural
identity. A direct function handler receives the family operands. If the
selected handler is itself non-function but callable through `__call`, the
ordinary callable convention prepends that selected handler Value—not the
base/left operand—to the family operands. Every function-style meta
application that can Yield also exposes the same finite continuation
projection as Call: retained Cells/captures, pending Values, live Guard
parameters, exact source successor, resumer correspondence, and every restored
outcome. No Call-only reconstruction is permitted.

A table-valued `__index` or `__newindex` handler is delegation, not a dynamic
application identity. Its Rule transfers `(table,key[,value])` back through
the same typed access relation with identity Guard transport; it never creates
a structural coordinate keyed by the selected table object. Function/callable handlers use
the sealed Link application. If the active equation graph becomes cyclic, the
equation compiler assigns its ordinary private `Mu`; no Go recursion follows a
metatable chain.

The concrete Target profile seals separate IndexGet and IndexSet delegation
laws. The original base is inspected first. Raw/direct success,
missing-handler completion, and function dispatch terminate the access; a
table-valued handler continues from that exact table identity. Revisiting a
base identity on the same concrete access takes the law's distinct cycle Throw
category/payload. A finite acyclic chain has no arbitrary maximum. The Heap
Rule computes the may/must reachability and cyclic alternatives over its
existing finite root relation, and the equation compiler represents cyclic
dependencies with its ordinary `Mu`; neither Target nor Solver carries an
attempt counter, recursion limit, or fuel. An unknown delegation relation
retains both terminating and cycle alternatives until its owning domain can
prove one absent. IndexGet and IndexSet remain separate because their raw
completion and handler operands differ.

One project-sealed external-operation relation maps a provider/import binding
to a dense sealed operation handle, exact calling/Values convention, result
and outcome convention, module-initialization boundary, and every concrete
boundary event permitted by its typed provider contract. Those typed events
cover synchronous callback application; zero/repeated application;
callback/capture retention and later re-entry; continuation retention,
suspension, resumption, cancellation, and handler transfer; and the Values and
outcome correspondence for each event.

The contract is not an unordered event inventory. It seals one finite typed
control/causal relation: each event occurrence has its enabling condition,
normal and non-local successors, Values transport, activation
creation/restoration identity, retention/release ownership transition, and
whether provider return waits for callback completion. It distinguishes
one-shot from repeated resumption and synchronous return from retained
asynchronous re-entry. Repeated/re-entrant event edges carry structural
recurrence and therefore participate in the ordinary compiled-equation
`Mu`/SCC model. This is the exact Program structure used by typestate,
ownership, escape, effect, and the other named Factor Rules; the placement
Query/certificate projects their converged facts and never reconstructs event
order.

That relation uses closed typed row families for callback dispatch, provider
re-entry, cancellation, and release; a trigger is always an existing
call/outcome/callback/resume/re-entry coordinate, never a free scheduler
operand. Each row names an existing acquisition result or operation-input
carrier, required protocol state, exact triggering coordinate, multiplicity,
successor state and duty action, and total Normal/Return/Throw/Yield/Cancel
correspondence. Provider re-entry additionally names the existing Values
source supplying its restored arguments. Cancellation states whether an
active continuation takes Cancel or only future delivery is released. No row
mints a provider-handle or event identity.

The contract derives one sealed finite protocol-carrier set from existing
operation input and outcome/result roles, callback argument and outcome roles,
and suspension/resume/re-entry roles. Protocol cases are exhaustive over every
declared applicable `(trigger, carrier, state)` triple. Every case names its
outcomes, successor state, and duty action; invalid-state Throw,
idempotent/no-op, state preservation, and opaque behavior are explicit cases,
never meanings assigned to absence. Seal rejects a missing or duplicate case
bundle. An abstract state unions the declared concrete cases it contains, so
channel close/send-after-close and resource release/error behavior cannot be
guessed by a Rule or defaulted by the engine. The carrier set is contract
coverage data over existing coordinates, not a generic lifecycle engine
relation.

Protocol acquisition and transition sources also admit exact callback
endpoints: a callback argument can be the acquired resource, and a callback
outcome can trigger its state transition/release. This is required to express
scoped provider laws on callback Throw/Yield/Cancel. Link composes these sealed
rows with its existing callback/resume applications and continuation
projection; analyzer Rules never infer lifecycle order from loose flags.

The Solver instantiates structural dependencies from these sealed event
relations; call Rules supply the possible bodies for callback Values, and
type/effect/heap/escape/ownership Rules interpret the same contract. The
placement Query/certificate then projects only their converged named-Factor
conclusions.
An opaque external operation is one explicit maximally nondeterministic typed
control relation over the callbacks/Values it receives, including retention,
re-entry, suspension, mutation, and outcomes—not an absent edge or fallback
registry. There is no second intrinsic/provider registry.

Actor/host transfer operations seal only their observable ABI relation:
success value contents at the transfer occurrence, aliasing within one
delivered graph, isolation from later sender mutation, capability preservation
or contract-declared loss, failure/rejection classes, and whether
cross-delivery identity is observable or explicitly unspecified. Domains infer
escape, ownership, portability, mutation, lifetime, and eligibility facts.
For every boot-global aggregate, Target owns one exact immutable
`BootShape(initial-root identity, aggregate schema, initial Value identity)`
ABI row. Link's actor-boot correspondence binds that row to the sealed
deployment/actor instance; the row's content-addressed identity is the only
boot-shape identity an ABI transfer may preserve. Heap alone owns every dynamic
shape conclusion for the associated aggregate root—field/slot state,
metatable route, shape eligibility, and later materialization. A transferred
actor Value preserves the applicable `BootShape` row identity across the
boundary. The row participates in Target content identity, transfer coverage,
and artifact dependency validation. This is only an ABI identity invariant; it
defines no delivery image and selects no runtime transfer policy.
A future executable consumer may choose retain, promotion, serialization,
move, clone, or COW only when observationally equivalent to that relation; this
wave neither chooses nor implements one.

Labels target source positions, including empty positions before `end`.
Multiple labels may share a position. Goto carries its resolved
source-position edge and binder proof that it does not enter a live local.
Sealing propagates normal, return, break, and goto, plus only those
throw/yield/resume/cancellation outcomes introduced by a supported
source/provider/runtime operation, through every nested lexical sequence. A
terminal outcome never fabricates the skipped suffix, and normal fallthrough
enters exactly one tail.

For a construct that can actually suspend or handle a non-local outcome,
Program records one typed continuation relation: suspension occurrence,
outgoing Values, resumed input Values, outcome kind, target body/scope,
handler/resumer boundary, and the lexically reachable binding/cell/capture
environment. Effect derives its semantic survival conclusion from it; the
placement Query/certificate may project the resulting named-Factor survival
facts. Ordinary structured control and goto do not use it.

Each call/module boundary owns one sealed typed correspondence covering
formal/actual Values, captures, receiver, arguments and open tails, every
outcome result pack, imported/exported bindings, type parameters, authored
effect-row variables, and structural activation/allocation sites. These
substitutions are over Program static/schema handles, never resolved inferred
types or effect rows. The correspondence names structural roots, not inferred
reachable heap. Domain boundary Rules consume this same
correspondence and the joint Factor product; no domain reconstructs it.

The supported parser grammar is authoritative. If it has no authored Koka
handler/row syntax, Program does not invent such source syntax: Koka rows are
the effect domain's abstraction of Lua operations and typed provider/module
contracts. The Program-schema closure wave fixes only missing parser evidence required by existing
typed syntax, including asserted-parameter token identity, static annotation
arguments, and the source occurrence referenced by `typeof`.

Program does not perform general SSA conversion and does not fabricate phi
nodes. Expressions already have immutable value identities; mutation is
represented by cell/lens reads and writes. A syntactically immutable binding
may point directly at its value. Physical SSA and register allocation are
future-consumer concerns and are not Program or analyzer semantics.

Sealing is bounded by the emitted rows and edges plus the materialized
decision-reachability/boundary-support incidence, with sorting and one-time
interning stated separately. That support can be quadratic in adversarial
source control and is measured honestly; no unsupported `O(V+E)` claim hides
it. A Rule never scans a Program relation or builds a private index: every
required relation is available as a frozen direct range or lookup.

### Lowerer organization

The lowerer is vertical, not one monolithic horizontal package. Its production
children write directly into one unfinished Program builder:

```text
program/lower
  lexical
  eval
  store
  control
  call
  static
  module
```

The parent only sequences phases and seals. The children share dense AST/binder
handles through the Program's internal builder; they do not exchange shadow
IRs, adapters, string codes, or callbacks that decide language semantics. A
single syntax walk allocates source occurrences and invokes typed builder
operations; each child owns and validates the canonical relation ranges for its
semantic family.

Lowering laws explicitly distinguish declaration/initializer visibility,
ordinary `local f = function` from recursive `local function f`, implicit
method `self`, vararg binding, loop-local lifetimes, and function boundaries
for break/label/goto.

Black-box tests lower source and assert Program semantics. No test inspects Go
files, package imports, directory layout, registrations, or composition shape.

The generated coverage matrix is schema-derived and closed over the current
AST fields, not maintained only as a hand-written node list. For every AST
implementation it takes the finite product of every semantically relevant
enum, boolean, nullable, list-cardinality class, token role, child-node class,
and target-law equivalence class at one constructor step; each product member
must map to a typed Program row or an explicitly justified impossible state.
Unbounded scalar payload domains—identifier text, source strings,
integer/float values, and literal contents—are parameters of those structural
cases, never matrix axes. Generated/property tests quantify over arbitrary
valid payloads and boundary/exceptional representatives for parsing,
normalization, target key equality, numeric overflow/rounding, serialization,
and alpha-renaming.

Arbitrary list length and nesting depth are structural and are covered
inductively, not called payloads or enumerated as an impossible finite product.
Every recursive AST/list schema generates a base law and one law per recursive
constructor. Sequence lowering separately proves empty, prefix-step, and final
element behavior, preserving left-to-right evaluation, lvalue commit order,
scope, non-local tails, and final-open `Values` expansion. Recursive nesting
proves that composing a lawful child row preserves binding, spans, control,
correspondence, and structural `Mu`.

Assignment matrix laws additionally prove the one transaction end-to-end:
every LHS address is evaluated in source order before the one RHS Values
production and adjustment; every LHS ordinal has exactly one adjusted Pack-slot
correspondence; and commits run from the last Write to the first. They include
aliasing Cells/Lenses, duplicate targets, dynamic keys, nil-fill/open tails,
ordinary and raw writes, `__newindex` normal/non-local outcomes, and a mixed
assignment containing a TypePublication pair. Encode/decode must preserve the
same address entries, slot/pair relation, Write order, and successor chain.

These are executable compositional proof obligations, not sampling claims.
The schema generator uses the exact canonical Program row declarations and
builders to construct partial rows whose recursive child positions are typed
symbolic holes. The checker for every base/constructor law validates
composition through the same generated row contracts used by ordinary
lowering. It may not define a fragment IR, alternate builder, operation
semantics, or second semantic switch. It must discharge row assembly, operand
correspondence, order, scope, tails, indexes, and `Mu` preservation for
arbitrary lawful substitutions into those holes; a missing or failed
obligation makes generated coverage/sealing fail. Structural induction over
those checked constructors establishes the result for every finite source
tree. Generated property/fuzz tests still exercise arbitrary heterogeneous
sequences and depths, but are adversarial evidence rather than the universal
proof. Thus the one-step structural denominator is finite and exact without
presenting a finite sample as 100%. The following human-readable list is a
minimum digest, not the source of coverage:

- expressions: true/false/nil/number/string, vararg, identifier, dot/bracket
  access, table, plain/method/generic call, `and`/`or`, every relational,
  concat, arithmetic/bitwise and unary operator, function literal, cast, and
  non-nil assertion; both cast syntaxes, adjusted/unadjusted call/vararg,
  qualified methods, and simple/qualified source names are separate cases;
- statements: assignment, annotated local assignment, call statement, `do`,
  `while`, `repeat`, `if`/`elseif`/empty or present `else`, numeric and generic
  `for`, named/method function definition, return, break, label, goto, type
  alias, and interface declaration; numeric-for default/explicit step,
  generic-for binding arity/open iterator tail, and every function-name/
  receiver form are separate cases;
- static forms: annotation, primitive, optional, union, intersection, array,
  map, record/optional field, type parameter/constraint, function/variadic/
  asserting return, reference, generic application, typed literal, metatype,
  self, tuple, `typeof`, `keyof`, indexed access, and conditional type;
  interface extends/fields/optional fields/methods, qualified type-reference
  paths, every generic/type-parameter binding site, and annotations on
  primitive, array element, array, record field, local, parameter, and vararg
  are separate generated cases; readonly array/map/record, literal
  boolean/string/integer/float kind and target-law class, named/anonymous function
  parameters, variadic position, nil/empty/singleton/pack returns, and
  `asserts x` versus `asserts x is T` are separate field products. Every
  ordinary expression subtree in a static operand proves its transitive
  static-containment disposition; `typeof(require("literal"))`, unresolved
  literal require, rejected nonliteral require, and every rejected contained operation
  are separate source-built/decoded cases. Source TypePublication cases cover
  each exact dotted path/original assignment pair, mixed published and ordinary
  pairs, returned versus non-exported roots, and qualified import lookup;
- use contexts: condition, scalar, parenthesized scalar, non-final list, final
  expanding list, fixed/open assignment, call argument/result, return, table
  array/named/dynamic field, lvalue base/key/commit, numeric/generic loop
  header, annotation/static argument, nested closure/capture, module/provider,
  and every normal/non-local lexical tail; table bracket-literal versus dynamic
  key and final-open versus non-final array field are distinct cases.

Adding or changing an AST implementation or semantic field without adding its
operation row and every lawful field-product/use-context case makes the generated coverage gate
or seal testing fail. Coverage is measured against the reflected/generated AST
schema denominator, never against the cases the lowerer happened to register.

## Formal engine

The engine receives an immutable Program plus declared Factors, Rules, and
Queries. It owns no Program-like inventory and no Lua relation.

### Guard

Guard is the only public path predicate. Decisions and edges are Program
handles; polarity is one BDD literal and the engine canonicalizes their
predicates privately in one fixed-order reduced BDD universe. A boundary's
typed source/target interfaces are declared coordinate views of that one
universe, not separately allocated BDDs or an untyped common carrier. Guard atoms name
only presealed Program/Link decision occurrences: they never name values,
heap cells, types, equality constraints, numeric predicates, or effects.
Conjunction, disjunction, conflict, entailment, Boolean substitution,
existential elimination, renaming, and alternative selection are exact and
structurally shared. Guard exposes no DNF API, first-order term language,
theory solver, or cross-domain relation store. A domain may use a typed
relation, partition, or constraint representation inside its opaque `V`; Guard
and the joint guarded Factor carrier cannot interpret it. Each exact
Program/Link boundary row declares typed source and target Guard interfaces,
which decisions are re-evaluated, and which predicate parameters are live
across that boundary. Its reindexing `ω_G^σ : Guard_src(σ) -> Guard_tgt(σ)`
existentially discharges the former and renames the latter before confluence,
with the source/target soundness, canonical composition, exact idempotent
normalization, and finite-canonical-eventual-orbit laws above. The finite
presealed Guard-support lattice is joined through the ordinary equation
iteration; Factor widening/ranks, rather than a transport-orbit cap, provide
convergence. A compiled `Mu`
declares no Guard interface; ordinary Rule/reduction edges use typed identity
transport. A cached body uses canonical body-local source Guards. Boundary
Rules normalize and reindex them into an activation target interface when
applying the formal result; instantiated Guard identity includes Program
decision provenance and substitution, so unrelated bodies cannot alias while
equal callers can still share the formal solution. No hot operation sorts
slices or allocates guard lists.

Guard partitions are joint-state alternatives. They are not repeated
independently inside every Factor key.

### Factor

A Factor owns:

- its typed key order/hash;
- element bottom/top, equality, order, join, widening, and optional narrowing;
- its semantic default and canonical sparse-storage law;
- immutable sparse storage and interning.

Body transport is not a set of independent Factor actions. Domain-owned
boundary Rules consume the Program's single typed correspondence and execute
over the same joint guarded coordinate as ordinary Rules. They declare every Factor
read/write, stabilize with reductions, and obey identity, composition,
alpha-renaming, monotonicity, guard preservation, and freshness laws. Adding a
Factor adds its owning boundary Rules; it never enlarges a universal summary
struct or creates a parallel carrier.

The heterogeneous hot state is a flat vector of small private root handles.
Typed Factor arenas resolve those handles. It is not `[]any`, and joining a
point does not box or reflect on domain values.

### Rule

A Rule declares its exact Factor reads, exact or staged write-target schema,
explicit whole-plane carries, decisions, revocations, and the Program
occurrences at which it applies. Reads are logged at `(Factor, dependency unit,
Guard)` granularity, and writes log their resolved presealed target capability
and output Guard. The dependency unit is the Factor key unless the owning
Factor supplies the finer stable-token closure law defined above.

The same sealed Rule schema declares every non-Factor scheduling effect. A
Rule that can remove its current successor support declares a support-prune
capability. A Rule that can select and activate a relation declares the finite
relation-family constructor and constituent ranges it may instantiate,
including the existing Program/Link application/endpoint ranges and target Rule identity. Runtime
`Prune` and `Activate` accept only the corresponding opaque declaration
capability and reject undeclared support removal, target, or family use.
`Activate` may admit only a tuple whose constituents are sealed and whose pure
family predicate succeeds; it never invents a constituent or portable identity. Support-prune declarations
and reverse activation-family declarations
participate in demand closure, scheduling, artifact identity, and coverage;
they are not inferred from a callback that happened to execute. An exact
whole-plane carry likewise names its source position and output Factor and is
rejected outside that declaration.

Every Rule has one canonical output disposition: `FactorOutput(Factor)` or
`StructuralOutput`. A Factor-output Rule may return at most one typed patch for
its named Factor and may declare Carries only for that Factor; it declares no
support-prune or activation effect. A structural-output Rule returns no Factor
patch, has no write-target schema or Carry, and must declare at least one
support-prune or activation capability. This is one Rule vocabulary and one
Group evaluator, not a structural side channel. The disposition and its exact
capabilities participate in identity, demand, scheduling, persistence, and
coverage. An activation discovered by a structural-output Rule installs its
validated relation delta and restarts the affected epoch before any candidate
from the superseded revision can publish.

A support-prune Rule is output-free with respect to Factors, but it is not
restricted to one input or to zero Factor reads. It may declare the same typed
read projection and ordered Group inputs as any other Rule, and it returns only
a proved subset of the Group's structurally completed support. It may share an
atomic Group with distinct-Factor writers when the structural successor
requires both the prune and those patches; every member still reads the same
committed snapshot and no member reads a sibling result. Multiple prune results
meet on support inside `AtomicFinish`. This creates neither a reachability
Factor nor a Factor read-to-write incidence edge: its declared reads enter
demand, invalidation, recurrence membership, and evidence closure directly.
The zero-read, one-input contradiction is a required special-case law, not the
general support-prune API. In a Factor-bearing Group, a prune-only member does
not preserve predecessor facts by implication; every semantically live plane
must still be produced by its owner Rule or explicit Carry.

The compiler derives Factor read-to-write/carry incidence, reverse staged
write-target surfaces, and all support or activation followers directly from
these sealed Rule schemas. Runtime read and target logs refine invalidation to
the units actually observed; they never define the static demand graph. No
separately authored executable incidence edge list, registration order, or
domain work-package graph may duplicate the derived authority.

Typed writes distinguish replacement/strong update from domain join/weak
update. Program supplies only the candidate write identity and structural
freshness origin. Strong replacement is judged pointwise in the
concretization: in every represented concrete state, the write must target the
one current storage denoted by that abstract location. Binder-proven
non-captured, non-escaping invocation locals have this structural licence even
inside loops and recursive bodies. They live in the current formal activation;
suspended caller locals cross the call boundary as formal continuation data and
are restored after return, so recurrence never aliases them with the callee's
current local. Captured/upvalue/global/heap locations and any location whose
activation identity is stored or shared require the owning domain's
must-uniqueness proof. Revoking that proof restores weak update.
Heap/lens replacement likewise requires the owning
heap/equality/ownership Factor to prove current must-uniqueness. Without that
proof the owning Rule performs the lawful weak update. The engine never
guesses and Program never acts as an alias oracle. Reading an unstored key logs
the Factor default plus the storage presence/version, so a first write
invalidates the reader.

The Rule is invoked separately for each nonempty exact region of its complete
declared read projection. Its private typed read interface contains only the
resolved dependency units from that one Product result and never a per-Factor
join across Program-Guard alternatives.
The Product callback reports protocol success, not semantic feasibility:
`false` aborts the complete transfer. Every visited Factor-output row must
instead settle exactly once as either a staged typed value or an explicit
`NoOutput`. A staged value resolves at least one presealed write target;
`NoOutput` asserts that this Rule contributes no successor for that exact
row and emits no Factor patch. It is not encoded as the Factor's sparse
`Default`, which is an ordinary domain value and need not be lattice bottom.
Missing, duplicate, foreign, or stale row dispositions fail closed. The local
derivation checker receives one row-indexed disposition vector and may read
the exact same-row operands for either kind, so omission cannot underapproximate
silently; trusted theorem admission remains an explicit trusted-base choice.
This preserves correlation already represented by the shared Guard support and
inside each opaque Factor value. It does not invent a correspondence between
independent hidden alternatives in two Factors. If confluence loses a
route/outcome/effect relation that a concrete consumer needs, the loss is
marked and the proof is withheld until the owning domain supplies the smallest
justified relation Factor or reduction.

Rule application is deterministic and pure with respect to semantics. Reusable
scratch and memo tables may change operational state, but a Rule may not:

- scan a global Program relation;
- mutate semantic state outside its declared typed writes;
- call the Solver recursively;
- decode generic codes or strings;
- decide a sibling domain's judgment;
- use a Query.

A Rule may declare a dependency on another Program body after a call-domain
Rule proves a candidate. This is a generic equation dependency, not call
semantics in the engine. Entry and outcome-exit transport use domain boundary
Rules over the Program correspondence.

### Solver

The Solver compiles each Program body and its Rule instances once into an
entry-independent guarded equation system over the same coordinates/carrier used by
the whole analysis:

```text
E_body(formals, captures, imports, rep(ModuleCacheInstance), type/effect variables)
  → the exact outcome coordinates declared for this body by Program
```

This equation system is the reusable symbolic library artifact. It retains
formal boundary variables and exact recursive dependencies; it is not a
pre-solved exit tuple and not a universal closed relation.

`S#` is not an additional transformer carrier, summary Factor, persistence
section, or pre-solved exit tuple. For a non-recursive body, or the smallest
internally recursive body/module component derived by the equation compiler,
it is notation for that same normalized guarded Factor result with its
canonical boundary formals left free. Its universal meaning is the ordinary
universal concretization of `E#_body` over those formals, not a separately
stored function value. Every use is the existing domain boundary substitution
and ordinary Rule composition:

```text
Enterβ ; Freshen(S#) ; Exitβ,outcome
```

`β` is relational, may bind two formals to the same actual, and preserves the
complete alias/root-role shape. `Freshen` alpha-renames only body-local nominal
binders into the existing structural application coordinate; it never renames
or copies globals, modules, provider roots, retained callbacks, or caller heap
objects. Every Factor whose conclusion can enter a certificate supplies this
free-formal abstraction and boundary substitution soundly; if it cannot retain
the needed relation, it contributes its declared Top/unknown and precision-loss
fact instead of creating a symbolic-summary carrier. The boundary includes only
the Program/Link-observable Packs, captures, live Cells, reachable heap and
provider/Target state roots, `rep(ModuleCacheInstance)`, effect/type variables,
callback/module parameters, continuation state, and outcomes required by the
installed Factors. It is not a caller heap copy or fixed mega-summary payload.

Every reusable body has a domain-proved `FrameAccessClosure`: the least
interprocedural closure of roots read, written, identity/alias-tested, used for
dispatch/metatable decisions, exposed to callbacks/providers/resume, or named
by live Guard parameters. A Rule that can touch such state declares, through
its owning Factor, a pure versioned projection from its typed key/boundary
operands to existing Program/Link formal root roles and any sealed ambient
provider/Target state dependency. The equation compiler unions those opaque declarations and
follows the active boundary/candidate graph; it never interprets a domain key
as a root. Each owning domain proves that its projection frames every other
key. Its executable contract names `MayRead_r` and `MayWrite_r` and proves that
for every concrete transition `(s,t)` of Rule `r`, each root outside
`MayWrite_r(s)` is unchanged in `t`. Dynamic aliases/keys, metatable dispatch,
callbacks, and opaque providers include their reachable/ambient provider/Target state root
until a domain theorem narrows it. An opaque or absent projection forces
reevaluation and cannot authorize a dependency-minimal hit.

`FrameAccessClosure` is derived cache metadata, not a Factor, summary,
persisted semantic plane, or alternate analysis. Its frame-preservation theorem
preserves everything outside the proved set. The cache input is smaller than
the closure only when the body equation provably does not read a field; it still retains symbolic
write-target mapping and every alias relation needed for outcome transport. A
structural revision invalidates and rederives the metadata.

The reusable proof is universal local simulation plus the component's checked
post-fixpoint. If the selected domain cannot encode the needed Pack, alias,
heap, callback, or typestate relation, no solved result is cached: compiled
equations may still be reused and the relevant equations run again. Recursion
crossing the formal component participates in the caller's ordinary equation
SCC. This is honest recomputation, not a fallback semantic path.

The boundary alias/root-role key is exactly a canonical finite equivalence
partition of the declared formal roots with their existing Program/Link and
domain role tags. It contains no reachable heap shape. Deeper heap, typestate,
Value, and provider facts remain ordinary typed Factor inputs.

An optional solve-session-owned operational specialization cache is keyed by the
canonical body/component and formal boundary schema, exact dependency-minimal
Target/provider/import/relation-family projection, that canonical boundary
partition, live Guard interface, read-token projection, and the
semantic domain/Rule/widening versions. Only the canonical evaluator may insert
its deterministic result; open/completeness remains an ordinary call-Factor
conclusion with checked evidence tied to the fixed family schema, exact
accepted-relation-set digest, and all
predicate/read dependencies, never a cached status bit. There is no decoder or
externally supplied Factor witness. This cache is an input-specific operational
evaluation of `E_body`, never `S#` or a second bottom-up summary. A hit
revalidates every dependency and exact input projection before reusing that
same immutable result. It survives equation-epoch rebuilds inside that one
session and is discarded with the session's accepted tuples and evidence; it
is never a cross-session seed. Caller IDs, call strings, active schedules,
solve epochs, concrete allocations,
`OperationalEffects`, and path summaries never enter its identity. It is not a
Program artifact, portable summary, or second semantic path.

At a call boundary the Solver:

1. resolves the selected body through domain Rules;
2. runs joint domain boundary Rules over the Program correspondence;
3. validates a cached formal specialization against only the boundary reads that the
   body actually observed;
4. reuses the result when that read projection is identical;
5. otherwise evaluates only invalidated equations and records their exact read
   projection;
6. runs the corresponding guarded outcome Rules back into the caller.

Outcome application obeys the interprocedural valid-path law: a body outcome
is instantiated only through the same Program call/resume/handler
correspondence that supplied that formal application. Equal callers may share
the symbolic computation, but their outcome substitutions and successor
locations never join through a global callee-exit fact. Tail calls carry no
return correspondence; throw/yield/cancel/resume outcomes use only their
Program-declared handler/resumer correspondence. Recursive summaries therefore
match calls and returns without a call-string coordinate, reconstructed CFG,
or unbounded context identity.

Boundary transport is lazy and memoized by the joint input roots, Program
correspondence, formal Guard, and structural activation-renaming law. Body-local keys are not
eagerly copied through a deep call chain. Interned composed renamings are
path-compressed to direct dense translations on first demand, and only demanded
Factor keys/results are materialized. Reusing a child therefore neither walks
its internal points nor replays the caller chain.

Before cache lookup, domain boundary Rules normalize actual identities to the
child's canonical formals while retaining the exact alias/equality pattern and
externally observable identities. Isomorphic caller boundaries may share the
formal computation; outcome Rules rename mutations/results back to their own
actual objects. Non-isomorphic alias, heap, effect, type, or ownership inputs
remain distinct projections.

Closed sub-equations are partially evaluated once. Equal callers share one
formal specialization even at different call sites. The result contains
symbolic body-local allocations, mutable cells, closure environments, and
continuation-resident cells. Abstract activation is the canonical structural
tuple `(kind, Program creation occurrence, structural invocation coordinate)`.
An acyclic sibling call has a distinct coordinate; recurrent execution
revisits the same stable creation/invocation tuple while the active equation
SCC carries `Mu`. This normalization happens before Guard
substitution, boundary-key translation, cache identity, allocation identity,
or any Factor key. It has no capacity policy and cannot mint one identity per
dynamic iteration. Program's activation relation determines which structural
identity is created or restored:

Invocation-local Cell storage is scoped to the current formal body
application, not interned as one global `(Cell, I)` heap location. A recursive
call boundary transports the caller's live locals as suspended continuation
formals and alpha-renames the callee's current locals; the return boundary
restores the former. Those typed boundary correspondences prevent current-frame
locals from aliasing suspended ancestor frames. The equation `Mu` only solves
the resulting recursive equations. Only captured or otherwise reified
Cells enter an environment/heap identity governed by alias and uniqueness
Factors.

- a new ordinary/direct/callback/closure invocation creates its structural
  call activation; closure creation separately creates its structural captured
  environment; coroutine creation/first entry and first module initialization
  create their corresponding structural activation;
- a closure invocation retains the environment captured by that closure while
  receiving fresh invocation-local cells;
- coroutine resume and handler re-entry restore the continuation's existing
  activation instead of fabricating a new one. Their boundary Rule is a typed
  two-input merge: continuation-resident Cells, captures, body-local Values,
  and live local Guard parameters come from the suspended continuation;
  globals, shared/actor heap, module/provider state, and other
  provider/Target-declared state-owning Factors
  come from the resumer's current leaf. The Program continuation relation
  declares this ownership per subject, and the two Guard namespaces compose
  by the same alpha-renaming/correlation laws as calls. No stale resumer heap is
  restored and no continuation-local correlation is dropped;
- module cache identity ensures a successful initialization is not repeated;
  the project Link has already rejected every import cycle.

Aliases within one abstract activation are preserved. Repeated execution at a
recurrent site revisits the same structural coordinate and its owning Factors
join/widen at `Mu`; therefore it cannot allocate an unbounded sequence of Guard
atoms, renaming maps, Factor keys, or cache identities. Because that coordinate
may denote multiple concrete activations, singleton/must privileges require a
separate owning-domain proof and are revoked when the proof no longer holds.

A caller difference that no Rule reads is absent from the cache key. A
semantically relevant callback, reachable boundary, type argument, effect row,
or heap/context abstraction is present, so precision is never traded for an
unsound cache hit.

Recursive direct, mutual, higher-order, and callback dependencies enter the
Solver's finite structural invocation graph. Dynamic relation installation can
add only endpoints resolved through the sealed Program/Link. An accepted
insertion recompiles and restarts the complete disposable equation epoch from
canonical `Init` and current external-boundary contributions. A prior widened
Factor or Guard value never seeds the new widening; independently validated
pure transfer caches may be reused.
The recurrence interface is never derived from component membership: reset
decisions and live alpha-correspondence come only from the exact presealed
Program/Link boundary crossed by each transfer. There is no Go recursive
evaluation and no depth cutoff.

Argument, heap, and domain values never create, rename, or alter an invocation
coordinate. Through its declared typed trigger Rule, such a value may prove and
admit an eligible relation tuple over sealed constituents. Every accepted structural coordinate
has one boundary equation whose incoming guarded Factors are joined by the
ordinary lattice laws. Context precision lives in that joint guarded state and
in domain abstractions. Program/Link boundaries determine transport only. Each
Factor widens its own
plane pointwise at the compiled equation `Mu`; no heterogeneous tuple or
Product has a widening operator. Read-projected specialization is only a
memoized evaluation of the same symbolic body equation. It may share work for
equal inputs but cannot merge, split, admit, or redirect semantic coordinates.
Demand closure follows every structural incoming dependency of a reached
coordinate; a newly discovered dynamic edge invalidates and extends that
equation before publication.

A dynamic dispatch set is open until completeness is proven. An open set
always contributes the typed opaque boundary—unknown effects, escape,
mutation, typestate, placement and outcomes—so no negative/must proof can be
published prematurely.

Structurally complete calls use their closed transfer from the first
iteration. Completeness or must-alias discovered by analysis is a separate
must-proof and cannot remove opaque/weak may facts during the ascending phase.
After the active equation solution stabilizes, that proof may trigger a
dependency-tracked reduction/narrowing phase whose owning operators have a
well-founded termination proof. It reevaluates every affected boundary,
update, conclusion, and evidence coordinate to stability and rechecks a
post-fixpoint. Discovery of a new eligible relation tuple or alias revokes the proof,
invalidates certificates, restores the sound open/weak contribution, and
restarts the canonical equation epoch. No definitive proof is
published before the final stable epoch. If no termination-proven refinement
exists, the sound open/weak result remains; the engine never forces
completeness to terminate.

The phase sequence has a lexicographic termination measure, not a retry limit.
Let `U` be the implicit finite set denoted by the fixed family constructors over
sealed constituents and `E` the monotonically growing accepted-relation set of
the current solve session. `U` is mathematical only and is never enumerated or
retained. The outer component is the strictly shrinking finite complement
`U \ E`. A relation insertion cancels descent, revokes every
dependent must-proof/certificate, restores sound ascending contributions, and
rebuilds the deterministic equation epoch. With the edge set fixed, each condensation component reaches
its widening-stable post-fixpoint in topological order. Only then may its
dependency-tracked reduction/narrowing closure descend under the combined
well-founded ranks declared by the owning domains; proof additions and every
downstream consequence are included in that rank. Revocation invalidates the
entire dependent descent closure rather than leaving a locally refined result.
A whole-epoch post-fixpoint verification succeeds before any exact diagnostic
or certificate publishes. Guarded infeasibility may hide an edge contribution
but cannot delete its dependency, so restoration never requires rediscovery
and cannot oscillate.

Completion is guaranteed only for a sealed input/structural snapshot after a
finite revision sequence. A nonquiescent external environment may cause
restart, cancellation, or incomplete status, never a completed State; this
introduces no retry bound, iteration fuel, or reduced-precision result.

### Canonical Solver composition identity

Every Factor entering `Solver.Seal` supplies an available `SemanticKey` whose
version covers its complete key space, default, lattice, normalization,
fingerprint, widening/narrowing, and rank semantics. Producer identities are
unique in one composition. Seal sorts the complete Factor declarations by
their semantic identities and only then assigns dense physical Facts slots in
one transactional candidate; a failed seal publishes no slot assignment.
Factor declaration order, package initialization/registration order, Go
pointer identity, and a prior process-local slot are never semantic, cache, or
scheduling identity.

Every unanchored Rule schema entering `Solver.Seal` likewise supplies one
available Rule `SemanticKey`. Seal canonicalizes that schema vector before any
Program/Link anchor expansion and rejects duplicate Rule `SemanticKey`
declarations or collisions rather than coalescing or choosing by registration
order. A single admitted schema may still produce any finite number of anchored
Rule instances; their separate legality and uniqueness use the full
Rule-instance identity below.

Every Rule instance has exactly one canonical output disposition.
`FactorOutput(Factor)` permits at most one typed output-plane patch; every exact
write, staged write target, summary capability, and Carry in that instance
belongs to that Factor, and the Rule cannot prune support or activate a
relation. `StructuralOutput` permits no Factor write, staged target, summary,
or Carry and declares at least one exact support-prune or activation
capability. Its canonical identity consists of its available Rule
`SemanticKey`, output disposition, exact Program/Link anchor, canonical typed
read schema, and its conditional write-target or structural-capability schema.
Seal applies that
same collision law to execution and persistence, rejects collisions after one canonical
`O(R log R)` sort, and uses the full Rule-instance identity—not output-Factor
slot or declaration order—to order otherwise unordered private Group members.
Only canonical Point identity breaks WTO ties.
Queries likewise carry canonical semantic/versioned projection identities and
never obtain result-layout identity from declaration order.

Seal derives one whole-Solver schema-level `CompositionID` from the canonical Factor vector;
the canonical unanchored Rule-schema vector (each Rule `SemanticKey`, output
disposition, typed read schema, and conditional write-target DAG/coverage law
and Carry or support-prune/activation-family capability declarations, but no
resolved dependency unit or Program/Link anchor);
engine/compiler/widening schema versions; and the
independently rederived cold Factor-incidence and canonically encoded
SCC-condensation report. The report order is only the canonical order of the
reported Factor/component identities; it is not a WTO or execution order. The
identity contains no body/component Rule-instance vector,
dynamic relation, executable schedule, or SCC head. A Wave-E template instead
