names only its exact engine equation-schema and locally rederived anchored
Rule-instance, Factor, boundary, and applicability closure.
`CompositionID` is the complete Solver/State schema identity and a digest
projection of those existing authorities, never an authored manifest, second
Rule registry, persisted schedule, SCC head, or executable closure. A successful seal also freezes one
read-only admission index for all subsequent artifact/cache admission and
emission; cache count or shard count cannot multiply scans of the Factor,
Rule, or Link declarations.

All cross-session reusable semantic cache records obey one dependency-minimal
validity law and are limited to results whose complete observed relation set is
sealed static Program/Link/Target structure. A result that observes a
solve-session-admitted tuple is eligible only for the session-owned operational
cache above. Its identity contains every applicable producer and
Factor/Rule/widening/proof version, Target contract, exact
boundary/provider/module dependencies, and structural revisions. A shard
record carries its immutable shard/Term namespace. A coordinate-local Rule
transfer additionally carries its exact occurrence/boundary coordinate and
normalized Guard/substitution/read projection and resolved staged write-target
projection.

A cross-session body specialization refines, and never replaces, that universal
validity law only when its observed relation closure is wholly sealed static
structure. Its identity carries the canonical child body/formal interface,
that template's exact engine equation-schema and complete anchored Rule-instance,
Factor-incidence, boundary, and applicability closure within its owning shard
and retained Term namespace, exact observed Target/provider/import/static
relation-family schemas and structural revisions, alias/freshness pattern,
read and staged write-target projections, and widening semantics. Observing a
dynamic tuple makes the result session-local instead; equality of a prior
accepted-set digest cannot authorize replay. An
unrelated change in a different shard, or in a dependency outside the
template's closure, leaves its canonical identity and bytes unchanged; a change
to its owning shard or Term namespace does not. A whole-Solver `CompositionID`
mismatch triggers exact local rederivation; reuse is legal only when that
owning-shard/Term-namespace precondition and the closure and applicability
coverage match, otherwise it is an ordinary dependency-minimal miss. The exact
imported-boundary
shard digests it observed replace only irrelevant components of the
whole-project Link, never those dependencies.
The caller's application occurrence exists only in `Enterβ` and
`Exitβ,outcome`; it is not a specialization key. Any
mismatch misses before it can affect scheduling, State, diagnostics,
certificates, or publication; a dependency may be omitted only under a tested
independence law. Eviction/rebuild changes work only and never remaps handles.

### Scheduling and publication

The cold executable topology is stored as dense canonical tables, not action
objects: Point-to-producer Group CSR, Point-to-consumer Group CSR, ordered
Group-input/reindex rows, and Group-member ranges. The point-only WTO is derived
from those tables in linear time after canonical sorting. `RuleAction`,
`CommitAction`, `PublishAction`, staged Rule Points, action candidates, and
action Query cones do not exist in the live model.

The work queue contains Points only and is a preallocated deduplicating queue.
Every clean-to-dirty Group transition atomically marks its output Point dirty
and enqueues that Point exactly once for the current queue generation. A dirty
Point visits only its dirty producer Groups; no wake scans Points or producer
tables to rediscover work. Visiting it reevaluates those Groups privately,
installs only complete candidates, folds the Point's exact RHS once, applies
the Point's current WTO phase, and publishes at most once. Group and Point
dirty bits/generations are derived epoch-local scheduling cache, never semantic
state or persistence identity. Parallel member or Group evaluation is
permitted only behind that atomic cut; it cannot publish, route, own a WTO
event, or expose a partial candidate.

Each Group has one disposable candidate cache keyed by structural revision,
activation revision, ordered input structural/support versions, exact dynamic
read versions, reindex/boundary identities, and Rule/Factor schema versions.
The cached value is only that Group's support plus declared sparse
write/carry contribution and member read traces. It is never Point state,
evidence, Query truth, or a persisted authority. Replacing a candidate first
removes its old reverse-read rows and invalidates its old contribution.
Within one fixed ascending epoch, monotonicity requires every recomputed Group
contribution to be semantically above its prior contribution. A weaker result
is a Rule/carrier law failure and aborts publication; legitimate decreases occur
only in the separately ranked narrowing phase or after a structural/interface
revision has discarded the old epoch.

A Point is the sole publisher. Its exact current RHS is the canonical join of
`Init` and every currently valid producer contribution. At an acyclic or
nonhead Point that RHS is published exactly; a cyclic head applies only the
localized widening/narrowing protocol above before publication. Because join
is not invertible, candidate replacement or retraction never joins onto the old
RHS or Point. It either refolds all producers in canonical order or updates a canonical
persistent balanced reduction tree over producer contributions. That tree is
derived cache state only and cannot appear in the semantic graph. The Point
then emits one exact `ChangeSet`, advances one version, and routes only to
reverse-indexed consumer Groups.
Every structural, input, support, read, and Point-publication version token is
non-aliasing for the lifetime of its solve epoch. Fixed-width tokens never
silently wrap: before a token could be reused, the entire epoch candidate,
dirty, reverse-log, and fold cache is discarded and rebuilt under a fresh
non-equal epoch identity.

- A changed Factor key wakes only Rule instances that actually read that key
  under a compatible Guard, but the wake dirties and reevaluates the complete
  owning Group against one coherent snapshot.
- An added or removed outer-support region wakes every applicable structural
  transfer and Rule at that coordinate under the exact region, including a
  no-read or Carry-only Rule. A removed region retracts or recomputes its prior
  guarded output before any successor result is reused; neither path is encoded
  as a synthetic Factor-key change.
- A changed decision wakes only readers of that decision.
- A split, alpha-renaming, or canonical rewrite of the joint Guard cover wakes
  correlation-sensitive readers even when each projected Factor value is
  unchanged.
- A cached Rule result is validated by the universal semantic identity above
  plus its recorded read projection.
- Unchanged re-solve performs zero transfers and zero allocations.
- The worklist carries compact joint-state handles, not one interface value per
  Factor.
- Persistent structures share unchanged roots.

Reverse subscriber buckets have deterministic iteration without allocating
and sorting a fresh equation vector for each delta; transaction-owned reusable
scratch is permitted, but declaration order and map iteration are not. Routing
keys are qualified by input Point:
`(Point, Factor dependency unit, compatible Guard) -> Group`, with separate
Point-qualified support/Carry and decision/selector buckets. Dynamic read-log
replacement unlinks the old rows before linking the new rows; stale historical
subscribers are never retained.
Routing
one change is `O(changed Factor/key/Guard units + compatible readers + changed
support regions/compatible consumer Groups)` and never scans all Factors,
Rules, modules, bodies, or Queries. Replacing one equation's read log removes
and adds its old/new units and computes all
per-source support unions in one grouped pass, `O(old reads + new reads)` plus
the required Guard unions; it never rescans the complete projection once per
touched source. These routing costs exclude the separately measured `O(F)`
copy/join/widen work of constructing a new immutable correlated Facts root.

Every carrier update produces its exact semantic change set while it performs
the synchronized typed operation. The result separately names canonical
support-added and support-removed BDD regions, plus each changed plane,
dependency key or summary unit, and exact Guard region; equality confirmation
remains the owning Factor's authority. Publication consumes that change set
directly and does not rescan every plane and the union of every populated key
after the new root exists. A no-op update has neither a support nor plane delta.
Join/confluence and recurrence `Merge3Under` compose both plane and support
deltas under the same three-region algebra, and a debug law compares them
against an independent exhaustive delta only in tests.

An acceptance batch is only a structural transaction over separately
identified, independently owned Rule instances whose validated single-output
patches are ready at the same commit cut, with at most one ready patch per
output Factor. Each Factor-output Rule evaluation contributes at most one typed
plane patch for its named output Factor; no Rule writes or stages another
Factor. Structural-output members contribute only their declared prune result
or activation selection to `AtomicFinish` and never occupy a Factor patch slot.
A patch is
the owning Factor's typed update from that transaction's common predecessor
under the Rule's exact resolved target capability and output Guard, not a full
post-state plane. One Group admits at most one writer for a Factor, and that
owner Rule may update several correlated units of the Factor. Sequential
same-Factor updates occupy distinct structural Points/Groups in Program order;
alternative same-Factor producers occupy separate Groups and join only at the
common output Point. No scheduler order is semantic authority. The transaction
attaches its distinct-Factor patch handles in sealed Factor order and may copy
the flat outer vector once for the complete transaction. This physical batching
never merges Rule identity, reads, evidence, activation, scheduling, or
ownership. A typed patch returns its validated plane handle directly to the
transaction; it is not first attached to a temporary Facts root and then
extracted and `Put` again. Read-only and pruned Rule instances allocate no
output vector or patch map.

When one Program/Link structural row declares an atomic successor requiring
several distinct-Factor owner patches, every participating Rule evaluates only
against the same committed predecessor carrier and that row's presealed
structural operands. The acceptance transaction validates the complete
declared patch set and common predecessor before attachment; no Rule can read a
sibling patch or an intermediate carrier. This is commit atomicity over
separate single-output Rules, not a staged evaluator or multi-output Rule.

Within one uncommitted Rule evaluation, read logs use a deduplicated append
buffer of fixed-width interned tokens. Successful commit atomically replaces
that equation's complete prior read projection and reverse edges; reads that
disappear after reevaluation, narrowing, or revocation are not retained. Warm
validation hashes immutable Factor-root/key versions and allocates no object
per read. Cache/evidence retention is epoch-bounded: active specializations pin shared roots,
while unreachable traces and proof-term nodes are reclaimed without changing
semantic identity.

For one sealed Program/Link, root set, and Solver composition, there is one
canonical analyzer route and one opaque immutable State. State contains only
the registered Query projections, their evidence/precision metadata, and
provenance. It contains no joint guarded Factor-carrier root, internal
coordinate root, schedule, read log, or body specialization. Diagnostics,
placement, provider facts, and consumer certificates are registered Queries of
that same State; no compiler, linter, placement, or diagnostic profile may own
a separate Solver, State, fact projection, or semantic pipeline. Solver-bound
process-resident operational caches own any immutable roots needed for reuse
under their separate validity law; they never publish them through State. This
plan does not persist or decode a State, a Query result, a dense result slot, a
Solver-owner pointer, or an erased typed value across processes.

There is no cross-session State-result cache. A completed immutable State is
the publication of exactly one solve session and carries its owning Solver,
`CompositionID`, accepted-relation-set digest, Program/Link content, root
`Init`/boundary-input and external-contribution revisions, observation schema,
proof/evidence-checker version, and Query-projection versions only as
provenance. Query projections may memoize inside that State; they cannot seed a
new solve. A new session independently admits its dynamic tuples and may reuse
only independently valid static Program/Link artifacts, equation templates,
and cross-session cache records satisfying the static-observation law above.
Old State roots, tuple evidence, and accepted-set digests never cross that
boundary.

Each Factor Query projection identity combines its available semantic/version
key with the observed Factor semantic identity, exact semantic Program/Link
occurrence or outcome, exact Factor key or finer dependency unit, and declared
precision/evidence mode. A structural support-completion projection instead
combines its Solver-owned projection version, exact occurrence/outcome
selector, and reachability-evidence mode; it contains no Factor identity or
key. Solver seal rejects duplicate projection identities and orders Query roots
and same-coordinate result slots by that canonical identity. Query declaration
or package registration order therefore changes neither the Query-set identity
nor State layout. State retains the resulting compact values only; the exact
typed Query capability remains the sole reader, and a foreign Solver owner is
rejected.

The external-contribution/observation revision in this validity check licenses
only operational State-bound reuse. It neither discharges a semantic premise
about mutable provider/resumer state nor becomes a runtime condition on a
future-consumer certificate.

Query roots are declared before Solve as either an exact queried Factor/key (or
finer dependency unit) plus Program occurrence/body/outcome selector, or the
registered structural support-completion selector alone; both declare output
family and precision/evidence mode. For a Factor root, Solver begins with that
exact unit and structural root, then computes the finite backward
Rule/unit/body dependency closure from each demanded output through only the
sealed Rules whose exact write/carry or staged write-target surface can cover
it, those Rules' declared reads, write-target predecessors, and support-prune
effects, and the required structural predecessors. For
a support-completion root, Solver begins at the exact structural coordinate and
closes every structural incoming predecessor plus every support-prune Rule and
its declared reads that can remove that coordinate's support; it publishes the
two-valued projection only after that closure completes. Factors and Rules
disconnected from the selected closure neither execute nor acquire
widening/narrowing rank obligations. Reaching a Point includes every producer
Group, every ordered Point input of those Groups, and every support-prune
dependency. If any reached Point lies in a cyclic Point component, the closure
includes the complete component and its nested WTO regions; a demanded fragment
may not select a different head or widening history from full execution.
Before deciding demanded membership,
Solver also includes, for every declared lazy inactive relation family incident
through its indexed application/endpoint range, the family's declared reverse
activation-trigger/read-schema closure.
Those trigger coordinates run as ordinary typed Rules but do not enumerate or
contribute a relation transfer until activation proves an individual tuple.
When a rooted slice's declared trigger Rule selects a dynamic call/resumption
edge, membership grows monotonically through its canonical tuple and is finite
because both the implicit constructor product and every staged read/write-target
DAG are finite; the opaque boundary is seeded before the new coordinates run.
Trigger values cannot invent constituents or portable identity. An undemanded coordinate is operationally uncomputed, not
semantic top/default. A Query not declared before Solver seal cannot read that
State and never performs hidden solving. Multiple registered Queries over one
State perform no solving. The complete oracle registers every
diagnostic/native observation root before sealing. Post-publication root
growth and a separate `Extend`/`Ask` lifecycle are deliberately absent; a
different root set uses a new sealed Solver over the same reusable Program/body
artifacts.

This static closure selects the equations admitted to the sole scheduler; it
does not replace or coarsen the epoch-private exact
`(coordinate, Factor, dependency unit, Guard)` observation index that performs
runtime invalidation. There is one schedule, one worklist, and one fixed-point
protocol for both initially rooted and later activated members.

Query may select, filter, order, and render converged facts. It may read a
declared Factor at a Program occurrence or body boundary, or render the
Solver-produced structural support-completion projection. It may not:

- scan all points to infer a missing result;
- compute a closure, reachability, ownership, placement, type, effect, or call
  judgment;
- parse strings or inspect Rule instances;
- call the Solver or mutate State.

If a result requires recursion, is consumed by another Rule or a correctness
certificate, or reconstructs a fact not present in State, it is a Factor/Rule
judgment, not a Query.

Reachability is the sole structural projection exception: Solver, not Query,
establishes completed nonempty/empty outer support and attaches the checked
structural/support-prune evidence. The projection is never available as a Rule
read, Factor default, or semantic premise.

Each semantic domain therefore writes typed violation/proof evidence at the
occurrence where its judgment is made. A diagnostic Query does not compare
types, rediscover an unsafe use, or infer trust; it renders and applies advisory
publication policy to those converged domain facts.

## Domain ownership

Domains declare Factors first; then primitive and relational-reduction Rules
are bound. A reduction may read any number of Factors from the same exact terminal tuple
but writes only the one Factor that owns its conclusion. There is no fixed
semantic import ladder.

This is the complete extension boundary. A domain supplies its typed Factor
key/value/default, order/join/widening, any rank required when it is a
member of a derived cyclic Point component's recurrence closure, deterministic normalization,
equality/hash, and concretization law; its typed Rules and reductions; and its
pure Queries/evidence checker. A codec exists only for a domain-owned datum
that an explicitly authorized persistence section actually stores; portable
Factor values are not such a section. It
does not modify Solver, scheduling, Guard, State, recurrence, sparse storage,
publication, or engine key semantics. An n-ary relation remains opaque inside
the domain's Factor value. Adding a domain therefore requires no engine or
central-carrier branch. A proposed domain that needs a new engine lifecycle or
semantic switch is rejected unless it first proves a genuinely
domain-independent missing engine law.

There is exactly one semantic fixed point: Solver's Point equation over the
parallel Factor state vector. A computation is merely domain-local
normalization only when it consumes one immutable owner value and its sealed
schema, writes only that owner's representation, cannot observe Point, State,
Guard, equation topology, scheduling, or another Factor, is deterministic and
idempotent, and terminates by a finite carrier or a strict declared
well-founded reduction rank. Finite numeric closure, canonical antichain
reduction, and a reduced-product axis work queue are lawful only under that
contract. In particular, an axis reducer may narrow lanes inside one Value
product but may not read another Factor or propagate facts between Program
points. Any computation outside this boundary is a semantic transfer and must
be an ordinary typed Rule in the one Point graph; no domain-local solver,
summary worklist, alternate Guard/State, or hidden semantic cache is admitted.

### Boundary ownership ledger

The Module domain owns the Module Factor and the full Factor/boundary contract
specified for it above. Target owns the frozen module law, and Link owns its
sealed module/instance key and structural transition correspondence; neither
can introduce a second module-state carrier.

`Capability(value, capability)` belongs exclusively to the Value/identity
Factor. That Factor seeds, aliases, stores, transports, loses, and reads it
under the sealed capability-transfer rows. Host selector Rules consume the
relation but never create a capability Factor or provenance plane.

Every mutable provider or resumer root is named by its provider/Target contract
together with one owning domain Factor. That Factor supplies the full
key/value/default, abstraction/concretization, normalization, join/widening,
read/write, invalidation, and boundary-transport laws. There is no generic
`World` Factor or unowned ambient-world semantic: a contract row that reads or
changes such a root names its owning Factor and exact boundary role.

### Exclusive boundary and lifetime responsibility ledger

The following conclusions are exclusive; a Rule may read another owner but may
not duplicate or infer its coordinate in a second carrier:

- **Escape** alone answers whether a root or alias may cross, be retained by,
  or be reachable from a capture, store, return, callback, module, global, or
  transfer boundary. It establishes neither an entitlement/duty nor a current
  residence or continuation generation.
- **Ownership** alone answers the normative owner/borrow/move/share/send and
  lifetime obligations attached to an already identified root/origin. It does
  not establish boundary reachability, retention/liveness, a continuation
  generation, resource protocol state, holder multiplicity, or cleanup duty.
- **Suspension** alone answers the liveness and consumption of a particular
  continuation generation and the Values/Cells/private roles retained by that
  generation. It does not establish a root's actor/module/global residence or
  a resource holder count/duty.
- **Residence** alone answers a root's actor-local/shared/module/global
  residence, structural retention, liveness, and last-use eligibility. It does
  not create an ownership obligation, prove boundary escape, or create/consume
  a continuation generation.
- **Typestate** alone answers resource protocol state, exact holder-origin
  multiplicity, and cleanup duty. It does not decide alias escape, ordinary
  root residence/liveness, or non-resource ownership obligations.

This ledger separates may reachability, duties, continuation generations,
root residence/liveness, and resource holders without adding a Factor, state
plane, scheduler, or Query-side closure.

### Canonical Factor inventory

The current canonical inventory contains **exactly 16 Factors**. A Factor is
one semantic authority with its own sparse map/default and laws; a Factor
family parameterized by sealed Program/Link keys still counts once. Provider or
resumer state does not add a seventeenth Factor: every such root belongs to one
of the owners below. Evidence is a non-semantic sidecar, and diagnostics and
placement are Queries over this inventory, not Factors, profiles, or pipelines.
The numbering is an inventory only. The stated work package is an
implementation grouping, never a semantic dependency order or permission to
seal a partial composition.

Structural reachability is not a Factor. It is one registered Solver
support-completion projection over an exact occurrence/outcome coordinate. For
a demanded coordinate, Solver closes its structural incoming-control and
boundary predecessors together with every Rule that can retain or prune its
support. After that closure reaches its completed post-fixpoint,
`reachable` means its shared outer Guard support is nonempty and `unreachable`
means that completed support is empty. The projection owns no lattice, sparse
map, key, Rule, incidence edge, or semantic read capability; a Query/evidence
consumer cannot compute it. The Solver emits the projection and its structural
support/prune evidence only after completing the registered root's closure.
Thus an undemanded coordinate remains operationally uncomputed rather than
silently unreachable.

1. **Value/identity.** It denotes runtime presence, kind, truthiness,
   nilability, finite reference/alias alternatives, and
   `Capability(value, capability)`. Its carrier is the normalized correlated
   Value product over sealed subjects, with the owning domain's finite
   alternatives, summary/widening rank, and sparse bottom default; its keys are
   existing Program Value/Cell/result subjects, never paths or fabricated
   identities. Literal, operation-result, read/write, alias, capture, and
   capability-transfer and two-input resume-value Rules write it after reading
   Program rows, Pack, Heap, Call, TypeValue, Module Ready-hit alternatives, the matching
   Suspension generation's retained Value/Cell role and private/shared
   classification, exact continuation/resumer Value/Cell inputs, and declared
   static/Target facts. The runtime-TypeValue comparison result Rule is the
   unique owner of its ordinary Boolean Value conclusion: it reads both exact
   TypeValue descriptor alternatives and reads Static type only for the
   structural subtype premise of `<`, `<=`, and parser-lowered `>=`; it writes
   no TypeValue, Static-type, or Numeric/equality conclusion. The
   require-result Rule reads the exact Module `Ready(Value subject)` alternative
   and writes the caller result as that same subject; Module never writes a
   Value conclusion. Boundary Rules preserve aliases, capabilities, fresh
   results, and Recent/Summary substitutions. Runtime-value and capability
   Queries/evidence consume it. Only its schema/seed authority persists in
   Program/Link; values and evidence are State-bound. Its implementation
   belongs to Wave-D work package 1.

2. **Static type.** It denotes authored type interpretation, generic bounds,
   subtype/refinement judgments, annotation validity, and asserting-return
   postconditions. Its carrier is the canonical
   `Closed | Symbolic | Invalid` type judgment with the finite symbolic
   recurrence boundary; its stable key is the sealed static reference,
   namespace content ID, normalized formal environment, operation, and Target
   static-law ID. Context-free static evaluation reads only the Program type
   forest and Link static namespace/ResolverRef and owns the derived
   `ContainedOperand` judgment above; its `typeof` branch returns either an
   exact static result/diagnostic, an exact symbolic static-unknown result, or
   the canonical symbolic handle for its one `RuntimeSubject` case. The sole
   state-dependent `typeof` Rule writes that handle's existing static-type
   result coordinate after reading only that case's exact operand Value/type
   coordinate. Annotation and normal-outcome assertion Rules consume the
   resulting Static-type judgment. Boundaries substitute only their existing
   type formals and asserting actuals; it has no nominal materialization. Type
   diagnostics and static evidence consume it. Program persists authored types
   and resolver authority, never this Factor value or a contained-operand
   result. Its implementation belongs to Wave-D work package 1.

3. **TypeValue.** It denotes runtime `LType` objects, reflected children,
   bound selector closures, iterator cursors, validation, and generic runtime
   construction. Its carrier is the finite correlated descriptor/atom powerset
   with constant Top and `Widen = Join`; its stable key is the existing abstract
   runtime root of an `LType` or produced closure. Its source-seed authority is
   only the existing binder-authorized Program TypeValue row; the Factor never
   resolves a spelling, repeats shadow/declaration lookup, or creates a seed
   from an ordinary Value. Selector Read/Call/Iter and Target
   fresh-result/capture Rules write it after reading Value/identity, Static
   type, Pack, and the exact access/call rows. Boundaries carry captured roots
   and apply ordinary fresh/Recent/Summary transport. The Value/identity-owned
   runtime-TypeValue comparison result Rule consumes its exact descriptor
   alternatives; reflection and validation Queries/evidence also consume it.
   Only the Program seed authority, its normalized
   seed, and ResolverRefs persist; atoms, cursors, and values do not. Its
   implementation belongs to Wave-D work package 1.

4. **Pack.** It denotes full ordered Lua result/argument lists, including nil
   slots, open tails, and sharing of one tail occurrence. Its carrier is the
   normalized finite antichain of `Closed`, `Open`, and `AnyPack` cases with the
   declared class/rank widening; its keys are the sealed body/boundary/outcome
   Pack schemas and existing ports. Adjustment, bind, splice, take/drop,
   vararg, protected-outcome, and call/return Rules write it after reading
   Program Values rows and declared Numeric/Heap premises for dynamic access.
   Boundaries perform the exact non-injective Pack substitution and freshen only
   local bound tails. Pack Queries/evidence consume it. Schemas persist, not
   Factor values or caller substitutions; for a `typeof` class, only the
   Program/Link schema inputs persist and the handle and finite class entry are
   rederived under their domain identities. Its implementation belongs to
   Wave-D work package 1.

5. **Numeric/equality.** It denotes numeric eligibility, primitive/raw
   equality and disequality, integral equality, and fixed-template difference
   bounds. Its carrier is the normalized finite atom relation with threshold
   chains; `Join` is its widening and termination follows the sealed finite
   template universe. Its key is the sealed body/boundary root and its support
   is the declared atom/pair set. Coercion, arithmetic, comparison, equality,
   and key-normalization Rules write it after reading Value/identity, Pack, and
   Heap key observations. Boundaries reindex only the declared atoms and apply
   the same materialization substitution as other root-containing Factors.
   Numeric/equality diagnostics, loop facts, and evidence consume it. It is
   State-bound; thresholds/templates persist only as Program/Target schema. Its
   implementation belongs to Wave-D work package 1.

6. **Effect.** It denotes the may-set of sealed operation templates plus
   `UnknownExternal`, not control or lifecycle state. Its carrier is the finite
   powerset of the sealed template universe, with empty default and
   `Widen = Join`; its stable key is the sealed body/boundary root. Target
   operation, RowSpec, opaque-boundary, and effect-boundary Rules write it after
   reading Call completeness and the exact outcome/boundary correspondence.
   Boundaries reindex only the existing RowSpec formals/tail capability.
   Effect Queries and negative-effect evidence consume it. RowSpec/templates
   persist as Program/Link authority; may-sets do not. Its implementation
   belongs to Wave-D work package 1.

7. **Heap.** It denotes abstract allocation identity, slots, raw
   presence/payload, metatable routes, containment, shape eligibility, and
   shallow frozen state. Its carrier is the root-local symbolic-key partition
   with raw/count/Recent/Summary alternatives and its declared partition rank;
   its stable key is the structural allocation/aggregate root. Allocation,
   read/write, raw access, metatable, freeze, containment, materialization, and
   two-input resume-pullback Rules write it after reading Value/identity,
   Numeric/equality, Pack, Call, Ownership must-uniqueness, the matching Suspension private/shared
   classification, exact continuation/resumer root inputs, and exact Program
   access rows. Every boundary applies its
   declared identity pullback; recurring creation performs the authoritative Recent-to-Summary
   substitution. Heap/access/immutability Queries and evidence consume it.
   Only structural roots and schemas persist; heap values do not. Its
   implementation belongs to Wave-D work package 2.

8. **Call.** It denotes callee alternatives, dispatch completeness,
   epoch-local relation admission, and the required opaque boundary for an open
   set. Its carrier is the finite set of Program-body/Target-endpoint
   alternatives with open/complete status and an opaque alternative; stable
   keys are existing application/callback/resume coordinates, never argument
   contexts or materialized pair ranges. Callee-flow, endpoint-selector,
   metatable-call, and callback activation Rules write it after reading
   Value/identity (including Capability), Heap, TypeValue, Program Flow body
   relations, exact Link boundaries, and the virtual Target eligibility
   predicate. Its structural-output Rules admit the corresponding canonical
   relation tuple and preserve boundary/freshness; it owns no heap
   materialization. Call completeness Queries and evidence consume it. Only
   Program/Target/Link constituents persist; Call Factor values and accepted
   relation tuples do not. Its implementation belongs to Wave-D work package 2.

9. **Module.** It denotes the cache state of one
    `(actor, resolved module, canonical ModuleCacheInstance representative)`. Its
    carrier is the finite normalized may-relation over `Cold`,
    `Pending(Suspension generation subject)`, and `Ready(Value subject)`, with
    default `{Cold}` and `Widen = Join`; that pair is its stable key/support.
    The `ModuleInit` cache Rule writes it after reading the common predecessor
    Module Cold alternative and the correspondence's presealed fresh-generation
    and predecessor-materialization inputs; it never reads its sibling
    Suspension Rule's uncommitted output. Require, entry, completion, throw,
    yield, cancel, and resume Rules write it after reading Call, Pack,
    Value/identity, the matching already committed live Suspension generation
    where applicable, and the exact Target/module transition rows named by the
    boundary. Module boundaries retain the
    canonical instance and refer to,
    but never own, that Suspension generation; they never materialize a Heap
    Cell. Import/cache diagnostics and evidence consume it. The module schema
    and instance alias relation persist; state values do not. Its
    implementation belongs to Wave-D work package 3.

10. **Escape.** It denotes may containment/alias escape through capture,
    store, return, global, callback, module, and send boundaries. Its carrier
    is the domain's finite root-role relation plus declared summary/widening
    rank; its stable support is sealed actor-root and boundary-role pairs.
    Capture/store/return/send/callback and two-input resume-boundary Rules write
    it after reading Value/identity, Heap containment, Call/Module, the
    matching Suspension private/shared classification, exact
    continuation/resumer boundary inputs, and provider protocol rows.
    Boundaries carry only the declared escaping roots and apply Recent/Summary
    substitution. Escape/blocker Queries and evidence consume it. It is
    State-bound, has no portable Factor-value codec, and its implementation
    belongs to Wave-D work package 4.

11. **Ownership.** It denotes owner, borrow, move, share, send, and lifetime
    obligations only. Its carrier is the domain-owned finite/interval
    obligation relation with a termination-proven widening; stable keys are
    sealed actor root, origin, and obligation role. Owner/borrow/move/share/send,
    transfer, lifetime, publication, and two-input resume-duty Rules write it
    after reading Escape, Heap, Call/Module, the matching Suspension
    private/shared classification, exact continuation/resumer obligation inputs,
    and Typestate. Boundaries transport only the declared root/obligation roles
    and apply materialization substitutions.
    Ownership never owns resource holder counts, holder-origin multiplicity,
    or cleanup duty; those belong exclusively to Typestate. Ownership
    violations, eligibility facts, and evidence consume it. It is State-bound,
    and its implementation belongs to Wave-D work package 4.

12. **Transfer.** It denotes the Target-declared observable sender-side
    contents, aliases, isolation, success/failure, and non-local outcome of one
    publish/send/host-transfer occurrence. Its carrier is the finite sealed
    transfer-arm relation plus its explicit opaque arm, with union/rank supplied
    by the owning domain; its stable key is that existing transfer occurrence
    and arm. Transfer Rules write it after reading Heap, Value/identity, Escape,
    Ownership, Footprint, and the exact provider/Target transfer row. A
    cross-actor Send/Receive permits only a sealed immutable transferred graph
    and otherwise takes its declared failure/blocker; it materializes no
    receiver image, version, schedule, or physical policy.
    Transfer/placement-eligibility Queries and evidence consume it. It is
    State-bound, and its implementation belongs to Wave-D work package 4.

13. **Suspension.** It denotes retained continuation generations, pending
    Values/Cells, private-versus-shared root roles, and re-entry consumption.
    Its carrier is the finite continuation/protocol relation with a
    termination-proven role summary; stable keys are existing suspension,
    continuation, and resume/re-entry coordinates. `ModuleInit`, Yield, resume,
    cancel, handler, and provider re-entry Rules write it after reading Program
    continuation rows, the matching Module Cold/initiation input where
    applicable, Value/identity, Pack, Heap, Escape, Ownership, Typestate when a
    resource role is affected, and the exact provider/Target rows. It alone
    writes generation liveness/consumption and private/shared retained-root
    classification. Its
    two-input boundary transport restores continuation-private projections and
    reads current shared roots, applying
    the declared generation/materialization substitution. Suspension/lifetime
    Queries and evidence consume it. It is State-bound, and its implementation
    belongs to Wave-D work package 3.

14. **Residence.** It denotes actor-local/shared/module/global residence,
    retention, last-use eligibility, and lifetime survival. Its carrier is the
    domain-owned finite role relation with declared summary/widening rank; its
    stable keys are actor root and exact structural retention boundary. Escape,
    capture, store, callback, suspension, module, transfer, and two-input
    resume-residence Rules write it after reading Heap, Value/identity, Escape,
    Ownership, the matching Suspension private/shared classification, exact
    continuation/resumer residence inputs, and Transfer. Boundaries retain only
    the declared residence roles and apply Recent/Summary substitution.
    Residence and placement-eligibility Queries
    with evidence consume it. It is State-bound, and its implementation belongs
    to Wave-D work package 4.

15. **Footprint.** It denotes exact or abstract allocation/object-graph and
    element/capacity bounds, overflow, and uncertainty at an occurrence. Its
    carrier is the domain-owned symbolic bound/graph relation with a
    termination-proven widening; stable keys are sealed allocation roots and
    transfer/operation occurrences. Allocation, table/Pack construction,
    mutation, loop, and transfer Rules write it after reading Heap,
    Numeric/equality, Pack, Value/identity, and Transfer. Boundaries substitute
    structural roots and apply the same materialization law; no physical
    allocation policy is derived. Footprint and placement-eligibility Queries
    with evidence consume it. It is State-bound, and its implementation belongs
    to Wave-D work package 4.

16. **Typestate.** It is the sole owner of resource protocol state, resource
    holder counts/holder-origin multiplicity, and cleanup duty. Its carrier is
    the finite
    `protocol state × cleanup duty × holder-origin -> P({0,1,many})` relation,
    with finite-height join/widening; stable keys are finite structural resource
    origins. Acquisition, transition, retain/release, suspend/resume, cancel,
    and handoff Rules write it after reading Target protocol rows, Call/Module,
    Suspension, Ownership, and the exact provider/Target transition rows.
    Boundaries transfer exact holders, pending generations, and Recent/Summary
    resource roles. Cleanup/typestate diagnostics and evidence consume it.
    Protocol schemas persist; values and duties are State-bound. Its
    implementation belongs to Wave-D work package 3.

**Not Factors.** Structural reachability is the Solver support-completion
projection above; placement is a Query/certificate-facing eligibility fact
projected from Heap, Escape, Ownership, Transfer, Suspension, Residence,
Footprint, and Typestate; it never chooses stack/heap/COW/refcount policy and
has no placement Factor, placement Rule conclusion, or placement-specific
State. Every placement-facing operation-matrix output names the one constituent
Factor that writes it. The placement-certificate family is one explicitly
declared multi-Factor consumer/query child: it receives only the named owners'
typed read capabilities and completed State/evidence, and only projects those
already-converged facts. It owns no Factor, Rule, support, semantic conclusion,
or analysis route. Diagnostics are likewise Queries over converged owned
conclusions. `RowSpec`, capability, raw presence,
metatable dispatch, candidate completeness, module-cache states, continuation
roles, resource holders, Recent/Summary, and all Value axes are subrelations of
the numbered Factors, not additional Factors.

Current `analysis/domain` package layout does not alter this count.
`control/reachability` is retired from the Factor composition in favor of the
registered Solver support-completion projection; `value/product` and
`value/axis`, `type/factor`, `type/pack`/`type/table`, `effect/*`, and
`typestate/*` are respectively implementation material for the numbered
owners when they pass their Factor laws. `constraint/*`, `formal/*`, `path/*`,
and `placement/*` are not semantic authorities or extra key planes: a retained
helper must be hidden inside its owner's typed carrier, and otherwise is
quarantined as legacy. In particular, the current placement vocabulary cannot
become a placement Factor or separate analysis route.

### Pre-domain composition/API cut (D0)

Wave D opens with one cold composition cut before any production domain is
added. D0 changes neither the guarded sparse carrier nor Guard, State, Rule
semantics, Query publication, or Solver evaluation. It fixes only declaration
ownership, dependency direction, and the one-shot seal lifecycle needed to
compose the complete Factor schema without historical package coupling.

- An owner declaration constructs only its domain authorities and typed Factor
  carrier and returns a public typed capability for that exact declaration. It
  declares no Rule, Query, or evidence checker and imports neither a child nor
  a sibling semantic owner.
- A Rule child owns one writing judgment and accepts the output owner's typed
  capability and every typed read-owner capability explicitly. Each Query or
  evidence child owns one output family. A single-owner child accepts only that
  owner's typed read capability. A multi-Factor diagnostic/certificate family
  is declared by its own consumer/query child, its unique output owner but not
  a semantic domain: it receives only explicit typed read capabilities from
  its named Factor owners and completed State/evidence, and purely projects
  them. It cannot write a fact, schedule work, infer a closure, own a semantic
  conclusion, or become a composition-root helper. The composition root wires
  each such named child individually; no generic horizontal Query package is
  admitted. No child discovers a Factor, lattice, Program family, or dependency
  through a registry, string, reflection, `any`, ambient singleton, or
  package-init side effect.
- The sole composition root constructs a fresh Solver, declares every Factor
  carrier first, then invokes the complete Rule/reduction, Query, and evidence
  child declarations, and calls `Seal` exactly once. Any failed declaration or
  seal discards that entire Solver and every capability derived from it. The
  same Solver is never repaired, rolled back, or retried, and D0 introduces no
  rollback protocol, registration transaction, priority, or install registry.
- The composition root may additionally register the Solver's one structural
  support-completion projection for exact occurrence/outcome roots. It declares
  no Factor carrier, Rule, lattice, key, or semantic read capability; at seal
  the Solver verifies its structural-predecessor and support-prune closure
  inputs and supplies its evidence only after the demanded closure completes.
- Factor declaration creates a typed semantic identity, not a caller-chosen
  physical slot. At the seal cut the Solver validates the complete Factor
  semantic-key set for availability and uniqueness and derives its canonical
  slot order from that set. Package import order, declaration call order,
  inventory numbering, and child installation order cannot affect slots,
  scheduling, cache identity, or semantics.
- Program/Target/Link exposes sealed typed ranges for the exact structural row
  families consumed by children. A child consumes only its declared ranges; it
  never rescans all shards or reconstructs a Program view. One authoritative
  typed family disposition supplies Rule declaration and Query/evidence
  coverage, so literal, operation, boundary, or outcome family lists are not
  repeated in installers and admission checks.
- The obsolete ordered State-lane inventory is removed or quarantined outside
  every live import, generation, test, and gate path before Factor composition;
  it supplies neither Factor count, identity, slot order, nor wiring. The
  existing Value-axis registry remains a private implementation detail of the
  one Value owner and cannot admit another Factor or schedule cross-Factor
  closure. Every cross-Factor reduction is an ordinary typed Rule in the child
  of the Factor it writes.
- The current monolithic Type Factor is implementation material only. Its
  combined carrier and installer are decomposed among the declared Static type,
  Pack, and other unique owners and their children; wrapping its existing
  `Install` route cannot enter the complete composition.

These are behavioral dependency and lifecycle invariants, not prescribed
filenames, directories, package counts, interfaces, structural tests, adapters,
or authorization to edit a production entrypoint. Semantic and law tests prove
the resulting typed composition; they do not freeze its Go layout.

### Derived Factor incidence and closure

Wave D declares all 16 typed Factor carriers before it binds any production
domain composition. It then binds the complete Rule/reduction set and every
staged read/write-target DAG, derives one typed edge `A -> B` whenever a Rule
that writes or carries Factor `B` reads Factor `A`, derives each finite reverse
write-target surface for exact-unit demand, incorporates each Rule's declared
support-prune and activation-family effects, and computes the SCC condensation
with a canonical report order over Factor/component identities. This derivation
consumes only the sealed Rule schemas; runtime read/target logs refine unit
invalidation and cannot add an incidence edge or a demanded writer, and no
separately authored executable edge list or installation graph exists.
The canonical report order is a serialization/rendering rule only; it never
uses the Point scheduler or produces a schedule. The reviewed incidence and
complete Rule-schema/instance inventories below are independent validation
oracles: exact equality may reject the derived result but neither may supply an
executable edge. Incidence equality alone cannot prove Rule closure because two
Rules may induce the same Factor edge, so the complete Rule inventory and
coverage matrix are checked separately. A
component closes only as a whole after all of its carrier laws, Rules,
reductions, boundary transports, evidence checkers, Queries, and Program supply
rows pass. Inventory order and implementation work packages do not add edges
and cannot authorize partial semantic closure.

The derived Factor incidence and SCC-condensation report are cold
build/admission metadata only. They own no runtime queue, dirty bit, read log,
widening/narrowing step, compiled-equation head, Point/Group schedule order, or State field.
The executable Point/Group hypergraph independently instantiates the admitted
Rules at exact Program/Link coordinates and is the sole user of the generic
point-only SCC/WTO scheduler. Runtime observations, dirty Group caches, and the
one Point queue remain the only
value-change invalidation path. Factor metadata may validate that graph's
schema, but cannot filter its edges, stage Factor components as separate fixed
points, or impose `K0`/`K1`/`K2` execution phases.

For the current inventory, the following is the reviewed expected report and
composition-gate assertion over that derivation, never an input used to build
the graph or schedule:

- **Structural reachability is a Solver support-completion projection, not a
  Factor or SCC member.** Incoming Program/boundary structure and contradiction
  support-prunes determine the completed outer support; semantic Rules never
  read a reachability conclusion. Its registered root contributes structural
  predecessor/support-prune demand only and no Factor read-to-write edge.
- **`K0/module/lifecycle = {Value/identity, Static type, TypeValue, Pack,
  Numeric/equality, Heap, Call, Module, Escape, Ownership, Suspension,
  Typestate}`.** The original core remains cyclic: Value reads Pack/Heap/Call
  and reads TypeValue plus Static type for its owned runtime-TypeValue Boolean
  comparison result;
  Static reads Value; TypeValue reads Value/Static/Pack; Pack reads
  Numeric/Heap; Numeric reads Value/Pack/Heap; Heap reads
  Value/Numeric/Pack/Call/Ownership; and Call reads Value/identity, including Capability,
  plus Heap and TypeValue. The ready-hit Value Rule reads Module, giving
  `Module -> Value`, while Module reads Call/Pack/Value, giving `K0/core ->
  Module`. The exact `ModuleInit` Suspension Rule reads Module Cold
  (`Module -> Suspension`); both initiation Rules read only their common
  predecessor plus the structural presealed fresh-generation/materialization
  inputs. Later Pending completion/Yield/Cancel/resume Module Rules read the
  already committed matching Suspension generation (`Suspension -> Module`). Escape,
  Ownership, and Typestate read Module, and the lifecycle members read Escape.
  The two-input resume Rules additionally read Suspension classification in
  Value/identity, Heap, Escape, Ownership, and Residence; each writes only its
  own conclusion. Together with the existing Ownership/Suspension/Typestate
  cycle, these edges merge the former core, Module, Escape, and K1 into this
  one SCC. Typestate alone still owns resource holder counts and cleanup duty;
  Suspension alone owns generation lifecycle/classification, while Module owns
  only cache state correlated to it.
- **Self-incidence remains explicit in the exact Rule inventory.** Boundary or
  state-transport Rules require self-reads for Value/identity, TypeValue, Pack,
  Numeric/equality, Effect, Heap, Module, Escape, Ownership, Suspension,
  Residence, Footprint, and Typestate. These self-loops do not change the SCC
  condensation, but the composition gate must derive and validate them; the
  condensed report cannot stand in for exact Rule-schema coverage. Static type
  has no state self-edge. Call and Transfer gain a self-edge only if an exact
  admitted Rule schema declares one.
- **`Effect`** is a singleton component that reads
  `K0/module/lifecycle` through Call.
- **`K2 = {Transfer, Footprint}`.** It reads `K0/module/lifecycle`;
  Transfer reads Footprint and Footprint reads Transfer.
- **`Residence`** is a singleton component that reads
  `K0/module/lifecycle` and `K2`.

The condensation report has `K0/module/lifecycle` as the predecessor of Effect
and `K2`, with `K2` as the predecessor of Residence. It specifies no execution
or closure phase. Structural support completion is resolved through the
equation compiler's ordinary predecessor/support-prune closure, never as a
Factor phase. Any added or removed typed incidence recomputes this condensation
and its canonical report encoding; it is never forced into these historical
components or work packages. The exact sealed Rule inventory and its coverage
matrix catch a missing Rule; a hand-maintained incidence edge cannot compensate
for one.

### Factor extension admission

Adding a future Factor is a declarative change to the sealed composition, not
an engine feature. The proposal must name one new concrete semantic relation
and unique conclusion owner; prove it is not a subrelation of an existing
Factor; define typed stable keys/support, default, abstraction/concretization,
normalization, order/join, widening, any narrowing law, the ranks required only
when it is a member of a derived cyclic Point component's recurrence closure, and its
finite-support or stabilization law; and supply every writing/carrying Rule,
major typed reads,
staged read/write DAG, support-prune or activation-family declaration, reverse
invalidation closure, and Query/evidence consumers.

It must also name its exact Program/Target/Link operands, boundary transport,
freshness/materialization behavior, persistence disposition, and the expected
dependency impact for review. The composition
gate derives every incoming/outgoing incidence edge and the resulting
condensation change from the supplied sealed Rule schemas and reports any
component merge in canonical report order; the proposal does not author those
edges or a second schedule.
A work
package may be named only as a non-semantic implementation assignment; there
is no fixed dependency leaf. Its law suite proves the Factor and Rule laws
required by this plan, boundary substitution, invalidation, termination,
demanded/full equivalence where relevant, and no hidden Cartesian product or
allocation path. The composition may add only the declared Factor and Rules to
the existing carrier. It may not change Guard atoms or transport,
Solver scheduling/lifecycle, State shape/publication, Query lifecycle, or
introduce a generic World, adapter, profile, or second analysis pipeline. A
proposal requiring any of those changes is rejected unless a separately
authorized, domain-independent engine-law revision proves the missing generic
law first.

### Vertical domain composition

Domain composition is vertical by semantic owner, not a horizontal package
layer. An owner root exposes only its typed Factor carrier, stable key and
observations, normalization/lattice laws, and boundary/materialization law. It
never imports its Rule, Query, or evidence children or a sibling semantic
owner.

A child Rule package owns one named semantic judgment or boundary transfer. It
imports only the typed public APIs of the owners it reads and registers Rules
that write exclusively to its own owner Factor. A domain may split children by
judgment or boundary when that keeps their read schemas and laws small; no
child recreates a sibling lattice, Program view, or relation carrier.
Single-owner Query/evidence children only project or check that owner's
converged conclusions. A multi-Factor diagnostic/certificate family instead
has one dedicated consumer/query child as its output owner, not a semantic
owner: it imports only the explicit typed read capabilities of its named
owners, consumes only completed State/evidence, and only projects those facts.
It cannot write facts, schedule work, infer closure, add a domain judgment, or
serve as a generic horizontal Query package. The placement-certificate family
is one such child.

The one composition root imports the declared owners and child declaration/Rule
packages and lists their Factors and Rules under the D0 one-shot lifecycle. It
decides neither semantic dependencies, priorities, reductions, slot order, nor
evaluation order; sealed Factor identities and the sealed read/write graph with
the ordinary Solver SCC/WTO own those. The owner/Rule/Query/evidence package
directions are binding rows in the generated import-DAG ledger above; only
filenames remain unconstrained. They authorize no runtime registry, adapter,
or generic relation package, and are enforced by the non-test structural gate.

### Structural reachability projection

Reachability has no domain child or Factor. A registered Solver
support-completion root names one occurrence/outcome coordinate. Solver closes
that coordinate's incoming Program/boundary predecessors and every declared
support-prune dependency before projecting `reachable` from nonempty shared
Guard support or `unreachable` from completed empty support. A contradiction
Rule prunes its own successor support; it never writes a reachability datum.
Semantic Rules run only on nonempty support and cannot read the projection.
Dead-code diagnostics and reachability certificates render the registered
Solver result and its structural/prune evidence; they neither compute a control
closure nor acquire a Factor capability. The projection is unavailable for an
undemanded coordinate, so lack of work is never reported as unreachable.

### Value and static type

Program supplies literals, operations, declarations, annotations, type syntax,
value/list shape, bindings, and predicates. Domain Factors own runtime
possibilities, finite reference sets, truthiness, nilability, type
instantiation, subtype/refinement results, and typed evidence.

Static types are interpreted from the existing content-addressed Program type
forest; there is no second type graph, syntax copy, or generic solver. For the
immutable Link lexical namespace `N` at a term's binder scope and an
identity-keyed type-parameter environment `rho`, let `S` be the exact Target
static-type-law digest paired with the static-evaluator/schema digest. The one
evaluator has the judgment:

```text
[[term]]N,rho,S = Closed(T) | Symbolic(term,N.contentID,S,rho,reason) | Invalid(error)
```

`Closed(T)` denotes exactly the canonical closed type `T`. `Symbolic` denotes
all valid closed interpretations satisfying the residual Program type
equation, namespace, normalized environment, bounds, and the Target
static-type law selected by `S`. The residue may arise from an unresolved
parameter or from a closed changed-environment generative recurrence; it does
not require an unresolved formal and is not `any`, `unknown`, or `never`.
`Invalid` is a proved static-language failure and is not runtime Bottom.
Memoization is by `(static reference, N.contentID, normalized rho, operation,
S)`. Every structural and recursive evaluator subjudgment retains that same
`N` and `S`; active re-entry produces the existing recursive/symbolic backedge
instead of expanding by depth.
Qualified lookup reads only `N`, the immutable Program/Link static namespace
created by authored declarations, eligible source `TypePublication` export
surfaces, `R`'s exact-literal alias or static-operand projections, and sealed
host exports. Link obtains a module publication surface only through the exact
module export/return-root correspondence and the sealed publication rows; an
ordinary returned Heap Value, cache result, or later assignment cannot emulate
one. `N` routes references into the same canonical Program type forest and
sealed `ResolverRef` authority; it is not a type graph or resolver of its own.
It has no runtime Guard, module-cache, Heap, Module Factor, or Value input;
those affect runtime Values and annotation checks, not compile-time name
authority. A cached qualified answer never crosses different `N.contentID`s.

Primitive/literal/optional/union/intersection, resolved references, exact
generic application, arrays/maps/records, function signatures, admitted
interfaces, and productive recursive aliases form the initial closed
fragment. Static generic application requires exact arity and checks each
argument against its bound after the preceding substitutions. Authored call
type arguments are static facts, not inferred runtime Values.

`keyof` and indexed access operate per exact alternative through cycle-safe
structural projections. A failed or unresolved projection remains
Symbolic/diagnostic, never silently `never`. Conditional distribution is a
syntactic law, not a general union rewrite: it occurs only when the checked
source term is a naked type formal. Each union member is substituted for that
formal in the complete conditional environment before either arm is
evaluated. Every other check evaluates its union as one type. Closed
conditionals then obey:

```text
Cond(C,U,A,B)
  = Never                     when the checked term is a naked distributed
                              formal and its substituted union is Never
  = [[A]]N,rho,S              when C <: U is proved
  = [[B]]N,rho,S              when exact C <: U is disproved
  = Symbolic(...,N.contentID,S,rho,...) otherwise
```

Static recurrence is keyed by `(declaration, N.contentID, normalized
environment, S)`. Re-entry under the identical environment uses the
declaration's productive recursive authority. Re-entry under a changed
environment that would generate an unbounded generic tower publishes the finite
`Symbolic` boundary with its current namespace, substitutions, and bounds; it
neither unfolds again nor uses a depth, context, or instantiation cap. This is
the static counterpart of the finite TypeValue quotient below, not the same
State value.

For an abstract runtime alternative `q`, the type Rule yields the first arm
when subtype is proved, the second only when `q` is exact and subtype is
disproved, and both arms otherwise; results are then joined across candidates.
This is the monotonicity law. Absence of a proof never selects the false arm.
The context-free evaluator never evaluates `typeof(scope, operand)` as a
runtime expression. It first derives Static type's `ContainedOperand` judgment
from the sealed static-containment disposition and exact `N` at that operand.
For an admitted static-only literal `require`, the judgment uses the
Link-supplied module namespace to return its exact static namespace type; a
rejected contained operation returns its exact static diagnostic with no
runtime fallback. `Known(T)` makes `typeof` return that exact `T` directly.
`Unknown(reason)` makes it return a canonical symbolic static result carrying
that reason and its Target/Link dependencies, not a Value-Factor Top or
runtime read. Only `RuntimeSubject(v)` returns the canonical existing-authority
`Symbolic(TypeofHandle(static-reference, operand/source identity, N.contentID,
normalized rho, S))`; this is a distinguished residual Program type equation,
not a new carrier, type graph, or second type authority. The static-type Rule
alone interprets that handle by reading `v`'s exact operand Value/type Factor
coordinate at the exact source frontier and writes the existing static-type
result coordinate. Its read is logged normally, so a change to that coordinate
invalidates the Rule result while the pure evaluator memo and handle remain
unchanged. Conditional types, generic-bound checks, annotation enforcement,
asserting-return postconditions, and boundary class validation consume that
Rule result rather than reducing `typeof` themselves. Program preserves only
the operand and source identity.

Annotations and declared types are authored compile-time constraints. They are
not runtime type identity and do not create a call or effect. Their enforcement
is a domain Rule over the declared boundary and subject Value. Unknown
annotation semantics remain unknown/diagnostic. `ValueClaim` (`as`, `::`, and
postfix nonnil) is erased source intent: it preserves evaluation order and may
support advisory diagnostics, but never validates, narrows the semantic Value,
guards control, or adds a failure path.

An authored asserting return, `asserts p` or `asserts p is T`, is instead a
call-boundary postcondition. On that function's Normal outcome only, Link's
existing formal/actual Pack correspondence substitutes the declared parameter
to the exact caller Value, and the static-type Rule adds respectively its
truthiness predicate or substituted type predicate. It has no effect on Throw,
Yield, Cancel, or any unrelated alias. A missing/mismatched actual cannot yield
the proof and produces the owned static diagnostic. No assertion is executed
at runtime.

The historical fixture that expected postfix `!` on `nil` to Throw is outside
this frozen language law: it must expect erased source intent (and any advisory
diagnostic), not a synthesized Target outcome. No compatibility Rule preserves
that obsolete behavior.

`TypeValue` is separate and is a real runtime scalar Value. It can flow,
alias, be stored, and serve as the base of direct, exact-member, exact-indexed,
and method calls through ordinary Program topology. Static type syntax does
not automatically create one.

The sole source seed is the existing Program TypeValue row copied from a
binder mark on that exact call-base occurrence. Marking first recognizes the
frozen runtime-loadable primitive set, then a direct type/interface declaration
owned by the current chunk. It never recognizes a nested declaration. An
active local, parameter, upvalue, or pending local-function binding suppresses
the mark. Consequently the marked base evaluates to its TypeValue without a
mutable global lookup, whereas every unmarked spelling, alias, stored Value,
computed member, or other dynamic base follows ordinary Value lookup, flow,
and Call semantics. This row is source authority, not a hint: no Rule, Link
step, decoded artifact, static type reference, or runtime name can synthesize
the mark or choose a second target.

The TypeValue domain uses one private finite relation inside the joint
runtime-kind alternative; it adds no carrier or dispatch service to the
engine. Its stable dependency key is an existing abstract runtime root of an
`LType` or produced closure. Primitive seeds of one existing `PrimitiveKind`
share that primitive root. Named seeds share one existing representative root
exactly within `(Link shard, Program BodyActivation(owner), exact name)`;
ordinary branch bodies therefore share their function's object while nested
functions do not. Produced and reflected objects retain their existing fresh
allocation root. Ordinary Value/identity facts map every flowed source Value
and alias to those roots. Thus mutable iterator state is shared by all aliases
of one closure and never keyed by syntax or a second TypeValue identity plane.

Before sealing, `typeauthority` resolves every runtime seed's owner-fenced
analyzer structural view from the existing Program reference and canonical
scoped type bytes. That view removes transparent annotation and alias wrappers
for reflection while preserving the exact source name and resolver. It is
deliberately not the runtime `LType` validation view, which retains annotation
semantics, and Target owns only primitive loadability and ABI admission. The
TypeValue domain retains the sealed structural handle rather than Static's
authority or a decoded type graph. It then constructs a finite correlated
descriptor relation `D`; it is not the Cartesian product of independent inner,
name, and resolver sets:

```text
d = (inner, name, resolver) in D
inner    = one canonical known authority | OtherInner
name     = one exact reached name | None | OtherName
resolver = one sealed ResolverRef | None | OtherResolver
```

`D` is the least finite closure of exact Program/Link seeds under the finite
reflection constructors, syntactically fixed generic results, and each Rule's
declared summary image. A summary preserves a known coordinate only when that
constructor guarantees it—for example an unrepresented generic result keeps
the subject name and clears its resolver. An otherwise unsealed runtime object
maps to one opaque descriptor. The sealer rejects a Rule that can mint an
undeclared descriptor. Consequently descriptor count is proportional to
reachable seeds and constructor images, not cubic in three coordinate
vocabularies.

Known inner authority is only an authored type reference, existing canonical
closed type bytes, or a canonical scoped-formal authority containing the
owning static-scope content identity, validated external-formal count, and
scoped bytes. The latter tuple preserves enclosing-formal references exposed
by reflection and is the only context in which those bytes decode. The exact
static `unknown` authority remains distinct from `OtherInner`. A `ResolverRef`
is a content-addressed sealed tuple of module path, complete
name-to-canonical-type map, and Target builtin-resolution identity supplied by
the Program artifact or host contract. Its law resolves a reference locally
only when the reference module is empty or equals that path, then applies the
sealed builtin fallback only to an unqualified reference. Link validates the
whole tuple, and the validation Rule reads it through the reference. No raw Go
type, resolver pointer, or newly invented type graph becomes authority.

For the finite selectors and descriptors, the private atoms are:

```text
Object(d)                 runtime type object at the Factor's root
Method(selector, object)  bound method closure capturing an Object root
Iter(selector, object, p) reflection closure capturing Object and cursor p
```

For an exact descriptor with `m` reflected entries, `p` ranges over `0..m`;
an imprecise descriptor uses the one absorbing cursor `?`. The lattice is the
powerset of this finite atom set, stored as interned singleton/sparse sets with
a constant Top tag:

```text
Bottom = empty
a <= b iff a is a subset of b
Join(a,b) = a union b
Widen = Join
```

The ordinary identity/Heap roles distinguish separately allocated Objects and
closures. A descriptor never proves pointer identity. A fresh iterator result
has its own ordinary Target fresh root initialized to cursor zero. Calling an
exact Recent iterator strongly replaces `p` by `p+1`; aliases observe that
same root. A Summary/unknown-root call weakly joins every feasible cursor
successor. This is the TypeValue Factor's own root update, not an engine cursor
or duplicated Heap slot.

Program contributes only existing Read, Call, receiver, argument, result, and
TypeValue-seed rows. Rule construction indexes those rows directly; it does
not persist duplicate `TypeRead`/`TypeCall` relations. A Read activates on any
flowed Object. An exact string key uses this fixed priority: the reserved
selector set (`is`, `kind`, `name`, `elem`, `key`, `val`, `inner`, `ret`,
`fields`, `variants`, `params`, `tparams`) first; then an exact `Record.Fields`
member; then—only for the exact sealed `LTypeString` identity—the corresponding
ordinary `string` operation; otherwise one `nil`. Thus a reserved name never
reads a same-named record member or string-library member. A proved non-string
key likewise returns exactly one `nil` with no ordinary indexing or metatable
fallback. An imprecise key joins its feasible string branches and that nil
branch. Aliases, heap loads, callbacks, and dynamically proved singleton
strings use the same Rule. Every selector Read allocates one fresh ordinary
Target root for its bound `Method` closure and captures the selected Object root
there; aliases of that returned closure share the same captured subject, while
a later selector Read receives a distinct ordinary closure identity.

A Call activates on the flowed callee: `Object` performs direct validation or
generic instantiation, `Method` performs its selector, and `Iter` advances its
root state. Program's existing receiver coordinate is prepended exactly when
source syntax supplies it. A Target Produced row returning a bound method or
iterator captures one typed TypeValue subject because an aliased zero-argument
closure may have no actual Pack coordinate containing it. This is one exact
Link capture source for those rows, not a raw pointer, synthetic formal, or
general capture language.

Each operation seals exact Packs, aliases/freshness, outcomes, and effects. A
direct Object call with no arguments throws. If every supplied argument is an
`Object` and at least one exists, the call attempts raw-generic instantiation;
wrong arity or a non-generic base throws. `Method` and `Iter` are ordinary
function Values for this classification, not type-object arguments. Every
other successful validation form requires exactly one Value, returns that same
identity, and otherwise throws. Runtime construction does not replace the
static domain's authored generic-bound check.

Generic instantiation preserves the subject's exact name and clears its
resolver. Structural reflection children have `name=None,resolver=None`.
The argument-ignoring selectors have these exact one-value results: `kind`
returns the canonical `KindString` literal (`nil`, `boolean`, `number`,
`integer`, `string`, `any`, `unknown`, `never`, `optional`, `union`,
`intersection`, `tuple`, `function`, `array`, `map`, `record`, `generic`, or
`instantiated`), and `unknown` for a nil or otherwise unlisted inner; `name`
returns its exact nonempty name string or `nil` for `None`; `elem`, `key`,
`val`, and `inner` return respectively a fresh reflected child only for an
Array with non-nil element, Map with non-nil key, Map with non-nil value, or
Optional with non-nil inner, and otherwise one `nil`; and `ret` returns nil,
one reflected child, or a fresh canonical tuple Object for multiple returns
according to the exact Function type. `kind`, `name`, `elem`, `key`, `val`,
`inner`, `ret`, `fields`, `variants`, `params`, and `tparams` ignore every
supplied argument. Only `is` uses its sealed stack convention. Let `P` be its
final assembled Pack after Program prepends the source receiver for colon
syntax; that receiver remains in `P`. `is` selects `P[0]` when `|P| <= 1` and
`P[1]` otherwise, with a missing `P[0]` equal to Lua `nil`. Thus `T.is()`
selects missing `nil`, while `T:is()` selects the receiver `T`. `is` always
returns normally with exactly two results: successful validation returns the
selected Value and `nil`, while failed validation returns `nil` and the
validation Error Value. Validation failure is not a Throw outcome.

`OtherName` returns one String Value ranging over every feasible nonempty name;
it returns `nil` only from a separate feasible `None` descriptor. `OtherResolver`
is the finite descriptor's unresolved choice among its sealed resolver
alternatives, not a new resolver authority. Where the inner can reach resolver
lookup, validation joins every feasible resolver result: `is` joins only the
normal `(Value,nil)` and `(nil,ValidationError)` two-result Packs, while a
direct Object validation call joins the same-identity successful return and the
failed-validation Throw. It cannot prove either validation outcome; where the
inner cannot consult a resolver it has no additional effect.

The iterator selectors are partial. `fields`, `variants`, `params`, and
`tparams` create a fresh ordinary closure only for respectively a Record,
Union, Function, and raw Generic inner; an incompatible exact inner returns
one `nil` and creates no fresh root. `OtherInner` retains both feasible
alternatives. For a compatible exact descriptor, the sealed VM-order tuple
determines every transition:
at `p<m`, `fields` returns `(field-name String, field-type Object-or-nil)`,
`variants` returns `(member Object-or-nil)`, `params` returns `(parameter-type
Object-or-nil)`, and `tparams` returns `(parameter-name String,
parameter-constraint Object-or-nil)`, in the Target's stored tuple order; each
updates the same closure root to `p+1`. At `p=m` it returns one `nil` and stays
terminal. Reflection result Objects receive fresh roots and cleared
name/resolver as above. Each returned iterator closure accepts and ignores its
entire supplied Pack on every call. At cursor `?`, a call may return one
correctly shaped unknown entry and remain `?`, or return terminal `nil`. No
cardinality or depth cap creates `?`.

Runtime type comparison and table identity remain separate:

```text
Object(a) == Object(b)  iff inner(a) is structurally equal to inner(b)
Object(a) <  Object(b)  iff inner(a) <: inner(b) and they are not equal
Object(a) <= Object(b)  iff inner(a) <: inner(b)
parser-lowered a >= b   applies the same non-strict law as b <= a
table-key equality      uses the ordinary runtime object identity
```

The existing Value/identity Boolean-result Rule is the unique conclusion owner
for these runtime comparison operations. Anchored at the exact Program/Target
comparison row, it reads both operands' TypeValue descriptor alternatives. For
`<`, `<=`, and parser-lowered `>=` it additionally reads the exact Static-type
subtype judgment selected by those descriptors; equality uses the TypeValue
inner structural-equality observation and does not turn object identity into
structural identity. On a proved comparison it writes the single exact Boolean
alternative, and otherwise writes both feasible Boolean alternatives under
their shared Guard regions; ordinary Value truthiness then refines an existing
Program decision when the result is tested. It writes only Value/identity.
Numeric/equality owns neither this Boolean result nor a parallel runtime-type
comparison relation, and table-key equality continues to use ordinary object
identity.

Name and resolver affect reflection/validation only. Two fresh structurally
equal Objects may compare equal yet occupy distinct table keys. For an
`OtherInner`, equality and order retain every feasible Boolean alternative
unless ordinary identity or another domain theorem decides it; direct call
retains both valid generic/validation results and every feasible Throw. It can
never supply a structural-equality, subtype, raw-generic, or arity must proof.
The string-library branch likewise requires the one `LTypeString` identity,
not merely structural string type.

Runtime generic recurrence is cap-free. A result already in `D` stays exact.
At the first changed-environment recursive boundary outside that closure, its
declared summary descriptor is used and all further construction remains in
the same finite relation. Re-entry under an identical environment uses the
productive recursive static authority. Genuine validation/type errors remain
Target-visible outcomes. A VM defensive recursion/depth guard is a conformance
