defect, never Target behavior, analyzer convergence, or a reason to discard an
outcome.

Recursive source types use declaration graph identity and domain-owned
coinductive/fixed-point laws. Persisted Program artifacts carry only normalized
seed authorities, sealed ResolverRefs, and Target/Link capture structure.
TypeValue Factor atoms, cursor states, dynamic instantiations, host pointers,
solver state, recurrence counts, and Factor Top are State-bound and never
portable type authority.

### Packs and list adjustment

The Pack domain owns relations over `Val*`; `nil` is a slot and the empty Pack
is a different value. Its primitive Rules are the exact graphs required by the
dialect: scalarization, final-expression splice, fixed bind with nil-fill and
residual tail, fixed take/drop, length, dynamic `select`, table unpack/slice,
call/return adjustment, protected-call outcome Packs, and vararg forwarding.
A Rule consumes and produces the whole Pack relation. `FixedAt`-style scalar
answers are read-only marginals and cannot be recombined.

For each existing Program body or Link boundary and each outcome separately,
derive one finite, demand-independent schema from existing handles only:

```text
E_s = sorted typed scalar endpoints: existing Value/formal/result subjects and
      Cell contents at their existing Program input/output location
ClassOfScalar = total immutable map E_s -> ValueClass
P = sorted Program Values and Target ValuesVar/outcome Pack ports
F = incoming/free Pack ports in P, each with its sealed immutable ValueClass
B = locally produced open Pack ports used only as lexical binders
```

For a Link application/boundary row `a`, let `N_a` be its immutable lexical
namespace and `rho_a` its exact static-substitution environment. The same
context-free static evaluator and semantic digest `S` defined above produce,
before Pack seal, the application-scoped opaque reference

```text
h(a,e) = [[Apply(static-substitution(a), DeclaredType(e))]]N_a,rho_a,S
BoundaryClass(a,e) = Class(h(a,e))
```

for each endpoint `e` with a declared class; absence defaults to `AnyValue`,
while `Invalid` rejects the boundary. A `Closed` result names its existing
closed authority. A symbolic static-unknown result seals its exact reason and
predeclared finite class-LUB/rank entry; it has no Factor read. A
`Symbolic(TypeofHandle(...))` result instead seals only that canonical handle
plus the corresponding predeclared entry sound for every possible result of
its static-type Rule; it never requests or seals a converged `Closed`
interpretation. The application boundary schema derived from Link `a` seals
these results as its `ClassOfScalar` and free-port classes. Pack receives only
their canonical opaque authorities and never evaluates, substitutes, resolves,
reconstructs, or interprets a type. The class-map/schema identity contains
`a`, `N_a.contentID`, `S`, and the existing class-LUB/rank-law digest.
Program-body classes without a Link application remain supplied by their
existing Program/Target rows under the same canonical class authority.

Every endpoint is tagged `In`, `Carry`, or `Out` relative to the owning
body/boundary relation. A case constrains only Out endpoints; In endpoints are
inputs, and Carry endpoints preserve the same existing subject unless a
declared Rule supplies its corresponding Out equation. These are schema tags,
not new Program handles.

The conservative body schema includes every Values row owned by the body and
all incident scalar endpoints, including Cells written by Pack bind/list
adjustment; a boundary schema includes exactly its Link ports and the
application-scoped class map sealed by its `a`.
It mints no frame ID, tail identity, Rule-local layout, or copied
Value/type/heap fact. A later liveness projection is only a deterministically
derived optimization over this same schema.

Every exact `Closed` list and every exact `Open` prefix/suffix originates only
from a sealed Program/Target row whose scalar arity is statically enumerated in
this schema. A runtime- or recurrence-unbounded length enters the Pack Factor
as a `Free`, `Open`, or `Any` tail before any exact list is constructed. Seal
therefore enumerates the finite set of possible exact scalar slots for this
schema and closes its exact-slot and class ledgers under every existing
Program/Link call/callback/module/resume substitution layout and
`BoundaryClass` map received by the schema. Each ledger entry includes both its
slot layout and every imported scalar/tail `ValueClass`. An imported class is
preserved or mapped only through a sealed
receiver-safe coercion/LUB whose concretization contains the imported class;
the resulting receiver class set is closed under the same finite LUB and
strictly descending precision-rank law used by Pack widening. This is a finite
structural closure over existing rows and Link ranges, not an enumeration of
dynamic relation pairs or an unrolling of a recurrent substitution. It does
not bound concrete Pack length, which remains unbounded through those tails.

The domain-private term grammar is deliberately small:

```text
ValueClass = AnyValue | Class(existing canonical Value/static-type authority)
TailRef    = Free(F index, ValueClass) | Bound(B index, ValueClass)
Scalar     = Port(E_s index) | Literal(canonical target literal)
           | Head(TailRef, Nat) | AnyScalar(ValueClass)
Rest       = Tail(TailRef, Nat) | AnyTail(ValueClass)
Pack       = Closed([]Scalar)
           | Open(prefix []Scalar, middle Rest, suffix []Scalar)
           | AnyPack
```

A Factor value is a finite normalized antichain of cases. A case is a tuple of
equations for its output scalar/Pack ports, with only referenced `Bound` tails
existentially quantified. One valuation interprets every occurrence of a
given `Free` or `Bound` tail, so repeated use preserves sharing. Each
`AnyScalar(c)`/`AnyTail(c)` occurrence is independently unknown within `c`.
Every `Free(f,c)` repeats the immutable class sealed for `f`; seal rejects a
mismatch. `Head(t,k)` is the `k`th slot with nil-fill and `Tail(t,k)` is exact
drop. A `Bound` tail is a fresh logical choice, not disequality and not a
stable producer/iteration ID.
`Open(prefix,middle,suffix)` denotes prefix followed by an arbitrary finite
middle and then the fixed end-relative suffix; therefore dynamic
`string.unpack` can retain its final cursor Value without bounding the decoded
prefix. The two-argument spelling with an empty suffix is only notation.

`ValueClass` is a finite typed reference already supplied by Program/Target or
sealed as `BoundaryClass` in the application schema derived from Link; Pack
treats it opaquely and never reconstructs or copies a type. A symbolic class
identity contains its `N.contentID` and `S`; a canonical `Closed(T)` class uses
that existing closed authority. A static-unknown class carries its exact
contained-operand reason and has no dynamic validation read. For a
`TypeofHandle`, semantic class validation reads the one static-type Rule result
after solving has begun; the handle, its conservative concretization, and its
predeclared LUB/rank entry remain immutable. The Rule result does not mint or
replace a Pack class identity, and Pack widening never reads it. Its
concretization constrains every element of that scalar or tail, including every
actual element of a `Free(f,c)` or `Bound(b,c)` tail. The classes referenced by
one sealed Pack schema are closed under the existing authority's finite
least-upper-bound operation, with `AnyValue` as top. That authority also
supplies a finite precision rank strictly lowered by every strict class join;
seal rejects a class set without this law. `ClassOfScalar` is the total,
immutable class source for `Port(E_s)`, Target supplies a literal's class,
`AnyScalar(c)` has class `c`, and `Head(t,k)` has the sealed join of its tail
class and canonical `nil` because it nil-fills. These classes, free/bound-tail
classes, and literal classes all belong to the same sealed finite LUB/rank set.
Pack widening reads only this schema map and never reads the dynamic Value
Factor. A Pack/Value reduction may subsequently propagate `AnyScalar(c)`
directly; for `Head(t,k)` whose tail class is `c`, it propagates `c` only when
the same terminal tuple proves that slot present, otherwise the sealed join of
`c` and `nil`. Thus a dynamic `string.unpack` uses a fixed suffix
`AnyScalar(Class(integer))` while its decoded prefix remains an open typed
middle. Typed varargs, dynamic pattern captures, and dynamic format results
remain sound without inventing one Value endpoint per possible slot.

The checked Pack–Cell Bind Rule writes
`CellOut[i] = Head(PackIn,i)` with nil-fill and the declared residual-tail law,
while unchanged Pack ports remain available for forwarding/capture. A Cell
endpoint is only a reference to existing storage; Pack never owns its Value,
type, alias, or heap contents.

This grammar is exact for closed Packs, scalarization, final-position splice,
fixed bind with nil-fill/truncation and residual tail, fixed take/drop,
unchanged forwarding, and repeated use of one Pack source. Distinct evaluation
occurrences remain independent. Dynamic `select`/unpack is exact only when the
same terminal tuple and any consumer-justified Pack/Numeric/Heap relation prove
the index, range, slot presence, and error behavior. Otherwise every feasible
Target outcome, including Throw/Yield/Cancel where declared, remains live;
Normal `AnyPack` is never a substitute for an opaque operation outcome.

Order is structural after whole-case alpha normalization: exact expressions
cover only equal expressions, `AnyScalar(c)`, `AnyTail(c)`, and `AnyPack` cover their
respective classes, equal-shaped terms order pointwise, and
`Closed(prefix+suffix) <= Open(prefix,AnyTail(AnyValue),suffix)`. Case sets use the Hoare antichain
order. This order is sound for concretization inclusion; it is not advertised
as a decision procedure for all Pack-language inclusion. Bottom is the empty
case set and Top has one binder-free all-unknown case. Normalization:

1. alpha-normalizes bound tails by first use;
2. canonicalizes literals and arbitrary-precision natural offsets;
3. rewrites `Open([],AnyTail(AnyValue),[])` to `AnyPack` and removes unused binders;
4. sorts/deduplicates cases and deletes structurally covered cases.

`Join(A,B) = normalize(A union B)` is the binary least upper bound and its
concretization is exact union. No arbitrary-language inclusion solver is
required.

At a compiled `Mu`, a noncovered update widens the union to one generalized
case. A scalar expression is retained only when unanimous, otherwise it is
`AnyScalar(joined class)` using the sealed class of each input expression. A
Pack term is retained when unanimous; equal prefix and suffix
whose middle varies become `Open(prefix,AnyTail(joined class),suffix)`; if only
one end is unanimous the other is conservatively absorbed into the middle,
and every other variation becomes `AnyPack`. Shared bound-tail structure
survives only when the complete binder graph and its classes are unanimous.
Offset variation immediately loses that residual to `AnyTail(joined class)`;
it never increments a counter.

The executable rank is lexicographic. Its phase is Bottom > multi-case > one
non-Top case > Top. Within the singleton phase, each exact scalar contributes
one finite syntax-precision unit, every scalar/tail class contributes its
sealed class rank, and each Pack contributes exact=2, open-middle=1,
AnyPack=0. The sealed exact-list construction law fixes the finite number of
components; seal verifies that its mathematical maximum is representable by the
rank type, which is a representation-validity theorem rather than a semantic
budget. Every strict widening lowers phase or one component, so
recurrence terminates without bounding Pack length, offset, case count, or
execution depth.

Boundary substitution maps a callee free Pack parameter to a complete caller
Pack term, never merely another tail name. It maps `Head(Free(f,c),k)` through
exact Pack indexing and `Tail(Free(f,c),k)` through exact drop, validates the
callee's sealed `c` against the caller Pack relation and the receiving schema's
sealed class-ledger closure, preserves fixed suffixes and the
application-scoped substituted `ValueClass` handles, and freshens only local
`Bound` tails. It may
inline a caller exact Closed/prefix/suffix segment only when that segment's
sealed structural layout and complete class layout are both present in the
receiving schema's exact-slot and class ledgers; otherwise it retains the existing
`Port`/`Free` reference or normalizes the segment to an `Open`/`Any` tail with a
receiver-ledgered safe class before it enters that Factor. Call,
callback, module, and resume apply their own existing Link entry/outcome
correspondence separately. Portable witnesses retain only interface-indexed
free tails plus the Pack grammar/normalizer/widening/schema/Rule identities. A
witness for application `a` also names `a`, `N_a.contentID`, `S`, and the
class-LUB/rank-law digest already present in that schema identity. It never
persists caller tails or a derived substitution map.

### Numeric and equality

Numeric and primitive equality are one domain-owned Factor over one sealed
body/boundary root. Its finite atom universe is derived from relevant Program
Cells, produced and formal Values, exact numeric/key literals, and zero. No
Rule mints an atom, edge, threshold, range cell, or Factor key.

The opaque Factor element retains only the relations it owns:

```text
numeric eligibility: Atom -> P({integer, finite-float, infinity, NaN, other})
must raw-equality / raw-disequality for non-NaN primitives or singleton refs
definitely-integral equality
integer difference facts x - y <= c over sealed edge templates
```

The eligibility mask is a numeric projection introduced by Value/Target Rules,
not a second runtime-kind authority. It canonically derives
`NoNaN | MayNaN | OnlyNaN` and `MustInteger`; a difference edge is absent from
normal form unless both endpoints are MustInteger. Primitive/raw Lua equality
and integral equality are distinct. Primitive equality may
relate integer `1` and float `1.0`, but only definitely-integral equality may
quotient an integer difference fact. NaN is never encoded as a reflexive
equivalence or disequality edge. `MustEqual(x,y)` additionally requires both
operands definitely non-NaN; `x ~= x` narrows to NaN instead of creating a
self-disequality. Float comparison negation is never converted to an integer
complement.

The Target contract owns numeric coercion, overflow, primitive equality,
nil/NaN key errors, and integer/float table-key normalization. The Factor
consumes those typed operation laws; it is not a second numeric language.

Every element is normalized before equality, hashing, join, or publication.
Normalization constructs the full must raw-equality congruence over endpoints
proved non-NaN, chooses the least stable atom as each class representative,
stores must-disequality as a canonical symmetric relation between those
classes, and closes it under the equality congruence. Per-atom representation
masks remain per atom—numeric equality does not turn integer `1` and float
`1.0` into the same runtime representation. The definitely-integral
equivalence is the induced subrelation on endpoints whose masks prove integral
values; only that subrelation may quotient difference constraints.

The normalizer rejects empty masks, equality/disequality collisions, a pair of
exact literals that the Target primitive-equality law proves raw-unequal in
one class, a required reflexive equality that may be NaN, and any contradiction
between literal/mask facts and integer bounds. Integral-float equality and
signed-zero cases follow that Target law rather than literal representation
identity. It then
computes integer closure over the canonical integral classes and writes the
result back only on the fixed original templates. Thus representative choice
is not semantic, equivalent presentations have byte-identical normal form,
and `Join(A,A)=A`. Join computes exactly the equality congruence and class
disequalities entailed by both normalized operands before combining their
masks and bounds; it never unions must facts.

Let `E_Gamma` be the sealed finite set of oriented atom pairs whose difference
is read by a Program/Target operation, boundary projection, registered Query,
or another installed Rule. It is closed under the finite operand
correspondences those consumers declare; no runtime value adds a pair. Each
integer bound uses one fixed pair template in `E_Gamma` and a
finite Program/Target-derived threshold chain containing positive infinity.
Normalization computes exact integer closure over those fixed templates with
safe arithmetic, detects contradiction, then rounds each retained bound upward
to its least safe threshold. Derived shortest paths are a cache. Canonical
storage remains a normalized sparse map over original templates, never a
mutable DBM, representative-keyed matrix, generic constraint service, or SMT
state.

`Bottom` denotes no valuations. Canonical Top assigns every atom the full
eligibility mask, retains no raw/integer equality or disequality, and stores
positive infinity for every fixed bound. Every absent bound normalizes to that
positive-infinity threshold.

Concretization is the set of valuations satisfying every retained
primitive/raw equality/disequality fact, numeric eligibility mask, and integer
bound. Order is concrete-valuation inclusion. Join unions eligibility masks,
retains only equality/disequality facts entailed by both inputs, and for each
fixed edge `e` stores `max_threshold_e(bound_left[e], bound_right[e])`, then
normalizes. Bottom is the identity. It is admitted only with the
best-abstraction law for the fixed-template carrier. The carrier has finite
height: all atoms, pairs, templates, numeric flags, and threshold levels are
sealed and finite. `Widen = Join` therefore terminates at `Mu`; there is no
cardinality budget or Top-on-exhaustion path.

The initial exact fragment is primitive/raw literal and singleton-reference
equality, normalized static keys, no-overflow integer `x := y +/- c`, integer
comparisons, and their interval/difference consequences. Residues, general
affine forms, octagons/polyhedra, nonlinear or arbitrary float arithmetic, and
a stored loop-witness vocabulary are absent until a concrete consumer and a
complete Factor proof require them. Program owns loop shape; a Rule can later
emit a loop-bound certificate from the existing shape plus proved numeric and
frame facts.

### Heap and key space

Heap owns abstract allocation identity, slots/locations, mutation epochs,
containment, alias reachability, metatable effects, and shape eligibility.
`Cell` always means the Program's exact lexical/global/upvalue storage; a heap
slot is an allocation-plus-key abstraction and never a Program Cell.

The Heap Factor's stable key is the allocation/aggregate root. Inside that
root's lattice element, its state-dependent partition distinguishes:

- exact field atom;
- exact canonical boolean/string/number key atom;
- finite symbolic key set;
- equality-class key;
- array/range partition;
- unknown dynamic key.

The stored normal form is a full disjoint partition, not a set of overlapping
key covers. It contains one residual cell state for each legal non-nil runtime
key kind and a sorted vector of atomic exceptions. An exception is one exact
canonical Link key or exact rooted reference and is retained only when its
cell state differs from that kind's residual state. The residual denotes its
runtime-kind universe minus those exceptions. Finite and kind-unknown key sets
are operation selectors over this partition; they are never stored cells and
carry no caller-supplied exclusions. Consequently an exact distinction and
the all-other-keys state coexist without overlap, while an unknown write
weakly updates the matching kind residuals and exceptions. Join retains an
exception from the sorted union of operand exception atoms exactly when the
pointwise join of both operands' selected cell states differs from the joined
implicit residual state; one operand may therefore supply a distinction that
the other operand's residual safely preserves. Its complete
raw/payload/containment tuple moves together. This fixed-kind residual plus
finite-exception form is the Heap partition rank; no exception count threshold
triggers coarsening.

A rooted-reference exception obtains its possible runtime-kind mask only from
sealed Link/Target provenance. Program table and closure roots classify as
Table and Function; boot aggregate roots classify as Table; a guarded fresh
Target root uses the union of its exact Target-declared possible result kinds. Root family is
never used as runtime kind. When one structural reference has several possible
kinds, its one exception identity spans those residual kinds and its implicit
default is their joined state; it is not duplicated into several identities.
The Number residual contains only valid non-NaN table keys. Nil/NaN validity
remains an operation Rule outcome and never becomes stored Heap state.

Each partition cell carries raw occupancy and a payload conditional on the
present case:

```text
RawPresence in P({absent,present})
Slot = (RawPresence, payload for present worlds)
```

A present raw slot is non-nil; assigning nil deletes it. Raw miss and a
metamethod result of nil therefore remain distinct. Base identity, key
equality, occupancy, present payload, metatable route, and selected dispatch
are read from one opaque heap relation/terminal tuple. During ascending solve,
every may-present direct branch and may-absent fallback branch remains live.
Only dependency-tracked narrowing may use must-present, must-absent, or
must-no-dispatch to suppress an alternative. `must-no-dispatch` means
must-absent and every feasible metatable route is the no-handler route; it is
not an independent negative flag. New aliases, overlapping writes,
partition coarsening, metatable mutation, callback/opaque re-entry, dynamic
relation admission, or resume-world mutation revoke that proof and its
certificate.

Deep lenses remain structural through substitution across arbitrarily nested
libraries. Finite Program allocation/key handles use exact powersets.
Symbolic numeric/string/reference partitions use their domain join and
termination-proven widening at `Mu`; they never rise to a summary because a set
cardinality limit was reached. No string path or fixed semantic depth appears.
Partition join transports slot contents through the sound common partition;
partition refinement/coarsening never changes the enclosing Factor key or
escapes the owning heap lattice.

An allocation root is the finite structural tuple `(creation occurrence,
structural application, object ordinal)`, not a concrete object ID. Creation is
an existing Program Table/Function Term or an exact result ordinal whose sealed
Target outcome explicitly binds a fresh nominal object. That binder proves the
result is disjoint from every preexisting root and from each other fresh ordinal
in the outcome. A non-fresh Target result remains an alias/imported/unknown
identity and cannot create an allocation root. Application is the sealed
Program/Link application/candidate relation or declared analysis root, never a
Solver coordinate, generation, or visit count. A finite boundary-allocation
token is abstract provenance only; it cannot inhabit a concrete `ExactID` or
by itself justify must-equality on a recurrent visit. The Heap
element records the finite abstract count `P({0,1,many})` and two roles:
`Recent(root)` for the latest object in a represented state and
`Summary(root)` for older objects. On recurrent creation, the old Recent
contribution moves to Summary and a freshly initialized Recent is produced.
`many` denotes every count at least two. In a concrete state with `n` live
objects represented by one root, `n=0` has neither role; `n>=1` has exactly one
Recent object, and Summary denotes exactly the other `n-1` objects. The roles
are disjoint. Recent is a must-singleton only when zero is absent from the
abstract count; Summary is never must-singleton. This
preserves freshness, disequality, and strong-update proofs without minting a
Factor key, Guard atom, or cache identity per iteration.

Materialization is a semantic renaming: every surviving old Recent reference,
equality fact, containment edge, and heap content moves to Summary; the new
object becomes Recent and is proved distinct from the old summary. The
materialization/version token then invalidates dependent derived conclusions.
Invalidation without this transport is insufficient.

This is not a generic engine reindexing service. The creation occurrence is a
declared input to one joint successor transfer; every Factor whose value can
contain a root role owns a typed Rule that applies the same
`old Recent -> Summary, fresh result -> Recent` substitution before successor
confluence. Factors that only derive conclusions from those roles read the
materialization/version token and recompute. Failure to install any owning
Rule is a composition error, never permission for a reference scan or an
adapter.

The executable transport is split exactly into `Age` and `Fresh`. For one
creation occurrence `r`, `Age_r` maps every carried terminal occurrence of
`Recent(r)` to `Summary(r)`, preserves its correlated payload and
capabilities, and is identity on every other terminal and on Top. It neither
creates nor returns the new object. The same Factor patch then strongly
replaces only the declared allocation-result coordinate with
`Fresh(r) = {Recent(r)}`. Consequently an alias, Cell, capture, Pack member,
or contained reference surviving from the predecessor becomes Summary, while
only the new result becomes Recent. `Age_r` is a total, default-preserving,
monotone, idempotent Join homomorphism over the owning sealed lattice;
reapplying the same creation occurrence therefore reaches the identical
post-state. It is an ordinary transfer, not widening, fuel, an epoch counter,
or a second recurrence rank.

The engine exposes one typed transformed-carry form solely at a Factor owner
boundary. It replaces identity Carry for that Rule, maps the already-derived
finite carry-target closure under the Rule row Guard, and composes with that
Rule's ordinary exact/route writes in one predecessor-bound Patch and one
Group contribution. The carrier sees only an opaque typed terminal map and
the existing target capabilities; it never receives a root, role, domain
kind, raw key, or state-wide enumeration callback. The transform reports the
exact changed typed-key regions through the existing ChangeSet path, so exact
readers and whole-Factor carries are invalidated without a separate version
Factor. A transform-only Rule is a real candidate without a sentinel write;
a failed transfer, failed evidence check, or cancellation publishes neither
the transform nor any sibling write. Runtime work is proportional to the
presealed carried target surface and distinct encountered terminals, with
candidate-local terminal memoization; no `Value x allocation` instance table,
Factor-global reference scan, lazy overlay State, or optional truth-changing
cache exists.

The transformed-carry law is parameterized by the Rule's immutable canonical
instance operand: the cold Factor form has one semantic identity, while the
bound instance closes `map(operand, value)` once into its hot typed map. A
global `value -> value` closure is invalid because it cannot distinguish two
allocation roots using the same Rule schema. A carry closure's sealed dynamic
route-universe bit is resolved once into the same canonical target vector as
its static targets; route-produced aliases cannot escape `Age_r`, and an
overlapping static/route target is transformed once.

`ValueSourceAllocation` remains only sealed allocation provenance. It is not
an unconditional executable Value source and cannot publish `Exact(root)`.
The Value allocation Rule owns Value's `Age_r` plus the exact Fresh result.
Heap owns one fused constructor patch that applies Heap's structural `Age_r`
to every carried occurrence of `r` and constructs the selected principal
root; no direct-host enumeration or per-host aging Rule survives beside it.
Every later root-bearing Factor uses the same typed terminal-transport shape.
A Factor key is stable structural identity: in
particular Typestate moves its mutable Exact/Recent/Summary role from the key
into its correlated relation before its materialization Rule is installed,
rather than requiring a generic key-rewrite service.

Strong update requires must-existing Recent plus exact root/key, uniqueness,
no-unmodeled-mutation, and a raw operation or must-selected primitive/direct
ordinary-access route. A possible absent-key `__newindex` dispatch forbids
replacement of the raw slot. A
Summary/mixed/unknown root receives weak update. Value/identity,
Numeric/equality, Heap containment, and Escape Rules depend on the root
materialization/version token, so Recent-to-Summary transport invalidates them
through declared dependencies, not a global reference scan. The placement
Query/certificate reprojects those invalidated named-Factor conclusions; it
has no Rule dependency of its own. Module initialization and resume restore
existing identity according to their sealed relations; they do not masquerade
as fresh allocation.

The existing opaque Heap relation also carries the shallow frozen predicate
required by `table.freeze` and `table.isfrozen`. In the initial canonical
carrier it is conditional on the finite root and `Exact`/`Recent`/`Summary`
role only. Canonical Value currently seals one atom per structural root/role,
and Numeric seals scalar pairs; neither owns a finite class that can select one
concrete member of `Summary`. Therefore `Summary` denotes the whole old-object
aggregate in this stage. No source occurrence, generation, ContentID, or
Numeric pair may be promoted into an exact Summary identity. Freezing never
mints a table, root, class, or identity. `table.freeze(t)` requires a table and
returns the same Value identity. An undistinguished Summary/multi-root update
is weak and cannot prove every represented Summary object frozen. Exact
selected-Summary freezing, metatable/slot lookup, key equality, and must
diagnostics remain unavailable until Value/identity owns an explicit finite
sealed class relation with materialization substitution and evidence; adding
that relation is an atomic owner extension, not a Heap-local token or adapter.
Materialization transports the existing role predicates with the same
Recent-to-Summary substitution as slots and aliases.

`table.isfrozen(t)` reads the predicate for the exact `Exact`/`Recent`
identity, or the aggregate predicate for `Summary`; the latter produces a
must Boolean only when the relation proves it for every represented Summary
object. The Boolean is used by ordinary Guard refinement. Freezing is shallow:
replacing a field on that selected object is forbidden, while mutation through
a distinct child-table identity remains governed by the child's state.

A frozen-root Throw belongs only to a route that actually mutates that root.
`rawset`, `setmetatable`, and direct table mutators always take that route.
For ordinary assignment, a raw-present/direct-write route checks frozen state;
a must-selected `__newindex` handler instead follows its own application and
does not fail merely because the original base is frozen. A may route retains
all feasible handler, raw-write, and frozen-error alternatives. Only
must-frozen on the selected mutation target supports an exact error
diagnostic/evidence, and only must-mutable on that same route suppresses it.
Aliases observe the shared conditional predicate; resume/provider re-entry can
change it only through an explicit operation on that identity. No physical
immutability or copy policy enters the Factor.

Transitive immutability is a domain-owned derived theorem, never an effect of
`table.freeze` and never Query-side reachability. For each exact Guard terminal,
let `R` be the finite sealed root-role/identity-selection universe and let
`MayContain` be the converged Heap containment relation. The Heap immutability
Rule computes the ascending least fixed point

```text
Bad0 = { r | r lacks its kind-specific must-immutable seal
              or has a feasible opaque/externally mutable edge }
Bad  = lfp X. Bad0 union { r | exists q. MayContain(r,q) and q in X }
```

This positive equation is an ordinary cross-root Rule dependency and receives
the compiled equation `Mu` of its SCC; finite `R` and set union prove
termination. Only after the containing solve epoch, dynamic edge set, and
Heap ascent are stable may the domain's dependency-tracked must reduction
publish `DeepImmutable(r)` from `r notin Bad`. A new containment/alias/opaque
edge, loss of a seal, materialization, provider re-entry, or candidate
activation invalidates the complete reverse slice, removes the must
conclusion, and restarts the canonical phase. Evidence must cover every Guard
leaf and the closed outgoing containment set. No pre-stability Rule or Query
may use absence from `Bad` as proof.

For a table identity selection, the required seal is its conditional shallow
frozen predicate and containment includes keys, present values, and every
behaviorally reachable metatable edge. Other aggregate kinds use only their
own declared immutable/closed law; immutable scalars discharge immediately.
Closure Cells, userdata, providers, callbacks, and unknown dynamic slots enter
`Bad0` unless their exact contract supplies the required seal and containment.
Frozen cycles remain outside `Bad` without depth traversal. The resulting
theorem can establish semantic transfer/placement eligibility; it never
selects copy, share, move, COW, reference counting, or GC policy.

Heap order is concrete-state inclusion. Raw/count masks join by union;
conditional payload and metatable alternatives join only in their applicable
worlds; root partitions join through their sound common coarsening. Widening
combines the finite raw/count/role loss with the symbolic-key partition's
declared well-founded rank and the contained payload ranks. Narrowing may
refine may-occupancy only after stable ascent. This is the Heap Factor termination
law; no partition size or allocation count triggers summary/Top.

Ordinary Heap `Relation` and `Join` retain a canonical antichain of complete
control worlds. Incomparable worlds of the same control family remain distinct:
merging them would invent cross-combinations among occupancy, payload,
containment, metatable route, shape, frozen state, and the paired
`Recent`/`Summary` history. Only a changed recurrent `Widen` may coalesce those
worlds as an explicit sound precision loss; `Widen(v,v)` returns `v` before
coalescence.

For a root key let `F=3` for allocation families `Zero/One/Many` and `F=1`
for boot family `Exact`, and let `families(v)` count the active families. The
Heap widening rank is the fixed three-component lexicographic measure

```text
r0(Top) = 0
r0(v)   = 1 + F - families(v)                 otherwise
r1(Top) = 0
r1(v)   = len(worlds(v)) - families(v)        otherwise
r2(v)   = 0                                   when Top or r1(v) != 0
r2(v)   = sum fixed-coordinate Object scores otherwise
```

The last sum contains at most the `One.recent`, `Many.recent`, and
`Many.summary` object positions for an allocation root, or the one `Exact`
object for a boot root. An Object score measures remaining alternatives in
the finite shape, frozen, metatable-reference, residual-cell, and every sealed
atomic-key selected-cell coordinate. Atomic-key coordinates are compressed by
their owner-derived runtime-kind-mask counts; the score never uses sparse
exception count. A newly admitted family lowers `r0`; pre-existing same-family
alternatives coalesced by Widen lower `r1`; otherwise a strict pointwise Object
join lowers `r2`. Seal must prove the Object score and its at-most-three-object
sum fit the engine's `uint64` measure. Rejection of an unrepresentable sealed
schema is a representation/admission failure, never a semantic cardinality
cap, iteration budget, or Top-on-growth path.

Every table key and value is therefore a strong semantic containment edge.
Creation, mutation, or attachment of an active weak `__mode` or finalizer
`__gc` metatable relation cannot become an unknown heap branch: source
construction, Target contracts, and stored artifacts reject it before analysis.
Unattached inert `__mode` and `__gc` fields remain ordinary data. GC tracing,
cache eviction, and host-only destruction remain runtime implementation. If a
sealed provider exposes observable release or destruction, its ordinary typed
lifecycle boundary is analyzed like any other external operation; the engine
does not create a generic GC graph or finalizer domain for it.

### Effects

There is one typed effect authority: sealed Target/provider `RowSpec` contracts
and exact Program/Link boundary correspondences. Unsupported source syntax
does not invent Koka handlers, row syntax, or effect operations.

The Effect Factor contains one opaque finite may-set per sealed body/boundary
root. Its atom universe `U` contains the finite typed operation templates
reachable from the sealed Target/Link plus one `UnknownExternal` atom whose
concretization is every unsealed/unrepresented operation.

An operation template is a dense Target operation handle plus its sealed
Program value/type/Values/row formal coordinates. It has no string label,
reflection identity, `any` payload, runtime object identity, or dynamically
minted residual variable. For `M` a subset of `U`:

```text
gamma(M) = { E | abstraction(E) is a subset of M }
M <= N   iff M is a subset of N
Join     = set union
NoEffects = empty set
Top        = U
Widen      = Join
```

There is therefore one canonical value for every capability denotation; no
separate `Open` value duplicates `Top`. An opaque call or open authored row
adds `UnknownExternal`. A negative effect certificate requires absence of the
queried template and `UnknownExternal` on every feasible Guard leaf, plus the
call domain's complete-candidate evidence. The least value means exactly no
operation, not global unreachability; Guard support owns reachability.

`RowSpec` remains exact static contract syntax. Duplicate occurrences and row
variables survive sealing and boundary reindexing, but duplicates do not become
dynamic event multiplicities: in Koka they describe handler/evidence nesting.
Link's exact formal correspondence, constrained by the referenced Target law,
is the sole substitution authority.
A boundary Rule reads that correspondence and its Effect inputs, adds explicitly
named operation templates, transports the named tail capability, and propagates
`UnknownExternal` only from an opaque boundary/open row. It never reconstructs a contract or invokes
a generic row solver.

Effects do not determine control, outcome routing, callback scheduling,
resumption, heap mutation, ownership, or typestate. Exact Program relations and
their owning Factors do. Effect Rules may read those Factors from the same
terminal tuple but write only Effect.

The finite sealed template universe gives finite height, so
`Widen = Join` terminates at `Mu`. Repeated execution never increments a label
count; there is no residual-tail allocation, row/node budget, or
Top-on-exhaustion fallback.

Source-authored handlers, principal row unification, fresh residual inference
variables, masking/elimination, generalized join tails, and row anti-unification
remain absent until Target admits the corresponding concrete handler relation
and the whole substitution-graph LUB is proved. A set of possible tail names is
not a tail identity. There is no `Content any`, reflection label, parallel
operational-effects record, or effect payload for heap/type/placement facts.

### Calls and higher-order flow

The call domain owns callee sets, dispatch completeness, opaque/imported
boundary behavior, and candidate evidence. It does not own a mega-summary of
value/equality/heap/escape fields.

Once a candidate is proven, the Solver specializes that body's generic
equation artifact. A callee set explicitly carries open/complete status. The
open case always retains the opaque typed boundary contribution; only a
complete set licenses negative/must proofs. Higher-order callback applications
remain formal residual dependencies until a boundary correspondence supplies
the callback. Supplying it invalidates only equations that read it.

### Pulse-class memory and actor-placement standard

Pulse is a production comparison point, not a current capability claim. This
analyzer is not "on par" until the complete Wave-E/F corpus demonstrates the
laws below through the sole production Solver and real Oracle. Passing lattice
unit tests, owning the right structural rows, or having a richer intended actor
model is insufficient.

There is one concrete memory semantics in `Cfg(Γ)`: actor-indexed allocation
identities, raw slots and metatables, Value aliases, Cells/captures, module and
continuation roots, resource protocol state, and typed transfer occurrences.
Heap, Value/identity, Escape, Ownership, Transfer, Suspension, Residence,
Footprint, Typestate, and Static/TypeValue each abstract only their exclusive
projection of that configuration. No domain may retain a second points-to
graph, symbolic heap, ownership graph, effect heap, placement graph, or
procedure-summary state.

The reusable body equation and its domain-proved `FrameAccessClosure` are the
only compositional memory summary. For every admitted body/boundary
specialization, checked evidence establishes all of the following:

```text
reads/writes are contained in the declared footprint
roots outside MayWrite are frame-preserved
boundary substitution preserves alias equalities and disequalities
fresh binders are disjoint; recurrent binders apply Recent -> Summary
every ordinary and exceptional outcome has the same exact footprint law
```

A domain may infer the smallest sufficient local footprint or missing caller
precondition in the style of bi-abduction, but the result is a checked
derivation over existing Factor conclusions and body equations. It is not a
new Factor, mutable inferred specification, heaplet store, or alternate call
evaluator. Failure to prove a small footprint conservatively widens the owned
Footprint/Escape conclusion and blocks the must certificate; it never assumes
the frame.

Safety/placement and true-bug claims have explicit polarity. A must-safety or
placement theorem is over-approximating and covers every feasible Guard leaf.
A concrete bug report requires an under-approximating realizability derivation
or a named completeness theorem; an over-approximating may path is only a
possible defect. A latent defect retains its exact guarded caller condition in
evidence and becomes manifest only when ordinary boundary substitution proves
that condition at a call site. These are evidence interpretations of the same
Rules and equations, never an OX Solver plus a parallel UX Solver.

Actor placement is accepted only when the converged facts prove all of the
following for the exact transferred object graph: alias closure, mutable versus
deep-immutable descendants, capabilities, cycles, metatable reachability,
resource duties, mutation before and after the send, sender/receiver ownership
and residence, suspension/module retention, and every
normal/throw/yield/cancel outcome. A mutable graph cannot cross actors. An
immutable/sealed graph may be shared only under the Target transfer law; a
move/send may transfer authority only when all aliases and duties are
discharged. Unknown keys, opaque calls, mutable metatables, unresolved
capabilities, Summary identity, or incomplete call sets remain named blockers
rather than optimistic placement.

Types participate through declared Rules, not by overriding memory evidence.
Static type and TypeValue facts may refine kind, field/key eligibility,
protocol, and Target contracts; Heap/Value facts determine runtime aliasing and
contents. A type annotation never proves uniqueness, deep immutability,
residence, or sendability without the corresponding owning-Factor theorem.
Conversely the memory domains never reconstruct namespaces, subtyping, or
generic substitution.

The portable consumer boundary is a canonical typed theorem certificate, not
State or raw Factor values. One eligible certificate contains its semantic
claim and polarity, exact Program/Link/Target identities, dominating Guard,
typed premises/evidence root, domain/checker versions, and precision blockers.
Its canonical bytes and independent checker are backend-neutral. Compiler,
Oracle, placement, diagnostics, and later runtime/JIT consumers may interpret
that same immutable theorem repeatedly; none may inspect Solver coordinates or
rederive heap/type/ownership semantics. State-bound caches and lattice roots
remain non-portable, and absence or rejection of a certificate selects the
generic correct behavior.

A scalar digest of the final check is an attestation/cache key, not an
independently checkable post-fixpoint proof. Without an opening for the
Factor-owned values and Guard relations, a consumer cannot establish
`R_p == Y_p`, `R_h <= Y_h`, current candidate inputs, or local transfer
soundness from that digest. A portable certificate therefore either carries
the owning domains' typed, versioned local proof material plus the canonical
Guard-cover/post-fixpoint composition witness, or its checker replays the
sealed demanded slice from equivalent Program/Link/Target and composition
inputs. The replay is verification, not a second semantic authority, and
publishes no State. No completion serial, solver revision integer, opaque
RuleEvidence, 64-bit Factor fingerprint, or transcript hash may be presented
as a proof without one of those two checking paths.

The memory exit corpus includes Pulse-style local/latent heap defects plus the
Wippy-specific cases that are the intended differentiator: exact and dynamic
table keys, metatable delegation/mutation, closure capture, recursive
Recent/Summary allocation, module cache aliases, coroutine suspension/resume,
resource typestate, and cross-actor send/move/share. Each case exercises
accepted, rejected, unknown, foreign-owner, artifact-replay, and source/module
permutation laws. Measurements report lowering, equation compilation, solve,
certificate checking, allocations, and peak RSS separately. No
body-by-allocation, caller-by-heap, actor-by-object, or consumer-specific fact
plane is admissible; warm unchanged certificate queries allocate nothing.

### Escape, ownership, typestate, and placement facts

Escape, ownership, transfer, suspension, residence, footprint, and typestate
are semantic Factors with their own lattices and Rules. They retain maximum
per-Guard precision about:

- containment, aliasing, and escape through capture/store/return/send/global
  boundaries;
- owner, lifetime, last use, capture/callback retention, suspension survival,
  and actor/module/global reachability;
- borrow/move/share/send obligations and transfer outcomes;
- mutation/publication order, identity observability, contents at each transfer
  occurrence, non-interference by later mutation, and whether replacement
  would be observable;
- graph portability, cycles, capabilities, descendant reachability, and
  immutable/shared facts;
- exact or abstract symbolic allocation count, object/graph footprint,
  element-count/capacity bounds, overflow, and uncertainty;
- typestate transitions, invalidation, and every semantic blocker.

Their analysis scope is one actor, not a function, callback, message, or
receiver-image scope. At actor boot, the sealed initial-root ledger normalizes
each boot-global aggregate through its Target `BootShape` row and Link's
actor-boot correspondence to one distinct actor-local initial Value/Heap root.
The same correspondence chooses each actor's `GlobalEnvRoot` instance and
seeds every `GlobalBinding` Cell/lens pair from the one initial-root-ledger slot
before any body runs. Link rejects an alias of any mutable Heap, global, or
library root across actors; roots within one actor retain their exact aliasing.
Actors may agree only on sealed immutable Target or `BootShape` inputs, never
on a mutable boot root. All `_G`, library, and ordinary aliases in that actor
retain that one normalized root identity. `BootShape` is not a Heap carrier,
per-use copy, per-delivery image, or version plane; Heap alone owns dynamic
shape conclusions after boot.

Resume is an exact two-input boundary. A suspended continuation retains the
existing frame/capture Cell identities, already-evaluated pending Values, its
Program continuation location, and live Guard valuation. It does not retain a
whole ambient-state snapshot. Frame Cell contents and pending scalar operands come from
the suspended continuation. Capture correspondence maps an
inner captured Cell to the same outer Cell atom; it never copies the captured
contents.

Heap resume is an identity pullback, not a join of two complete heaps. Roots
proved reachable only from the retained frame carry their suspended contents
forward. Roots reachable from the ambient/shared/module/provider state use the
current resumer contents. The same root identity in both inputs denotes one
current object, and a may-private/may-shared root retains both alternatives and
blocks a must proof. Thus a continuation-private table is not lost, while a
captured table mutated by the resumer is not restored stale. There is no Alias
Factor. Suspension alone writes the exact generation's retained
Values/Cells and private/shared root classification; Value/identity owns
value-level alias/identity transport, Heap owns the pullback of selected root
contents, Escape owns boundary reachability, Ownership owns resulting duties,
and Residence owns resulting actor/module/global location and retention. The
two-input resume correspondence names both the consumed Suspension generation
and current resumer inputs. Its Suspension Rule writes only generation
consumption/classification; its Value/identity, Heap, Escape, Ownership, and
Residence Rules each read that exact classification plus only their declared
continuation/resumer inputs and write only their own Factor conclusion. They
commit the one declared resume successor/outcome together, so no intermediate
"continuation heap", alias carrier, or copied ambient state exists. The
composition inventory and resume matrix enumerate those reads/writes and their
reverse invalidation before seal. Each other domain transports only its own
projection across the same boundary. `ReentryOnce` consumes its continuation capability in the
  same restored transition. `ReentryMany` preserves its reusable carrier
  capability but atomically consumes the specific suspended generation before
  restoration; that generation either produces one successor generation on
  Yield or reaches terminal Normal/Return/Throw/Cancel. A concurrent or stale
  resume of a consumed or running generation takes the typed invalid-resume
  Throw. Guard combines the continuation and resumer valuations through the two
  exact boundary substitutions and discharges dead historical decisions.

Resource typestate/cleanup uses one small Typestate-owned relation keyed by a
finite structural resource origin: exact Target protocol plus acquisition
result/application, formal/imported identity source, or explicit unknown
origin. Recurrent fresh acquisitions use the same `Recent/Summary` materialization
law as other nominal roots; an unknown or Summary resource never supplies
singleton privileges. The acquisition boundary substitution maps surviving old
Value/identity/resource references to Summary and the new result to Recent
before successor join. Its element keeps
the correlations required by consumers:

```text
protocol state × cleanup duty × holder-origin -> P({0,1,many})
```

Typestate is the sole conclusion owner for this relation, including every
resource holder count and cleanup-duty transition. Ownership may read those
conclusions to derive owner/borrow/move/share/send/lifetime obligations, but it
does not duplicate holder multiplicity or cleanup duty in its own carrier.

Cleanup duty is local, explicitly handed off, discharged, or unknown. A holder
is an exact finite structural coordinate:

```text
Local(acquisition/application)
Callback(callback application)
Suspension(suspension/candidate)
External(handoff row)
Opaque
```

Acquire, exact outcome transition, retain/release,
suspend/resume, and explicit handoff are typed Rules over the Target lifecycle
rows. Retaining or releasing callback `c` changes only `Callback(c)`.
`ReleaseOne` maps `1` to `0` and `many` to `{1,many}`. For input `0`, the exact
Target lifecycle row must declare one total behavior: suppress the successor,
take its typed Throw/error successor, or succeed idempotently as `0`; the Rule
applies that table pointwise to every possible count class. `ReleaseAll` maps a
positive holder to `0` and uses the same row-declared zero behavior. Neither
changes protocol state or cleanup duty. Only an exact
resource transition endpoint changes state, and a final transition explicitly
discharges duty. Ordinary escape or callback retention never silently
discharges cleanup duty. An outcome with no live callback or continuation
successor requires discharge or explicit external handoff. A resumable Yield
instead transfers the exact `Local` holder to its `Suspension` holder and
preserves duty; the sealed cancellation endpoint determines later cleanup.
Resume
transfers the exact Suspension holder through the same identity pullback used
by Heap. A resource proved continuation-private carries its suspended protocol
state; an ambient/shared/module/provider resource reads the current resumer
state; the same identity in both inputs denotes one current resource; and a
may-private/may-shared origin retains both alternatives and blocks must proofs.
Releasing an exact Recent instance cannot discharge a different
Recent or a Summary set; a summary alias retains conservative duties unless an
identity theorem isolates the one transitioned resource.

Protocol states, materialization roles, holder coordinates, and `0/1/many`
multiplicities are sealed and
finite, so join has finite height and no activation-count identity or refcount
integer is required. This relation contains no heap shape, effect row,
placement choice, physical refcount, COW policy, or runtime handle.

These Factors never prescribe physical region/allocation/layout/root/COW policy
or a placement plan. Queries project their converged facts/evidence. A future
consumer may choose a guarded lawful representation or its generic correct
operation; this wave defines facts and certificates only.

Residence/lifetime conclusions are role-sensitive. Recent may support
singleton, strong-update, last-use, or exact-footprint evidence only while it
must exist and no capture/store/return/send/callback/suspension/opaque boundary
escapes or retains it. Such a transition revokes or lifts the affected
conclusion. Summary never inherits those singleton privileges, but it may retain
a common residence/lifetime theorem when the owning domain proves that theorem
for every represented older object. Otherwise its conclusion is the lawful
join/unknown. This is a semantic eligibility law, not a physical allocation
choice.

Heap, ownership, escape, lifetime, and transfer Rules interpret each finite
Program publish/send occurrence directly. Under its Guard they derive the
sender-side reachable aliases, contents relevant to the sealed transfer
contract, mutation before and after that occurrence, exact or unknown changed
lenses, descendant immutability and portability, ownership obligations,
exceptional outcomes, and uncertainty. A cross-actor Send/Receive admits a
heap value only when the sealed Target transfer row proves that its reachable
graph is immutable/sealed (`DeepImmutable` where that proof is required);
otherwise its declared transfer failure/blocker applies. Thus no mutable Heap,
global, library root, or Module-cache representative crosses actors. Send is
only this external escape/ownership/placement boundary: it creates no receiver
image or version, cross-actor schedule/state, per-delivery identity, or
physical-policy choice. Unknown aliases, dynamic keys/metatables, capabilities,
or cycles remain explicit blockers.

No analyzer Snapshot, published-object version, receiver image, or
shadow-ownership plane exists. Program order and ordinary Factors describe each
observable transfer; no abstract object is minted per iteration or delivery.
The sole cross-actor behavior is the sealed Send/Receive boundary over the
immutable/sealed transferred value; it creates no cross-actor schedule,
interference, or shared mutable-state relation. Transfer Rules model only the
Target-declared sender-side contents, aliases, isolation, failure, and
non-local outcomes.

Ownership abstracts semantic obligations. It may retain exact zero/one or an
interval with an unbounded upper endpoint under recurrent/multiple transfers;
its widening at `Mu` proves convergence without a cardinality cap. Footprint
facts include overflow, failed/yielded module initialization, nested ownership,
callback/provider re-entry, recurrence, and every outcome. Unknown trip count
or footprint remains unknown. Physical copy, allocation, refcount, GC, root,
and reclamation choices are outside this plan, and no engine component contains
a policy heuristic for them.

Evidence is a non-semantic sidecar keyed to the exact interned owning-Factor
conclusion, Guard, assumptions, and version. The sole non-Factor form is a
registered structural support-completion projection, keyed by its exact
occurrence/outcome, completed outer-support result, support/prune closure, and
projection version; it has structural predecessor/prune evidence rather than a
domain conclusion. Each Factor evidence node names its deriving Rule, Program
occurrence or boundary correspondence, and typed premise evidence edges for
every Factor conclusion used by that derivation. A refused
certificate/optimization conclusion retains the explicit blocking
conclusions, uncertainty, and precision-loss facts, so a Query can explain why
proof was unavailable rather than reconstructing the judgment. The owning
conclusion—not presentation policy—records whether it is a must theorem, a may
violation, or a result weakened by abstraction/widening; evidence preserves
that status through every premise edge. Evidence cannot
influence lattice equality or convergence. Join, Guard normalization,
widening, narrowing, revocation, and boundary transport keep/rebuild only
evidence valid for the resulting conclusion; exact proof publication is
refused otherwise.

Validity is constructive, not a version-only check. Typed evidence
constructors mirror Rule derivation, Guard branch injection, Guard-cover join,
Factor join/reduction, boundary substitution, widening/narrowing, the compiled
equation `Mu` knot, and exact Program/Link boundary backreferences. A must conclusion under Guard
`g` is publishable only when its premise evidence covers every feasible leaf
of the canonical cover of `g`; normalization carries a checked equivalence
between the old and new covers, the final `Y` satisfies
`Init# join Transfer#(Y) <= Y`, and the owning domain checker proves
`gamma(Y restricted to g)` is a subset of the claimed property. A post-fixpoint
alone is containment, not the property theorem. A may conclusion retains at least one feasible
abstract branch injection but is not thereby a concrete witness. Join,
reduction, and boundary constructors name all premises they semantically use.
Widening or incomplete coverage must propagate the owning conclusion's
uncertainty/precision-loss status and cannot construct an exact certificate.
Final post-fixpoint and version validation then checks that this complete
derivation still refers to current conclusions; it cannot repair missing Guard
coverage.

Schema sealing proves a Rule's authority and dependency shape; it does not by
itself prove the Rule's concrete transfer. A Rule evaluator is untrusted for
certificate publication. Every exact certificate path carries a local
derivation checked by the owning domain's versioned Rule checker against the
canonical Rule schema, exact Program/Link anchor, Guard/reindex, typed inputs,
resolved target, and claimed patch/prune/activation result. That checker
establishes the local concrete-simulation theorem; the generic evidence kernel
only composes checked derivations and independently validates the final
post-fixpoint. The checker receives only the supplied local derivation and
sealed operands, returns opaque evidence only, and cannot schedule, read ambient
State, emit a Patch, or publish a result. It is deterministic, total on its
admitted proof language, and fail-closed. Its identity and verifier version are
sealed into the Rule/Solver and every affected certificate; foreign, stale,
mismatched-anchor, or unchecked-premise derivations reject.

Every Rule evaluator has one sealed transfer-soundness admission before any of
its output may contribute ordinary State. That admission is either an
exhaustive proof, checked artifact, named versioned trusted theorem, or a local
derivation checked for every accepted evaluator result before Patch admission.
A certificate checker used only on demand is additional assurance for must
evidence; its mere presence never licenses unchecked ordinary publication. A
Rule whose accepted results are not all checked and which has no separately
admitted universal transfer theorem contributes no State. Replacing a trusted
checker with a machine-checked theorem artifact is dependency-minimal and does
not change Rule execution or create a second analyzer path.

Certificate eligibility is narrower than ordinary evidence. A published
certificate is only a must conclusion whose semantic premises have been
discharged by the completed analysis and whose remaining conditions are sealed
Program/Link/Target identities or Program decisions that dominate the certified
occurrence. It carries those structural conditions and their checked evidence;
it carries no opaque runtime Value, Heap, alias, numeric, ownership, or other
domain assumption, and no runtime validation/deoptimization relation. If a
conclusion needs any such undischargeable runtime condition, it remains an
ordinary analyzed fact or blocker and no future-consumer certificate publishes.
No certificate may assume a peer actor's mutable root or scheduler behavior:
the frozen Target admits cross-actor behavior only at a Send/Receive boundary
over a sealed immutable value. Nor may a certificate recover seed, history, or
result correlation from the state-insensitive `math.random` abstraction; that
explicit precision loss blocks publication of such a claim.

In particular, publication requires constructive evidence either that the
conclusion reads no mutable provider/resumer observation, or that the owning
domain proves it invariant under every admitted mutation of each such root for
the certificate's entire use lifetime. That invariant theorem, its complete
root/transition scope, and its semantic version are checked proof premises; the
current observation value or revision is not. Keying State-bound evidence or a
cached projection by an observation revision does not establish this theorem.
Without one of these two proofs, the conclusion remains only a State-bound fact
or blocker.

The sidecar is an interned immutable finite proof-term graph built with the
Factor conclusion. Ordinary justification nodes are acyclic. A conclusion that
depends on a cyclic equation component uses a typed backreference to that
component's compiled-equation `Mu`, validated against its final post-fixpoint,
conclusion, assumptions, and Factor versions. Each semantic boundary step also
names the exact Program/Link boundary it crossed. Evidence introduces no
proof-specific fixed-point binder, arbitrary cycle, or unrolling cutoff. A
proof through nested libraries shares the child's evidence and adds boundary
substitution edges; it does not rescan child points or rebuild the call chain.
Rendering traverses the finite graph iteratively and never uses Go recursion.
It can therefore present a source-anchored abstract derivation path, boundary
substitutions, recursive `Mu` step, and semantic blocker while leaving choice
of wording/severity to diagnostic policy. It may call that path a concrete
witness only when a concrete execution or a domain completeness theorem
establishes realizability; an overapproximating may-path is never reported as
an executable counterexample.

Certificate and diagnostic Queries only project already-converged
residence, lifetime, ownership, mutation/order, footprint, typestate, blocker, and
evidence conclusions; there is no separate placement-plan Query. Projection
performs no allocation-by-point scan, transitive closure, owner search,
suspension classification, or fallback inference. A future consumer owns any
physical decision.

## Future consumer contract

Program and converged typed certificates are sufficient inputs for a future
compiler/runtime integration, but this section authorizes no such work. A
consumer may use a certificate only under its exact Program/Link/Target
identities, checked dominating Program decisions, semantic versions, and proof
revision. By certificate eligibility, all semantic premises have already been
discharged by analysis; a certificate exposes neither opaque runtime
assumptions nor a runtime validation/deoptimization relation. Missing or
invalid proof selects the consumer's generic correct behavior. The consumer
does not pin, monitor, or compare mutable provider/resumer observation
revisions: eligibility has already proved independence or the owning-domain
lifetime invariant. This plan introduces no runtime revision guard,
invalidation protocol, or deoptimization machinery; any such future mechanism
requires separate authorization. The consumer
must not reconstruct semantic judgments from another IR, source patterns, path
strings, or physical layout, and disabling every certificate must change
performance only. Physical CFG/SSA, bytecode/native lowering, allocation, GC,
safepoints, deoptimization, and machine policy remain entirely outside the
analyzer.

## Complexity and performance laws

The system controls complexity by its mathematics, never by abandoning work at
a capacity:

Let `P` be Points, `G` Groups, `R` the total Group-member count, `I` ordered
Group-input occurrences, `C` explicit Group Carry occurrences, `L` live
reverse-read rows, `F` Factors, and `deg(p)` the producer count of Point `p`.
One Carry occurrence names one declared output Factor of one Group member; it
cannot imply a Factor scan or an undeclared family of carries. These quantities are reported
separately; no benchmark may hide quadratic producer discovery behind an
"action count."

- canonical topology sealing sorts once and then builds Point-producer,
  Point-consumer, Group-input, and Group-member CSR in
  `O((P+G+R+I+C) log(P+G+R+I+C))` time and
  `O(P+G+R+I+C)` storage; canonical construction orders all `I`
  consumer/input entries and all `C` Carry entries;
- point-only WTO construction is `O(P+I)` time and storage after canonical
  order, with no Rule/commit expansion;
- demanding a Point is proportional to reachable Points, producer Groups,
  their ordered inputs, members, support-prunes, and activation triggers;
- exact invalidation is proportional to changed support/unit/Guard rows plus
  compatible reverse readers and Point consumers; replacing a read log is
  `O(old+new)` and retains only `O(L)` live rows;
- replacing one producer contribution performs `Theta(deg(p))` contribution
  folds, or updates `O(log deg(p))` nodes in a canonical balanced reduction
  tree. Each fold separately reports its touched-plane/BDD carrier cost; neither
  bound hides an `F` scan. The
  tree uses `O(deg(p))` derived node handles plus separately measured
  structurally shared carrier nodes, has no semantic identity, and cannot
  become a publish action or persisted fact. A representation that materializes
  a flat `F`-root vector at every tree node has worst-case
  `O(deg(p)*F)` storage and is rejected unless a measured high-fan-in workload
  and an explicit memory gate justify it; the baseline exact refold remains the
  required simple implementation;
- Point state may use `O(P*F)` compact root handles. Group caches use
  `O(R+I+C)` sparse declared write/Carry incidence and may not add `O(G*F)`
  full carrier roots without a
  measured proof that structural sharing preserves the stated envelope;
- an unchanged warm solve executes zero transfers, performs no whole-cone
  scan, and allocates nothing.

- Program lowering is proportional to emitted typed rows; sealing is
  proportional to those rows/edges plus required decision-reachability and
  boundary-support incidence, apart from sorting and one-time interning;
- Guards: exact reduced symbolic functions plus termination-proven transport
  at each exact Program recurrence relation or Link boundary transport that
  references it; worst-case exponential size
  is measured and attacked by sharing and demand, never cardinality collapse;
- Program-handle reference, callee, allocation, and exact-key sets: exact
  finite powersets;
- numeric, symbolic-key, equality, and heap chains: domain widening with a
  termination proof, applied at each cyclic component's compiled `Mu`;
- invocation coordinates: the finite accepted Program/relation-tuple graph of
  one solve session;
  argument values never create coordinates;
- demanded Rule, Product, recurrence, and rank work is proportional to the
  derived slice rooted at the exact queried Factor/unit and structural root;
  adding Factors or Rules disconnected from that slice adds only cold schema
  declaration/validation work and cannot enlarge its reachable Point/Group set,
  Product work, active SCC membership, or rank obligations;
- specialization traces: evictable operational caches whose
  retention changes work only;
- evidence: hash-consed finite proof terms with typed backreferences to the
  compiled-equation `Mu` knots plus exact Program/Link boundary steps,
  rendered on demand;
- no depth bound represents a cycle as acyclic.

The analysis engine introduces no parallel-solver architecture. Its speed
comes from demand closure, exact invalidation, symbolic sharing, reusable body
equations, and zero-work cache hits.

No semantic algorithm has a cardinality cap, admission quota, context budget,
guard budget, iteration cutoff, or “first N then top” rule. A domain may
produce top/unknown only as its declared abstraction, join, or
termination-proven widening result.

Finite machine resources are an operational concern, never an abstract
operator. An explicit cancellation, host timeout, allocation failure, or
process/resource limit may end a solve only as `incomplete`, outside every
Factor lattice. The solve epoch is transactional: an incomplete run
publishes no State, Query result, diagnostic, certificate, oracle numerator,
evidence graph, or semantic-result cache entry. Previously committed immutable
artifacts remain valid, but no uncommitted conclusion from the failed epoch
becomes reusable truth. Retrying with more resources executes the
same equations and can change only incomplete to a completed semantic result;
there is no reduced-precision resource mode.

Cancellation is polled at deterministic safe boundaries: before Group
execution, between bounded chunks of Product/BDD traversal, before candidate
replacement, and before Point publication. Fault-injection laws exercise each
boundary after read-log installation and staged patch construction, during
atomic carrier publication after root reservation, and during Point
refold/reduction-tree replacement, and prove that no partial candidate, State,
evidence, or semantic cache entry survives. “Bounded chunks” controls polling
granularity only; it is not a tuple, BDD, or semantic work budget and cannot
change completion.
Any domain evaluator/checker traversal that can outlive one engine boundary
uses the same epoch-owned opaque poll between its implementation chunks and
returns only incomplete before producing a Patch or evidence result. The poll
cannot inspect or alter semantics.
The operational report records the incomplete cause, phase, and work counters;
none enters State, evidence identity, cache identity, Query output, a Factor
value, semantic result, or alternative solve mode.

A hard RSS/process kill or unrecoverable allocation failure is supervised out
of process: the worker produces no accepted output or persistence record and
the supervisor reports incomplete. It does not claim in-process cleanup after
process death. Cooperative cancellation instead drops every reserved but
unpublished carrier/evidence publisher exactly once and makes no root visible.

Each epoch has one terminal linearization transition,
`Running -> Completed | Incomplete`. Completion atomically revalidates and
claims its final demanded-equation/version snapshot before making State or any
Query result visible; cancellation atomically claims `Incomplete`. Exactly one
transition succeeds. When `Incomplete` wins, all epoch-local work is aborted
and no semantic output is published. When `Completed` wins, the sealed result
is published and returned; cancellation observed afterward is non-operative
for that epoch. Race and fault-injection laws surround final refold,
post-fixpoint validation, Point publication, Query materialization, and the
terminal claim. They may observe either lawful winner but never an incomplete
result with newly visible semantic output or a completed result from an
invalid snapshot.
Every fallible result materialization, validation, evidence/certificate or
diagnostic packaging, semantic-cache insertion preparation, and persistence
preflight completes while the epoch is still `Running`. Failure there claims
`Incomplete`. After `Completed` wins, only an infallible atomic publication of
already prepared immutable handles and their return may occur; it performs no
allocation or fallible callback. Fault injection covers every final
materialization and preflight allocation boundary as well as the terminal
claim.

The distinction between semantic work and structural cache is exact. In-flight
Rule evaluations, Point values/versions, Group candidates, guarded values,
dynamic read logs and reverse buckets, support observations, dirty bits/queue
generations, fold trees, SCC/WTO instances, diagnostics, evidence, and
semantic-result cache entries are epoch-local;
abort discards them and publishes no State. Only topology derived exclusively
from sealed Program/Link/Target rows and the sealed Rule schema is
input-independent Solver structure: its compiled body templates and interned
static coordinates may survive cancellation. A relation tuple admitted from a
domain trigger, its decision origin/evidence, and every tuple-derived Point,
Group, equation, SCC/WTO member, or topology edge are solve-session-local even
though admission is monotone within that session. They survive disposable
equation-epoch rebuilds but are discarded when publication, cancellation, or
input revision ends the session. Their presence asserts no reachability: the
epoch-local Guard/support and Rule read projection decide whether they
contribute.

One relation delta is installed atomically after its Program/Link endpoints,
Rule schema, and any claimed boundary transport validate. A valid ordinary
edge may create an equation SCC and therefore a compiled `Mu`; absence of a
source boundary does not reject a monotone Rule/reduction cycle. Static sealed
topology is never rolled back merely because a Solve is canceled.
Tuple-derived topology is not rolled back through a graph journal: the whole
session-owned arena is discarded, while reusable static templates remain
immutable. Retrying starts a new accepted set and must re-prove every dynamic
tuple; it reuses only the same constituent identities and static compiled
templates before executing the semantic equations again. A different Program, Link,
Rule/domain schema, provider contract, or target-contract digest constructs a
different Solver rather than trying to mutate or revalidate the old topology.
Diagnostic evidence may reference structural identities but never controls
activation or scheduling. Thus cancellation cannot publish a semantic
conclusion, while harmless cached structure cannot become a ghost fact.

Hot-path requirements:

- dense integer Program handles;
- packed canonical Guards;
- one flat sealed vector of small Factor-root handles as the initial outer
  carrier layout, copied at most once by a structural acceptance transaction
  over separately identified Factor-output Rule patches;
- typed arenas and monomorphized Factor operations;
- no string construction/parsing, reflection, `any`, interface decoding,
  parser, source-symbol interner lookup, or global relation scan; typed
  Factor-root hash-consing remains an intentional computed-table operation;
- no point-by-Factor State publication;
- no whole-State cache key;
- exact read logging and reverse invalidation;
- reusable scratch and zero-allocation cache hits;
- no Go recursion for program recursion.

The flat sealed vector is the required first implementation for 16--25
Factors. Persistent chunks, per-root change masks, generated heterogeneous
dispatch, or another physical vector representation are admitted only after a
profile on the required matrix below identifies outer-vector copying or erased
dispatch as a material share of solve time or allocation. Any replacement
preserves the same slot order, typed owner checks, one outer support, exact
deltas, and one State; it cannot add a second carrier or semantic path.

Required measurements run through one checked-in performance acceptance ledger
on a pinned corpus and machine. The ledger binds the corpus/source inventory,
Target-contract, resolved Link/deployment, and root-set digests; exact machine
and toolchain identities; benchmark-harness/version identity; and literal
per-metric limits for every listed latency, allocation, byte, and peak-memory
measurement. Each run must reproduce those identities and satisfy its own
named limit. These are reproducible release gates, never Factor, input,
context, Guard, or iteration capacity budgets:

- development checks use the repository's bounded runner and begin with the
  narrowest semantic law. Routine focused checks should remain within a few
  seconds where the toolchain permits it; shared lowering and other hot
  construction paths target sub-second execution on the pinned corpus. A run
  approaching twenty seconds, process-tree peak memory or cumulative
  allocation reaching multiple GiB, or safety exit 125 stops test broadening
  and repeated execution. It triggers one profile and an architectural
  highest-return review of duplicated traversal, representation, ownership,
  or proof work before further implementation. The review may justify a cold
  proof cost, but never hides it, raises a ceiling, weakens a law, adds a cap,
  or substitutes a cheaper semantic path. Ordinary semantic and ownership work
  continues without speculative micro-optimization while these tripwires are
  quiet;

- an independently regenerated workload manifest binds generator version,
  seed, complete scale vector, and required metrics. Every performance/scale
  workload must complete; incomplete is red. Dedicated cancellation fixtures
